# Uncertain rendering decisions: ko

Generated by `python3 scripts/review_checklist.py ko --write`; do not edit by hand. 1065 decisions in 60 books, every ko rendering decision the drafters marked uncertain. Tick an item once a reviewer has settled it, and regenerate the list after the content changes.

## 창세기 (51)

- [ ] [GEN 1:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/1/#v2) L/B/R · register · "하나님의 영께서 ... 계셨다", with the honorific — The Spirit of God is God's own presence (meaning decision m4), so Korean gives the Spirit the honorific that God has in the narrative. The reading "a wind from God", which the footnote of m4 gives, would take no honorific.
- [ ] [GEN 3:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/3/#v1) L/B/R · register · The serpent and the woman speak to each other in the plain style (해라체) — The Hebrew shows no rank between the serpent and the woman, and the terms note does not settle it; the plain style used between equals fits a conversation in which the serpent presumes to correct God. A reviewer may prefer the woman to speak more politely.
- [ ] [GEN 4:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/4/#v23) L/B/R · register · Lamech speaks to his wives in the plain style (해라체) at every level — The terms note gives husband and wife 해요체 at Balanced and Readable. Lamech's words, though, are a boasting song in which he commands his wives to listen (meaning decision m1); the plain style of a song or proclamation fits it better than everyday conversation. Husband and wife in conversation …
- [ ] [GEN 5:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/5/#v29) R · supplied_words · Readable says what the name sounds like: "히브리 말로 "쉼"이라는 말과 소리가 비슷하다" — The name Noah sounds like the Hebrew words for rest and comfort (meaning decision m1). As the terms note says for explained names, Readable tells the reader the wordplay, and Literal and Balanced keep the verb "위로하다", comfort, which the Hebrew name echoes.
- [ ] [GEN 9:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/9/#v5) L · setting_alternative · "그의 형제" at Literal, with no gender span — The English Literal gives "another" by default and "his brother" with the traditional setting. Korean Literal keeps the Hebrew word "형제", which here means any other person (meaning decision m2), and Balanced and Readable say "다른 사람"; Korean has no second form for the setting to switch to here.
- [ ] [GEN 9:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/9/#v11) R · word_choice · "언약" kept at Readable — "언약" is a key term in the terms note, and Korean readers meet it in the New Testament; Readable keeps it through this chapter and lets the context show that it is God's binding promise.
- [ ] [GEN 9:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/9/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "활" (L); "무지개" (B, R) — The Hebrew calls the rainbow by the word for a warrior's bow (meaning decision m1). Korean "무지개" has nothing of the bow in it, so Literal keeps "활" to show the picture, and Balanced and Readable say what is seen. The same holds in verses 14 and 16.
- [ ] [GEN 9:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/9/#v22) R · word_choice · "형제" (L, B); "두 형" (R) — Verse 24 calls Ham Noah's youngest son, so Readable, which uses the plainer word, can say "형", older brothers; Literal and Balanced keep the neutral "형제" of the Hebrew.
- [ ] [GEN 10:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/10/#v10) B/R · word_choice · "바벨" at every level — The terms note settles "바벨" for this city in Genesis, so that the reader hears the same name here and in 11:9. Later books and the New Testament, where the city is the empire's capital, call it "바빌론".
- [ ] [GEN 10:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/10/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "동생" for "his brother" — Korean must say whether a brother is older or younger. Peleg is named first and Joktan's line follows his, so Joktan is taken as the younger; the Hebrew does not say so outright.
- [ ] [GEN 18:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/18/#v5) L/B/R · register · the visitors speak to Abraham in the plain style (해라체) from their first words — The narrator has said that it is the LORD who appeared (verse 1), and from verse 10 the one who speaks is the LORD (verse 13). Keeping the visitors in the plain style from the start lets the scene keep one voice for them, as God speaks everywhere in Genesis; a reviewer may prefer polite guests …
- [ ] [GEN 19:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/19/#v31) L/B/R · register · the older sister speaks to the younger in the plain style: 해라체 at Literal and Balanced, the spoken 해체 at Readable — An older sister speaks down to a younger one, as the terms of this translation set for brothers. Readable uses the everyday spoken form between sisters, which is plain speech too.
- [ ] [GEN 20:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/20/#v16) B/R · setting_alternative · the silver in kilograms by default, "천 세겔" (1,000 shekels) as the alternative; Literal keeps the Hebrew's "천 [세겔]" — Korean uses metric weights. At 11.5 grams to the shekel, 1,000 shekels is about 11.5 kilograms. Abimelech speaks to Sarah in the plain style, as a king to a woman of another man's household; to Abraham he keeps the formal polite style.
- [ ] [GEN 23:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/23/#v4) L/B/R · register · Abraham and the Hittites speak to each other in the formal polite style (합쇼체); Abraham calls himself "저", and they call him "내 주" (L) or "나리" (B, R) — This is a public negotiation in the town gate, full of the courtesies the Hebrew records (bowing, "my lord", "hear me"). Abraham speaks humbly as a foreigner asking for land; the Hittites answer him with the honour due to a great man. Readable says "파십시오" (sell) where the English says "let me …
- [ ] [GEN 25:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/25/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "맞서서" (L), "맞서며 살았다" (B), "사이좋게 지내지 못했다" (R) — The words echo 16:12, where Ishmael was to live against all his brothers (meaning decision); Korean "맞서다" keeps the sense of opposition, and Readable says it plainly, as the English "at odds with" does.
- [ ] [GEN 25:34](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/25/#v34) L/B/R · word_choice · "팥죽" for the stew of 'adashim (lentils) — Lentils are a small red legume (meaning decision). "팥죽" is the familiar Korean red bean stew and keeps the red colour that gives Esau his name; "렌즈콩" is the exact plant but strange to most readers. A reviewer may prefer the exact word.
- [ ] [GEN 26:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/26/#v8) L · word_choice · "즐기고 있었다" (L), "껴안고 있었다" (B) — The verb metsaheq is a pun on Isaac's name and here means caressing as husband and wife (meaning decision). The pun cannot be kept in Korean; Literal uses a word that leaves the play open, Balanced and Readable say what Abimelech saw.
- [ ] [GEN 26:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/26/#v9) L/B/R · register · Abimelech speaks to Isaac in 합쇼체 with "당신" ("말했습니까?"), though he is the king — The terms note asks 합쇼체 between strangers and hosts. Abimelech speaks to a powerful guest whom he fears to wrong, and later (26:28-29) asks him for a covenant, so formal speech fits the scene throughout.
- [ ] [GEN 26:33](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/26/#v33) L/B/R · word_choice · "세바" for Shibah — The name links with the oath of 26:31 (meaning decision); "세바" keeps the sound of the second part of "브엘세바", so the explanation at Balanced and Readable follows from the names themselves.
- [ ] [GEN 27:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/27/#v6) L/B/R · register · Rebekah speaks to Jacob in 해라체; Jacob answers his mother in 해요체 with "어머니" and "저" ("어떡해요") — The terms note allows 합쇼체 or 해요체 from child to parent. Jacob speaks to Isaac formally in the blessing scene, but to his mother, in their private plan, in the warmer 해요체.
- [ ] [GEN 27:36](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/27/#v36) L/B/R · word_choice · "밀어냈습니다" (L), "속이고 발꿈치를 잡았습니다" (B), "속였습니다" (R) for ya'qeveni — Esau plays on the name Jacob with the verb 'aqav, to take by the heel, to supplant (meaning decision); Korean cannot echo the name. Balanced keeps the heel of 25:26 so the reader hears the link; Readable says what he did.
- [ ] [GEN 29:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/29/#v14) L/B/R · register · Laban speaks to Jacob in 해라체 with "너"; Jacob answers his uncle in 합쇼체 with "외삼촌" and "저" — The terms note gives 합쇼체 for strangers and hosts, but Laban greets Jacob as his own flesh and blood, and Korean speaks to a nephew as an older relative does, in the plain style. The levels stay the same when the bargaining turns into a master and his worker (29:15-31:55).
- [ ] [GEN 29:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/29/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "눈이 약했지만" (L); "눈매가 여렸지만" (B); "눈이 순했지만" (R) — Rakkot means soft or tender, and may mean dull or lovely (meaning decision). Literal keeps the plain word; Balanced and Readable choose gentle words, as the English "delicate" and "gentle" do, without deciding the sense.
- [ ] [GEN 30:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/30/#v8) L/B · word_choice · "하나님의 씨름" (L); "크게 겨루어" (B); "힘겹게 겨루었는데" (R) — "Wrestlings of God" is a way of saying mighty wrestlings (meaning decision). Literal keeps the Hebrew words; Balanced and Readable say how great the struggle was, as the English does. The verb "씨름하다" returns at Peniel (32:24-25), where Literal keeps it too.
- [ ] [GEN 30:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/30/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "합환채" for duda'im (mandrakes) — The mandrake was thought to help a woman conceive (meaning decision). "합환채" is the established Korean Bible word for the plant, and its meaning (a herb for the joy of union) fits the scene; Readable adds that it is a plant. A reviewer may prefer the botanical loanword.
- [ ] [GEN 30:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/30/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "점을 쳐서" (L), "징조를 보고" (B, R) for nichashti — The verb most likely means learning by divination, though it may mean "I have grown rich" (meaning decision). Korean follows the English reading at each level.
- [ ] [GEN 30:37](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/30/#v37) L/B/R · word_choice · "백양나무" (white poplar) for livneh; "아몬드나무"; "플라타너스" — Livneh and "white" (lavan) play on Laban's name (meaning decision); "백양나무", the white poplar, keeps the colour in the tree's name, which Korean can hear. The almond and plane tree are given by their usual modern Korean names.
- [ ] [GEN 31:35](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/31/#v35) L/B/R · register · Rachel to her father in 합쇼체; "내 주께서는" (L), "아버지" (B, R) for "my lord" — "My lord" is a respectful address (meaning decision). Literal keeps it in the form the terms note gives for adoni; Balanced and Readable use the address a daughter uses to her father, with the formal style carrying the respect, as the English Readable turns it into "Sir".
- [ ] [GEN 31:42](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/31/#v42) L/B/R · word_choice · "이삭의 두려움이신 분" (L); "이삭이 경외하는 분" (B); "이삭이 두려운 마음으로 섬기는 하나님" (R) — "The Fear of Isaac" is a name for God (meaning decision). Literal keeps the noun as a title; Balanced and Readable say whom it means, as the English does, using the Psalms note's "두려운 마음으로 섬기다" at Readable. The same forms are kept in 31:53.
- [ ] [GEN 32:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/32/#v5) L/B/R · register · Jacob's message to Esau in 합쇼체, with "내 주" (L, B) and "나리" (R) for adoni, and "당신의 종" (L), "이 종" (B), "나리의 종" (R) for "your servant" — The terms note's forms for "my lord" and "your servant". Jacob takes the place of a servant before Esau, reversing the blessing of 27:29 (meaning decision), so his message uses the formal style the note gives for a servant to his master; he gives orders to his own men in 해라체.
- [ ] [GEN 32:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/32/#v10) L/B/R · register · Jacob's prayer (32:10-13) in 합쇼체 with "저" and "주님"; the divine name as a vocative with "여" outside the span — The terms note's prayer forms. The vocative ending "여" fits both "주" and "야훼", as the style guide asks of particles. "My father Abraham" is his grandfather, so Korean says "할아버지" at every level; Literal keeps "내", Balanced and Readable the humble "제".
- [ ] [GEN 32:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/32/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "어떤 사람" with no honorific in the narrative; "씨름하다" for ye'aveq — Who the man was is the question the scene leaves open until Jacob names the place (32:31; meaning decision), so the narrative does not give him God's honorific, as the English says only "a man". He speaks in 해라체 and Jacob answers in 합쇼체, as to a superior. "씨름" is the Korean word for wrestling and …
- [ ] [GEN 32:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/32/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "축복하다" for Jacob's request "bless me", and "복을 빌어 주었다" for 32:30 — Korean must choose between "복을 주다" (God's own blessing) and the words for a person's blessing. Since the narrative leaves the man unnamed (32:25), the neutral "축복하다" keeps the question open; the reader learns in 32:31 that Jacob saw God.
- [ ] [GEN 36:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/36/#v2) L · word_choice · "아나의 딸이요 히위 사람 시브온의 딸인 오홀리바마" (L); "손녀" (B) — Read with verse 24, Zibeon is Anah's father, so Oholibamah is his granddaughter (meaning decision m2). Literal keeps the Hebrew's second "daughter", in the old sense of a female descendant, and attaches it to Oholibamah; Balanced says "손녀" and Readable makes Anah Zibeon's son, as the English does …
- [ ] [GEN 38:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/38/#v16) L/B/R · register · Judah and the veiled woman speak to each other as strangers, in polite styles: she in 합쇼체, he in 합쇼체 (L, B) and the plain-polite "-시오" (R) — The Genesis terms note sets the formal style between strangers, and Judah's request carries the Hebrew particle of courtesy. Readable lets him speak a little more bluntly with "-시오", still polite; a reviewer may prefer one style at every level.
- [ ] [GEN 38:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/38/#v22) L/B/R · register · Judah and his friend Hirah speak to each other in the familiar style between grown men (하게체: "-네", "-게") — They are friends and equals of mature age. Korean between such friends often uses 하게체, which is warmer than the plain 해라체 and less formal than 합쇼체; a reviewer may prefer 해라체.
- [ ] [GEN 41:43](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/41/#v43) B/R · word_choice · "아브렉" kept at Literal; "엎드려라!" (B, R) — The cry is an unclear word, perhaps "Bow down!" (meaning decision m2); Literal keeps the sound and Balanced and Readable give the likely sense, as the English does.
- [ ] [GEN 43:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/43/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "유향", "향품", "몰약" (L, B); one word "향료" for the resins (R) — The exact plants behind these resins are uncertain (meaning decision). Korean uses the customary names for the caravan goods of 37:25 at Literal and Balanced, and Readable gathers the resins under one plain word, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [GEN 44:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/44/#v4) L · register · the words Joseph gives the steward are in the plain style at Literal, but in the steward's own polite style at Balanced and Readable — At Literal the quoted message keeps Joseph's own voice as he dictates it. At the other levels it is what the steward will actually say, so it takes the formal polite style he uses with the brothers (43:23).
- [ ] [GEN 45:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/45/#v3) L/B/R · register · once he makes himself known, Joseph speaks to his older brothers in the informal polite style (해요체) with "나", and calls Jacob "내 아버지" with the honorific — The terms note sets this change at 45:1: Joseph is now a younger brother speaking to older ones, and the change of speech level itself tells the brothers who he is. He keeps "나" rather than the humble "저", as the man who still holds their lives in his hand. The honorific for his father ("살아 계세요") …
- [ ] [GEN 45:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/45/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "다투다" (quarrel) — Ragaz can mean to be stirred by anger or by fear (meaning decision). Korean follows the reading the English takes, a warning not to blame one another; a footnote gives the other.
- [ ] [GEN 47:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/47/#v8) L/B/R · register · Pharaoh speaks to the aged Jacob in the formal polite style with "당신" — The terms note sets the formal polite style between strangers and hosts. Pharaoh receives Jacob as an honoured old guest who has just blessed him, and Korean shows that respect to age even from a king. Reviewers may prefer a plainer royal style.
- [ ] [GEN 47:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/47/#v31) L/B/R · word_choice · "침상 머리" (head of the bed); "절하다" (bow down) at every level — The Hebrew text reads "bed"; the Greek read "staff", which Hebrews 11:21 quotes (meaning decision). Korean "절하다" can be a bow of worship or of respect, so it keeps both readings open; "경배하다" would choose worship.
- [ ] [GEN 48:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/48/#v14) B · word_choice · "알면서 일부러" (knowingly) added at Balanced — Sikkel can mean "cross" or "act wisely" (meaning decision); Balanced carries the second sense with "알면서 일부러", as the English Balanced adds "knowingly".
- [ ] [GEN 48:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/48/#v22) L · word_choice · "한 어깨" (one shoulder) at Literal — Shekhem means "shoulder" and is also the name of the town (meaning decision). Literal keeps the Hebrew word so the play can be seen; Balanced and Readable name Shechem, as the English does.
- [ ] [GEN 49:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/49/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "그것이 [속한] 분이 오실 때까지" (L), "그것의 참 주인이 오실 때까지" (B, R), with the honorific for the coming one — The reading of shiloh as "the one to whom it belongs" is the one chosen (meaning decision); the other readings are in that decision's footnote. Korean gives the coming ruler the honorific, as the peoples will obey him; the older Korean reading takes shiloh as a name.
- [ ] [GEN 49:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/49/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "열매 맺는 [나무의] 아들" (L); "열매 맺는 가지" (B, R) — "Son of" can mean a branch or shoot of a plant (meaning decision); Literal keeps "son" and brackets the plant, as the English Literal does. "열매 맺는" keeps the echo of Ephraim, "God has made me fruitful" (41:52).
- [ ] [GEN 50:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/50/#v4) L/B/R · register · Joseph speaks to Pharaoh's court in the formal polite style with "여러분" and "나" — Joseph is the highest official, but he is asking a favour of the king's household, so he uses the polite style of a request among equals at court; his message to Pharaoh himself (verse 5) uses "저".
- [ ] [GEN 50:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/50/#v16) L/B/R · register · the brothers, as older brothers, speak to Joseph in the plain style with "너" and "우리" — The terms note sets the plain style for older brothers to a younger one after 45:1. Their fear shows in their words, "please forgive" and "we are your slaves", not in a change of speech level; reviewers may prefer a humbler style here.
- [ ] [GEN 50:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/50/#v23) R · word_choice · "에브라임의 손자의 자녀들" at Readable — The third generation may be counted from Ephraim or from Joseph (meaning decision). Readable must pick a plain phrase and counts from Ephraim, as the English "down to the third generation" leans; Literal and Balanced keep "삼 대", which bears both.
- [ ] [GEN 50:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gen/50/#v25) L/B/R · register · the plain style with "너희" for "the sons of Israel" — "The sons of Israel" here are Jacob's whole family, the future Israelites (meaning decision), not only Joseph's older brothers, so Joseph speaks to them as the head of the family, in the plain style.

## 출애굽기 (38)

- [ ] [EXO 1:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/1/#v16) L/B · word_choice · "해산 돌" (birth stones) — Korean keeps the Hebrew picture of stones, which the meaning decision explains; a stool is one guess at what they were.
- [ ] [EXO 3:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/3/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "나는 있는 나다" for "I AM WHO I AM", and "'나는 있다' 하시는 분" for "I AM" as a name — Built from "있다" (to be), the verb Korean John 8:58 uses for "I am", so the name and its explanation stay linked as the English and its meaning decision link them; the familiar Korean wordings belong to copyrighted or archaic versions and were not followed.
- [ ] [EXO 4:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/4/#v18) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks to his father-in-law in 합쇼체; Jethro answers in 하게체 ("가게") — The traditional way an older man speaks to his son-in-law in Korean: familiar but respectful of a grown man.
- [ ] [EXO 7:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/7/#v3) L · word_choice · Literal keeps apart the three Hebrew verbs for Pharaoh's heart: "강하게 하다/강하다" (be strong), "굳게 하다" (make hard), "무겁다" (be heavy) — The meaning decision notes that three verbs are used; Balanced says "완고하다" or "굳어지다" and Readable "고집스럽다", as the English Balanced and Readable say "stubborn" and "hard".
- [ ] [EXO 7:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/7/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "큰 뱀" (serpent) here, "뱀" (snake) in 4:3 and 7:15 — The meaning decision notes that the Hebrew word differs from the snake of 4:3; "큰 뱀" keeps them apart without the mythical "용".
- [ ] [EXO 8:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/8/#v4) L/B/R · register · Pharaoh now speaks of {{divine_name:주|야훼}} with the honorific particle "께" — He is asking for God's help; Korean marks the change from 5:2, where he would not acknowledge the name, as the meaning decision notes that he now names Yahweh.
- [ ] [EXO 8:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/8/#v5) L · word_choice · "제 위에서 영광을 누리십시오" (glorify yourself over me) at Literal — Literal keeps the courtly Hebrew idiom; Balanced and Readable say what the meaning decision says it means, that Pharaoh may set the time.
- [ ] [EXO 8:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/8/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "각다귀" (gnats) — The Hebrew word names small biting insects whose kind is uncertain, as the meaning decision's footnote says; "각다귀" is a gnat-like insect, and the older Korean "이" (lice) is one guess among several.
- [ ] [EXO 8:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/8/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "벌레 떼" (swarm) at Literal; "파리 떼" (swarms of flies) at Balanced and Readable — The Hebrew word names a swarm without saying what insect it was; Literal keeps it open, and Balanced and Readable follow the English with flies, as the meaning decision's footnote allows.
- [ ] [EXO 9:32](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/9/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "스펠트밀" for spelt — Spelt is a kind of wheat (meaning decision); "쌀보리", the older Korean rendering, names a barley, and "스펠트밀" is the current name of the grain.
- [ ] [EXO 10:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/10/#v10) B/R · word_order · The mocking wish is built as "if I let you go ..., then that much will Yahweh be with you" — A plain wish would sound sincere in Korean; the conditional shows the sarcasm the meaning decision describes, that Pharaoh will never let them go.
- [ ] [EXO 11:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/11/#v8) L/B · register · Pharaoh's officials speaking to Moses use 하오체 ("나가시오") — Court officials addressing a foreign leader they now fear: neither the plain style they would use to an inferior nor the 합쇼체 they use to their king.
- [ ] [EXO 12:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/12/#v27) R · register · At Readable the parents answer their children in 해체 ("제사란다", "넘어가셨어") — Readable lets parents speak to children as Korean parents do at home; the other levels keep the plain style of the law.
- [ ] [EXO 12:45](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/12/#v45) L/B · word_choice · "거류민" for toshav, kept apart from "나그네" for ger — The two words name different people here (meaning decision): the toshav lives among Israel without belonging to it, while a circumcised ger may keep the Passover (12:48).
- [ ] [EXO 13:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/13/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}의 율법" (L) for torah; "가르침" at Balanced and Readable — The term sheet sets "율법" for the Law; here the meaning decision reads torah as Yahweh's instruction, his teaching, as the English Balanced does, so Balanced and Readable say "가르침".
- [ ] [EXO 13:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/13/#v19) B · word_choice · "이스라엘 자손" kept at Balanced here — Joseph made his brothers' families swear (Genesis 50:25); the sons of Israel are here his own kin, so Balanced keeps "자손" rather than the usual "백성".
- [ ] [EXO 14:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/14/#v20) R · supplied_words · "한쪽에는 어둠을 주고, 다른 쪽에는 ... 빛을" at Readable — One reading of the meaning decision (dark to Egypt, light to Israel); the English Readable leaves the two sides unnamed, so this is marked uncertain and the footnoted meaning decision gives the question.
- [ ] [EXO 14:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/14/#v24) L · word_choice · "새벽 경점" at Literal for the morning watch — "경점" is the old Korean name for a watch of the night; Balanced and Readable give the time plainly, as the English does (meaning decision).
- [ ] [EXO 16:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/16/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "신 광야" for the wilderness of Sin, "시내" for Sinai — The usual Protestant Korean forms. Korean writes this Sin and the Zin of Numbers alike ("신"), as the term sheet does for Zin; the context keeps them apart.
- [ ] [EXO 16:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/16/#v9) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks to Aaron, his older brother, in 해라체 at Literal, and at Balanced and Readable calls him "형님" and speaks in 해요체; Aaron speaks to Moses in 해라체, … — Aaron is older (7:7) but Moses leads. Literal keeps the plain style of speech used throughout that level; Balanced and Readable give the address a Korean younger brother uses to his older brother. Recorded once for Exodus; it holds wherever the two speak to each other.
- [ ] [EXO 18:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/18/#v6) L/B/R · register · Jethro speaks to Moses in 해라체 with "너"; Moses answers him in 합쇼체 — The Genesis sheet has a parent speak to a child in 해라체 and the child answer in 합쇼체; a father-in-law is treated the same way. 하게체 with "자네" would also fit the relationship and is a reviewer's choice.
- [ ] [EXO 20:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/20/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "천 대까지" (L); "수천 대에 이르기까지" (B, R) — The meaning decision leaves open whether "thousands" means thousands of people or the thousandth generation; set against "the third and fourth generation", Korean reads it as generations, and the footnote keeps the other reading.
- [ ] [EXO 23:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/23/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "여종의 아들", with no gender span — The English writes a span for "son"; Korean keeps "아들" as the Hebrew has it, since a household-born slave is meant and a span here would only switch "자녀" and "아들".
- [ ] [EXO 26:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/26/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "널판" for the upright frames — The meaning decision leaves open whether these were open frames or solid boards; "널판" fits the second reading, "틀" the first, and the meaning footnote gives the other. Shared craft vocabulary for Exodus 25-40, so that the instructions and the building account read as one text.
- [ ] [EXO 27:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/27/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "가름대" (Literal), "띠" (Balanced, Readable) for the bands — The meaning decision leaves open whether these were bands around each pillar or rods between them; "띠" gives the first, as the English "bands", and Literal keeps the older word that allows the second.
- [ ] [EXO 28:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/28/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "줄마노" for the onyx stones — "마노" (agate) is needed for another stone in verse 19; "줄마노" is the banded stone called onyx. The meaning decision notes the identification is not certain.
- [ ] [EXO 28:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/28/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · Stone names as in the Korean Revelation 21:19-20 where the stones are the same (홍옥수, 황옥, 녹주석, 사파이어, 에메랄드, 풍신자석, 자수정, 귀감람석, 벽옥); "터키석", "마노", "줄마노" for the … — Modern names a reader can look up; the meaning decision and its footnote say that several identifications are uncertain.
- [ ] [EXO 30:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/30/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "몰약", "향기로운 육계", "향기로운 창포"; "계피" for cassia (verse 24) — The meaning decisions say these spices are not certainly identified; Korean uses the names familiar from earlier Korean Bibles, keeping cinnamon and cassia apart.
- [ ] [EXO 30:34](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/30/#v34) L/B/R · word_choice · "소합향", "나감향", "풍자향", "유향" for stacte, onycha, galbanum, frankincense — The names familiar from earlier Korean Bibles; the meaning decisions and their footnotes say the first three are not certainly identified.
- [ ] [EXO 31:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/31/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "물두멍" for the basin — "물두멍" is the established name of the priests' washbasin and is used through Exodus 25-40 in Korean; Readable adds what it was for, as the English Readable does. A reviewer may prefer the everyday "대야".
- [ ] [EXO 32:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/32/#v21) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks to Aaron in 해라체 at Literal and calls him "형님" in 해요체 at Balanced and Readable (as at 16:9); Aaron answers in 합쇼체 with "내 주" — Moses calls Aaron to account, but Aaron is still his older brother, so the address recorded at 16:9 holds. Aaron answers him as "my lord" (32:22) and pleads, so his reply is in 합쇼체.
- [ ] [EXO 34:28](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/34/#v28) L/B/R · word_order · Who wrote: "그분께서 ... 쓰셨다" (L); "기록하였다" (B); "기록되었다" (R) — The Hebrew "he wrote" may be God (34:1) or Moses (34:27) (meaning decision m1). Korean must choose whether to add the honorific; Literal follows 34:1, where God says he will write, Balanced keeps the verb without the honorific and without a subject, and Readable uses the passive so as not to decide.
- [ ] [EXO 38:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/38/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "띠" for the bands — The meaning decision leaves open whether these were rings around the pillars or rods between them. "띠" fits rings; "가름대" would decide for rods.
- [ ] [EXO 38:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/38/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "우리" for the name Uri — "우리" is the established Protestant spelling of the name, and it is also the Korean word for "we". The phrase is built as "우리의 아들(인) 브살렐" so it reads as a name; a native reviewer may prefer a note.
- [ ] [EXO 39:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/39/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "짜서 만든 옷" (Literal), "정교하게 짠 옷" (Balanced), "특별한 옷" (Readable) for the woven garments — The garments are named by their weaving; "예복" would add the idea of ceremony that the word does not carry.
- [ ] [EXO 39:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/39/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "줄마노" for onyx — "줄마노" is the current Korean name of the banded stone, in the same family as "홍마노" (sardonyx) in Korean Revelation 21:20; "호마노" is the older Bible word.
- [ ] [EXO 39:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/39/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · the twelve stones named as in Korean Revelation 21:19-20 where the same English name is used: "홍옥수, 황옥, 녹주석, 사파이어, 에메랄드, 풍신자석, 자수정, 귀감람석, 벽옥"; "터키석", "마노" and … — The identity of several Hebrew stone names is uncertain (meaning decision); Korean follows the English identifications with the current Korean mineral names, and matches the names Korean Revelation already uses.
- [ ] [EXO 39:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/exo/39/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "긴 웃옷" for the tunics — Older Korean Bibles call the tunic "속옷", which today means underwear and would clash with the linen undergarments of verse 28; "긴 웃옷" describes the long shirt-like garment.

## 레위기 (36)

- [ ] [LEV 2:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/2/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "기념 몫" for the memorial portion; Readable "하나님께서 기억하시도록 바치는 부분" — The portion burned stands for the whole offering and brings the worshiper to God's remembrance; "몫" marks it as a part of the whole. Readable spells out the idea in a clause, as the English Readable does with "a reminder".
- [ ] [LEV 3:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/3/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "간에 붙은 돌출부" (Literal), "간에서 길게 튀어나온 부분" (Balanced), "간의 가장 좋은 부분" (Readable); "허리" for the loins — The older "간 꺼풀" (a membrane) does not match the likely sense, a lobe projecting from the liver; each level follows the English level's reading, and the meaning decision's footnote gives the uncertainty.
- [ ] [LEV 5:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/5/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "죄책[의 값]" (Literal), "죄에 대한 값" (Balanced), "죄를 갚기 위하여" (Readable) for "his guilt" (*ʾasham*) here — In verses 6-7 the word means what is owed for the wrong rather than the guilt offering itself; Literal marks the added word, as the English Literal does with "[payment]".
- [ ] [LEV 5:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/5/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "맡긴 물건" for the deposit and "담보물" (Readable "빚을 갚겠다는 약속으로 준 물건") for the pledge — The meaning decision reads the second term as goods given in pledge; the meaning decision's footnote gives the uncertainty, and Readable describes the thing instead of naming it.
- [ ] [LEV 5:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/5/#v24) L · supplied_words · "자기의 죄책[을 씻는] 날에" — Korean cannot leave "the day of his guilt" bare; Literal brackets the verb it supplies, while Balanced and Readable name the guilt offering as the English Balanced and Readable do; the meaning decision's footnote gives both readings.
- [ ] [LEV 6:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/6/#v14) L · word_choice · "구운 조각들" for a word of unknown exact sense — The meaning decision's footnote says the Hebrew word is uncertain; Literal gives the likely sense plainly, and Balanced and Readable fold it into "부순 곡식 제물".
- [ ] [LEV 7:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/7/#v18) L/B · word_choice · "혐오스러운 [고기]" for "foul meat"; Readable "상한 것" — The word names sacrificial meat that has become unfit, spoiled and offensive; "가증하다" is the older church word and suggests moral abomination.
- [ ] [LEV 7:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/7/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "백성 가운데서 끊어지다" (Literal, Balanced); "백성 가운데서 쫓겨나다" (Readable) — Literal and Balanced keep the Hebrew picture, whose exact force the meaning decision's footnote discusses; Readable follows the English Readable's "removed".
- [ ] [LEV 7:35](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/7/#v35) L/B/R · word_choice · "몫" for the priests' portion — The meaning decision's footnote gives the alternative reading "the anointed portion"; Korean keeps the plain "몫" in the text.
- [ ] [LEV 8:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/8/#v31) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks to Aaron, alone or with his sons, in 해라체 at Literal; at Balanced and Readable he calls his older brother "형님" (his sons "조카들") and speaks in 해요체. … — Aaron is Moses' older brother. At Literal Moses passes on God's commands in the plain style of the laws; at Balanced and Readable a Korean reader hears a younger brother speaking to an older one, as Joseph speaks to his brothers in Genesis, while Moses still calls himself "나" as the one who gives …
- [ ] [LEV 10:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/10/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "긴 웃옷" for the priests' tunic (kuttonet) — "속옷", the older church word, now means underwear in everyday Korean; "긴 웃옷" describes the long priestly tunic without that misreading.
- [ ] [LEV 10:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/10/#v19) L/B/R · register · Aaron answers Moses in the plain style (해라체) — Aaron is Moses' older brother and speaks to him as such, as the Genesis term sheet sets for brothers; the term sheet's 합쇼체 for the people speaking to Moses does not cover his own brother. A reviewer may prefer a more formal style between the two leaders.
- [ ] [LEV 11:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/11/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "혐오스러운 것" (L, B), "역겨운 것" (R) for detestable (sheqets) — "가증하다" now sounds archaic and is used of moral hatefulness; "혐오스럽다" says that the creatures are to be regarded with loathing, as the meaning decision says. Readable uses the everyday "역겹다". Recorded once for Leviticus 10-15.
- [ ] [LEV 11:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/11/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · The birds of verses 13-19 named with current Korean bird names following the English identifications: "독수리", "수염수리", "검은독수리", "붉은솔개", "솔개", "까마귀", "타조", "올빼미", … — Many of these identifications are uncertain, as the meaning decisions say; Korean follows the probable identifications the English uses. A reviewer with knowledge of Korean bird names should check the list.
- [ ] [LEV 11:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/11/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "떼메뚜기", "대머리메뚜기", "귀뚜라미", "여치" for the four leaping insects; Readable "모든 종류의 메뚜기와 귀뚜라미와 여치" — The exact insects are uncertain, as the meaning decision says; the Korean names follow the English identifications.
- [ ] [LEV 11:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/11/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "족제비", "쥐", "큰도마뱀" and, in verse 30, "도마뱀붙이", "왕도마뱀", "벽도마뱀", "모래도마뱀", "카멜레온" — The identifications are uncertain, as the meaning decisions say; Korean follows the English identifications with current Korean animal names.
- [ ] [LEV 13:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/13/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "버짐" for the scall (neteq); Readable "가렵고 비늘처럼 일어나는 버짐" — The meaning decision describes a scaly, itching patch on the scalp or chin; "버짐" is the Korean word for such a scaly patch, while "옴" names scabies. The exact condition is uncertain.
- [ ] [LEV 13:39](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/13/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "어루러기" for the harmless rash (bohaq) — The meaning decision describes a pale, harmless spotting of the skin; "어루러기" is the Korean name of such pale patches. The identification is uncertain.
- [ ] [LEV 13:45](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/13/#v45) L/B/R · word_choice · The cry "부정하다! 부정하다!" (L, B), "부정합니다, 부정합니다!" (R) — The cry warns others, as the meaning decision says. Literal and Balanced keep the plain declaration; Readable puts it in the polite style a person would use calling to strangers.
- [ ] [LEV 15:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/15/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "유출" (L, B) for the discharge (zov); Readable "고름 같은 것이 흘러나오다" and then "흘러나오는 것" — The meaning decision describes an abnormal flow that is a sign of illness. "유출" names the flow without saying what it is, as the Hebrew does; Readable describes it once in plain words.
- [ ] [LEV 19:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/19/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "배상" (compensation) for the rare word the meaning decision discusses — Follows the reading the English takes; the meaning decision notes that some take the word as "an inquiry" or "punishment".
- [ ] [LEV 23:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/23/#v24) B/R · supplied_words · "나팔을 크게 불어" at Balanced and Readable for the loud blast; Literal "크게 울리는 소리" — Korean needs to name what sounds; the meaning decision takes the blast most likely as horns or trumpets, though it may be a great shout.
- [ ] [LEV 24:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/24/#v19) L · word_choice · "상처" for the Hebrew "blemish" — The same Hebrew word is the "defect" (흠) of an animal; here it is an injury to a person, and "흠을 내다" would sound like damage to an object in Korean.
- [ ] [LEV 25:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/25/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "거류민" for the settler (*toshav*), "나그네" for the sojourner (*ger*); "품꾼" for the hired worker — Two kinds of outsiders stand side by side in this chapter; Korean keeps "나그네" for *ger* as the term sheet sets and "거류민" for the one who lives on another's land.
- [ ] [LEV 25:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/25/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "무르다" (Literal) and "다시 사들이다" (Balanced, Readable) for redeeming land — "무르다" is the Korean verb for taking back what was sold by paying its price; Balanced and Readable use the plainer "다시 사들이다". "속량하다" is kept for God's redeeming in the Psalms.
- [ ] [LEV 25:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/25/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "무를 자" (Literal), "무를 사람" (Balanced, Readable) for the redeemer (*goel*) — The nearest relative who buys back what the family lost; the noun follows the verb "무르다" chosen in verse 24, so the reader sees that they belong together.
- [ ] [LEV 25:33](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/25/#v33) L · word_choice · "무른 것" keeps the Hebrew verb "redeem" at Literal — The meaning decision's footnote explains that here the verb means acquiring from a Levite; Balanced and Readable say "사들이다".
- [ ] [LEV 25:39](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/25/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "종의 일" / "종처럼 부리다" for the work of a slave — The term sheet keeps "종" for *ʿeved*; the point here is that an Israelite may not be treated as one owned, which the meaning decision's footnote explains. "노예" is kept for those bought as property in verses 44-46.
- [ ] [LEV 25:42](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/25/#v42) L/B/R · word_choice · "내 종들" for Israel as God's servants, "노예" for the sale of a slave — The Hebrew uses one word for both; Korean keeps "종" where Israel belongs to God and "노예" where a person is sold as property, as the term sheet sets. The meaning decision's footnote explains the link.
- [ ] [LEV 25:48](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/25/#v48) L/B · word_choice · "무르다" also for buying back a person — The same Hebrew verb is used for land and for a person who sold himself; Korean keeps "무르다" so the two laws stay linked, and Readable says "다시 사서 풀어 주다".
- [ ] [LEV 26:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/26/#v16) L/B · word_choice · "갑작스러운 공포", "폐병" (Literal) / "몸을 마르게 하는 병" (Balanced), "열병" — The Hebrew names a wasting disease; Literal uses the traditional "폐병" only as a general name for a wasting illness, and Balanced describes it rather than naming a modern diagnosis.
- [ ] [LEV 26:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/26/#v25) L · word_choice · "언약의 원수를 갚는 칼" for "a sword avenging the vengeance of the covenant" — "원수를 갚다" is the plain Korean idiom for taking vengeance; Balanced says it is the penalty for breaking the covenant.
- [ ] [LEV 27:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/27/#v12) L · word_choice · "제사장이여, 네가 매기는 값대로" keeps the Hebrew turn to the priest at Literal — The English Literal reads the phrase as an address to the priest; Korean keeps it with the vocative "-이여".
- [ ] [LEV 27:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/27/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "무르다" (Literal, Balanced), "다시 사다" (Readable) for redeeming what was vowed — As in chapter 25: "무르다" is the Korean verb for taking back by paying the price; Readable uses the plainer "다시 사다".
- [ ] [LEV 27:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/27/#v27) L/B · word_choice · "되찾다" for ransom (*padah*) and "무르다" for redeem (*gaʾal*) — The meaning decision notes two Hebrew words for buying back; Korean keeps two verbs at Literal and Balanced, and Readable uses "다시 사다" for both.
- [ ] [LEV 27:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lev/27/#v29) B/R · word_choice · "멸망에 바쳐지다" for a person placed under the ban — The meaning decision's footnote explains the ban; Balanced and Readable say plainly that the person is given over to destruction, as the English does, while Literal keeps "온전히 바쳐진" from verse 28.

## 민수기 (18)

- [ ] [NUM 5:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/5/#v19) L/B/R · register · The priest speaks to the woman in 해라체 with "너" — The priest pronounces a set oath in God's name, in the same plain style as the law around it; a polite style would make the words his own conversation rather than the formula of the rite. A reviewer may prefer a softer level here.
- [ ] [NUM 5:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/5/#v21) L/B · word_choice · "넓적다리" for the thigh at Literal and Balanced; "태" (womb) at Readable — Literal and Balanced keep the Hebrew body word, which the footnoted meaning decision explains as harm that ends her ability to bear children; Readable follows the English Readable in naming the womb.
- [ ] [NUM 10:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/10/#v29) L/B · word_order · "모세의 장인인 미디안 사람 르우엘의 아들 호밥": the phrase "모세의 장인인" stands first, so it can belong to Reuel or to Hobab — The meaning decision leaves open whether Reuel or Hobab is Moses' father-in-law. Placing "모세의 장인인" before the whole name lets Korean keep the same ambiguity; putting it next to "호밥" would decide for Hobab.
- [ ] [NUM 10:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/10/#v29) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks to Hobab in 합쇼체 with "당신" and "우리"; Hobab answers in the plain style (verse 30) — Hobab is Moses' elder by marriage, so Moses speaks to him respectfully; Hobab, as the elder, answers in the plain style. A reviewer may prefer 해요체 for both.
- [ ] [NUM 11:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/11/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "수박" for the melons — The Hebrew word names a large melon of the Nile valley, most likely the watermelon, which was grown in ancient Egypt; "참외" is a Korean melon the Hebrew does not mean.
- [ ] [NUM 11:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/11/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "영" for the Spirit on Moses (ruach), at every level — The meaning decision reads this as God's Spirit given to Moses for his task; the English marks this with a capital S, which Korean cannot do. "성령" is the New Testament name and would read later doctrine into the passage, so the plain "영", with God as the giver, is kept through 11:17-29.
- [ ] [NUM 14:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/14/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "우리의 밥" (L), "우리 밥" (B); "밥 한 그릇 먹듯이" (R) — The Hebrew word for bread also means food in general; Korean "밥" is the everyday word for food and "우리 밥이다" is itself a Korean idiom for an easy opponent, so the picture and its sense (meaning decision) both come through.
- [ ] [NUM 16:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/16/#v3) L/B/R · register · The rebels speak to Moses and Aaron in 합쇼체 with "당신들" — As the term sheet for the people speaking to Moses and Aaron; their defiance is carried by what they say ("너무 지나칩니다"), not by dropping the formal style, which keeps the scene's speech levels the same as in chapters 14 and 16:12-14.
- [ ] [NUM 17:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/17/#v11) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks to Aaron in 해라체 at Literal; at Balanced and Readable he calls him "형님" and speaks in 해요체; "형님의 향로" (B, R) — As settled for these books and recorded at 12:11: Aaron is the older brother (Exodus 7:7), so Balanced and Readable give Moses a younger brother's respectful family speech, while Literal keeps the plain style of the narrative's commands. "형님" makes Aaron's own censer explicit, as the English "your …
- [ ] [NUM 25:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/25/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "매달다" (L), "처형하여 매달다" (B), "처형하고 시체를 기둥에 매달다" (R) for impaling — The meaning decision says putting to death and showing the bodies on stakes, not hanging by the neck; each level says more of that as the English does.
- [ ] [NUM 27:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/27/#v3) R · register · "돌아가셨습니다" at Readable for the father's death; "죽었습니다" at Literal and Balanced — A daughter speaking of her own father before others would naturally use the honorific verb; Literal and Balanced keep the plain verb of the Hebrew, as the Korean narrative does elsewhere.
- [ ] [NUM 28:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/28/#v22) R · supplied_words · "그것으로 너희의 죄를 씻어" at Readable — The English Readable keeps "make atonement"; Korean Readable adds a short phrase so the Sino-Korean "속죄하다" is understood, following the meaning decision (removing the people's sin so they can stay near God).
- [ ] [NUM 30:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/30/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "영혼을 괴롭게 하다" (Literal), "자신을 낮추어 절제하다" (Balanced), "음식이나 다른 것을 끊다" (Readable) — The idiom is denying oneself, as by fasting (meaning decision); Korean Leviticus 16 and 23 render the same idiom "자신을 괴롭게 하다" / "금식하며 자신을 낮추다". Here it covers more than fasting, so Balanced uses "절제하다".
- [ ] [NUM 31:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/31/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "이스라엘의 천천" (Literal), "이스라엘의 여러 가문" (Balanced, Readable) for "the thousands of Israel" — The thousands here are the clans of Israel (meaning decision); Literal keeps the Hebrew word in "천천" ("thousands upon thousands" in older Korean), Balanced and Readable name the clans.
- [ ] [NUM 31:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/31/#v15) L/B/R · register · Moses rebukes the officers in 해라체 at every level, Readable included — The officers are under his command and the question is a rebuke (meaning decision); 합쇼체 with "여러분", used at Readable when Moses addresses all Israel, would soften it. Balanced "단 말이냐" carries the rebuke.
- [ ] [NUM 31:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/31/#v21) L/B/R · register · Eleazar speaks to the soldiers in 해라체 at every level — The priest teaches the rules of clean and unclean to the men returning from battle (meaning decision); he speaks with the authority of the law, as Moses does to the officers in verse 15.
- [ ] [NUM 31:50](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/31/#v50) L/B/R · word_choice · "팔찌" (armlet), "손목고리" (bracelet), "인장 반지" (signet ring), "귀고리", "목걸이" (ornament) — The Midianite jewelry (meaning decision); "팔찌" is worn on the upper arm here and "손목고리" on the wrist, to keep the two Hebrew words apart; the last word is uncertain and is taken as a necklace, as the English does.
- [ ] [NUM 32:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/num/32/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "내 주" (Literal), "주인" (Balanced), "지도자님" (Readable) for "my lord" said to Moses — Polite speech to Moses (meaning decision); the Genesis sheet gives "내 주" for "my lord" to a man. Balanced uses "주인" so that "주" alone is not read as the divine name, and Readable uses the plain respectful title.

## 신명기 (18)

So at Readable Moses says "저" throughout his speeches to Israel, as he already does in chapters 7-15 and 9:9; the words …
- [ ] [DEU 2:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/2/#v12) R · register · The asides of verses 10-12 and 20-23 are in 합쇼체 at Readable, as part of Moses' address — The English sets these notes inside Moses' speech, in parentheses; Readable keeps the speech level of the address around them so the text does not seem to change speaker.
- [ ] [DEU 3:26](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/3/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · "노하다" for the anger of verse 26 — The meaning decision notes a play on "cross over" in the Hebrew verb; Korean cannot keep it, and the footnote of the meaning decision carries it.
- [ ] [DEU 4:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/4/#v29) L/B · word_choice · "마음을 다하고 목숨을 다하여" for "with all your heart and with all your soul"; Readable "온 마음과 온 힘을 다해" — Nefesh is the whole living self; "목숨" keeps the sense of one's very life (as the Korean John renders psyche), and the phrase will recur in 6:5. Readable uses a plainer pair, as the English Readable says "being".
- [ ] [DEU 4:48](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/4/#v48) L/B/R · word_choice · "시욘" for Siyon, kept apart from "시온" (Zion) — The Hebrew name here is not the name of Zion; writing "시온" would make readers think of Jerusalem. The meaning decision's footnote explains that it is another name for Hermon.
- [ ] [DEU 5:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/5/#v22) L/B · word_choice · "회중" for the assembly — The term sheet keeps "총회" for qahal as a formal gathering; here the whole people stand at the mountain as one body, which the Korean Exodus calls "회중". Uncertain: a reviewer may prefer "총회" to keep the Hebrew word distinct.

"한 분" says one and unique of a person; the meaning decisions' footnotes give the other ways the line can be read. The wording is drafted …
- [ ] [DEU 6:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/6/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "마음", "목숨" (Readable "생명"), "힘" for heart, soul and strength — Nefesh is the whole living self, which "목숨" (Readable "생명") says, as at 4:29; the familiar published wording uses other words for the second term, which the meaning decision does not support.
- [ ] [DEU 6:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/6/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "파 놓은 우물" (Literal), "바위를 파서 만든 물 저장소" (Balanced) and "빗물을 모아 두려고 바위를 파서 만든 웅덩이" (Readable) for hewn cisterns — The meaning decision explains that these are pits cut in rock to store rainwater, not springs; Korean has no single common word, so the levels describe them more fully as they go.
- [ ] [DEU 18:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/18/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "점을 치는 자", "길흉을 말하는 자", "징조를 풀이하는 자", "요술을 부리는 자" — Plain modern words for four kinds of divination and magic, kept distinct; the Korean Bibles' older terms are avoided. "점을 치다" matches Leviticus 19:26 in this translation.
- [ ] [DEU 21:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/21/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "맏아들의 권리" — Genesis uses "장자권" for Esau's birthright; here the law is about any family's eldest son, and "맏아들의 권리" links the word to "맏아들" (firstborn) in the same verses. A reviewer may prefer "장자권" for consistency with Genesis.
- [ ] [DEU 23:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/23/#v3) L · word_choice · "사생아" (Literal), "금지된 관계에서 태어난 사람" (Balanced, Readable) — The meaning of mamzer is uncertain (the meaning decision footnotes it); the Literal uses the nearest single Korean word, the other levels the reading the English gives.
- [ ] [DEU 23:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/23/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "신전 창녀", "신전 남창" (Literal), "신전에서 몸을 파는 사람" (Balanced, Readable) — The words describe sexual service tied to a shrine, as the meaning decision says; plain modern words are used rather than the older Bible terms.
- [ ] [DEU 25:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/25/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "남편의 형제 된 의무" (Literal), "남편의 형제로서 할 의무" (Balanced), a description at the Readable — Korean has no everyday word for levirate marriage; the technical terms would puzzle most readers, so the levels describe the duty, as the English "husband's brother" does.
- [ ] [DEU 28:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/28/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "종양과 부스럼과 옴" (L, B); "혹이 나고 가려운 피부병" (R) — The names of these diseases are uncertain; Korean uses general words for swellings, scabbing sores and itch rather than naming a modern disease.
- [ ] [DEU 32:42](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/32/#v42) L/B/R · word_choice · "머리를 길게 기른 우두머리들" (L, B); "우두머리들" (R) — The word is uncertain: long-haired warriors or the enemy's chiefs; Korean keeps both at Literal and Balanced, Readable only the leaders, as the English does.
- [ ] [DEU 33:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/deu/33/#v2) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks about God in the plain written style with the honorific, to God in 합쇼체 with "주님" (verses 3, 7-11), and to the tribes and to Israel in 해라체 with "너" … — The blessing is a dying father's last words over his family (meaning decision on verse 1), so it takes the plain style Jacob uses for his sons in the Korean Genesis 49, at every level; the 합쇼체 of Moses' speeches at Readable is a public address and would not fit a blessing spoken over each tribe by …
