Korean must mark the subject: the honorific -시- would name God, the plain verb would suggest people. Literal keeps the bare "그가" as the English keeps "he"; Balanced and Readable use the …

## 여호수아 (16)

- [ ] [JOS 5:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/5/#v3) L/B · word_choice · "할례산" (L, B), "포피 언덕" in quotation marks (R) — The Hebrew place name means "hill of the foreskins". Literal and Balanced give it as a short place name; Readable says what the name means, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [JOS 5:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/5/#v14) L/B/R · register · the commander speaks to Joshua in the plain style; Joshua answers in the formal polite style, with "내 주" and "종" ("저" at Readable) — Joshua honors the commander as a superior, the way a servant speaks to a master. "내 주" is the form the terms of this translation give for "my lord" said to someone other than God; "주님" is not used, because the text does not say whether he is the LORD himself.
- [ ] [JOS 9:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/9/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "종" at every level — The English uses "slaves" at every level here because the curse is servitude itself. Korean "종" already means a bond servant, and the terms of this translation keep "노예" for slavery as an institution; here the point is perpetual service at the sanctuary, which "종" carries.
- [ ] [JOS 10:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/10/#v4) L/B/R · register · king to king in the old formal style of equals (하오체: "도와주시오", "칩시다") — Adoni-Zedek writes to other kings as his equals. Korean has a formal style used between equals of rank, which fits a message between kings better than the plain style used for orders or the humble style used to a superior.
- [ ] [JOS 13:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/13/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "유산" for the inheritance (*nahalah*) at Literal and Balanced; Readable says "길이 차지할 땅" — The terms note gives "유산, 기업". "유산" is the current word for what passes down in a family; "기업" is the church word, but to most readers today it means a business firm. Readable says what the word means, land held for good (meaning decision m4). This holds for chapters 13-24.
- [ ] [JOS 14:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/14/#v6) L/B/R · register · Caleb speaks to Joshua in the formal polite style (합쇼체), calling himself "저" and Joshua "당신" — Caleb and Joshua were the two faithful spies, but Joshua is now the leader who gives out the land and Caleb comes with a request, so he speaks up to him. Quoting Moses (verse 9), he gives Moses's words in the plain style a leader uses to the one he speaks to.
- [ ] [JOS 14:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/14/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "혹시 ... 계시면" (L); "그래도 ... 계시면" (B); "계실지 모릅니다" (R) for "perhaps" — Caleb speaks with humble hope, not doubt (meaning decision m3). Korean "혹시" keeps the Hebrew "perhaps" at Literal; Balanced and Readable let his confidence in the LORD's word show in the sentence that follows.
- [ ] [JOS 15:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/15/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "형제" (L); "아우" (B, R) for "the brother of Caleb" — Korean must say whether a brother is older or younger. Literal keeps the open word "형제"; Balanced and Readable say "아우", since Caleb was already eighty-five (14:10) and Othniel lived on to be Israel's first judge. Meaning decision m1 notes that "Caleb's brother" may describe Kenaz instead; Korean …
- [ ] [JOS 17:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/17/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "세 고지" (L); "세 언덕 지역" (B, R) — The phrase is unclear (meaning decision m3); it is taken, as the English takes it, to name three hill districts. Literal keeps it short as the Hebrew does, and "딸들" for the villages, as in 15:45.
- [ ] [JOS 17:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/17/#v14) L/B/R · register · Joseph's descendants speak to Joshua in the formal polite style (합쇼체); "나" (L) and "우리" (B, R) — The tribes speak as one people, "me" and "I" in the Hebrew (meaning decision m1); Literal keeps the singular, and Balanced and Readable say "우리", as the English does. Their complaint is bold, so they keep "우리" rather than the humbler "저희". Joshua answers them in the plain style as their leader.
- [ ] [JOS 22:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/22/#v16) L/B/R · register · The delegation and the eastern tribes speak to each other in the formal polite style (합쇼체); Literal keeps "너희", Balanced and Readable say "여러분" — They are tribes of one people meeting as equals, and the delegation speaks for the whole congregation in a formal message. The formal polite style keeps that standing while the words carry the rebuke. "너희" at Literal follows the Hebrew "you"; "여러분" is the respectful plural of current Korean.
- [ ] [JOS 22:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/22/#v27) L/B/R · word_order · The plain style for the tribes' words among themselves ("쌓자", "-것이다"), inside their polite answer; "여러분" (B, R) where they speak of the westerners — Verses 26-28 quote what the eastern tribes said among themselves, so it is in the plain style; where they name the western tribes they keep the respectful "여러분" they use in the answer itself. Literal keeps "너희".
- [ ] [JOS 24:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/24/#v7) L/B/R · register · God speaks of himself by name with the honorific ("주께서 ... 두시고"), then again as "나" — In the Hebrew, God names himself in the third person here and then says "I" again (meaning decision m2). Korean keeps that shift; where the LORD is named, the narrator's honorific is kept, as it would be in any sentence about God.
- [ ] [JOS 24:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/24/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "여리고의 주민들" (L); "여리고의 유력한 사람들" (B); "여리고 사람들" (R) for "the lords of Jericho" — The Hebrew word means the leading citizens or landowners of the city (meaning decision m1), not its kings. Literal says "주민들" and keeps the list as one sentence; Balanced names them as the city's leading men.
- [ ] [JOS 24:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/24/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "왕벌" for the hornet — The meaning of the Hebrew word is uncertain (meaning decision m1); Korean keeps the usual sense, the hornet, as the English does.
- [ ] [JOS 24:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jos/24/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "질투하시는 하나님" for "a jealous God" — A God who demands his people's loyalty for himself alone (meaning decision m3). Korean "질투" can sound petty, but it is the word Korean readers know from the Ten Commandments, and Readable adds what it means, as the English Readable does.

## 사사기 (30)

- [ ] [JDG 1:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/1/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "한결같은 사랑" (L); "신의" (B); keeping a promise (R) for *hesed* between people — The terms of this translation keep "한결같은 사랑" for God's loyal love and use other words for kindness between people. Here the spies promise to keep faith with a stranger, so Balanced uses "신의" (keeping faith) and Readable says what that means. The spies speak to a stranger in the semi-formal style …
- [ ] [JDG 3:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/3/#v16) B/R · setting_alternative · the measures setting gives "45센티미터쯤" (metric) by default and "한 규빗" (one cubit) as the alternative — Korean readers use metric measures, so the modern default is metric, as in the other languages of this translation; Literal keeps the Hebrew measure. The Hebrew word is not the usual cubit and may be a short one (m2), so the figure is approximate.
- [ ] [JDG 4:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/4/#v6) L/B/R · register · Deborah speaks to Barak in the plain style (해라체); Barak answers her in the formal polite style (합쇼체) — Deborah speaks as the judge of Israel and as a prophet bringing God's command, and Barak answers the leader who summoned him. A reviewer may prefer a softer style for Deborah.
- [ ] [JDG 6:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/6/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "표징" for the sign Gideon asks for — "표징" is a plain word for a token or proof; "표적" is kept for the miraculous signs of the Gospels. Gideon speaks to God in the formal polite style with "주님" and "저", as in prayer.
- [ ] [JDG 6:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/6/#v23) B · word_choice · "평안" (peace) at Literal and Readable; Balanced "안심하여라" (be at peace, be safe) — The greeting here promises safety and well-being (m1); Balanced says it as a Korean speaker would say it to a frightened man, and the next verse names the altar with "평안".
- [ ] [JDG 6:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/6/#v30) B/R · register · the townsmen speak to Joash in the semi-formal style (하오체) at Balanced and Readable; Literal keeps the plain style — Men of the town making a hard demand of an older man of standing speak bluntly but not as to an inferior; Joash answers them in the plain style as the head of the clan.
- [ ] [JDG 6:34](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/6/#v34) L/B · word_choice · "옷 입듯 덮었다" (L) and "감싸 덮었다" (B) for the Spirit "clothing" Gideon; Readable "임하여 그를 사로잡았다" — The Hebrew verb here is "clothed", not the "came on" of 3:10, and Literal and Balanced keep the picture of the Spirit wrapping Gideon like a garment; Readable uses the usual Korean word with what the picture means (m1).
- [ ] [JDG 7:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/7/#v13) B/R · register · the two Midianite soldiers speak to each other as friends, in the familiar style (하게체, B) and the plain informal style (R) — Two comrades on guard talk freely; Korean gives them the speech of equals, which Literal keeps in the plain written style.
- [ ] [JDG 8:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/8/#v1) L/B/R · register · Ephraim and Gideon speak to each other in the semi-formal style (하오체) — Two tribes in a dispute about honor speak as equals who keep their distance; Korean shows this with the semi-formal style, which Gideon keeps as he calms them.
- [ ] [JDG 8:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/8/#v5) L/B/R · register · Gideon and the men of Succoth and Penuel speak in the semi-formal style (하오체) — A commander asking a town for help, and its leaders refusing him, speak as men of standing who are not friends; the same style holds through verse 9 and in verse 15.
- [ ] [JDG 8:35](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/8/#v35) B/R · word_choice · "한결같은 사랑" (L); "의리를 지키다" (B, R) for *hesed* owed to Gideon's family — Between people Korean keeps "한결같은 사랑" for Literal and uses a word for faithfulness owed in return for kindness at the other levels (m1); "의리" is the Korean word for loyalty owed to someone who has done you good.
- [ ] [JDG 9:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/9/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "영주들" (L) and "지도자들" (B, R) for the *baalim* of Shechem — The Hebrew word means the city's owners and masters, its leading landholders (m1); Literal keeps that sense with "영주", and the other levels say "leaders", as the English does, through the chapter.
- [ ] [JDG 9:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/9/#v31) L/B/R · register · Zebul writes to Abimelech in the formal polite style (합쇼체) with "임금님" — The governor reports to the man who made himself king; Korean shows his place by the speech level. With Gaal at the gate (verses 36, 38) Zebul speaks in the semi-formal style (하오체), as one official to another.
- [ ] [JDG 11:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/11/#v6) L/B/R · register · The elders speak to Jephthah in the formal polite style (합쇼체); Jephthah answers them in the plain style (해라체) with "너희" — The elders come asking for help from a man they once drove out, and they speak up to him. Jephthah speaks as one who now holds the stronger hand; the plain style also carries his bitterness in verse 7. Reviewers may prefer a more even level between them.
- [ ] [JDG 11:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/11/#v12) L/B/R · register · Jephthah and the king of Ammon address each other in the plain style (해라체) with "너" — The two leaders speak as equals on opposite sides of a quarrel; neither owes the other the honorific. The messengers carry each man's words as he spoke them.
- [ ] [JDG 11:37](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/11/#v37) L · word_choice · "처녀로 죽음을 두고 울다" (weep over dying a virgin) — Literal Korean cannot say "weep over my virginity" without sounding strange; it keeps the word "처녀" and says what the mourning is for in as few words as it can.
- [ ] [JDG 12:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/12/#v5) B/R · register · The fleeing Ephraimites speak in the 하오체 ("주시오", "아니오") at Balanced and Readable — A stranger asking to cross, trying not to give himself away, speaks neither down nor humbly; the guards answer in the plain style.
- [ ] [JDG 14:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/14/#v16) L/B/R · register · Samson's wife speaks to him in the polite informal style (해요체); Samson answers her in the 하오체 with "당신" — Husband and wife speak with mutual respect, as the terms set; Samson's 하오체 is a little firmer and older-sounding, which suits his refusal. A reviewer may prefer 해요체 for both.
- [ ] [JDG 15:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/15/#v2) L/B/R · register · Samson's father-in-law speaks to him in the 하게체 with "자네" — This is how an older Korean man speaks to his son-in-law: with some respect, but from above. Samson's angry reply in verse 3 is in the plain style.
- [ ] [JDG 15:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/15/#v11) L/B/R · register · The men of Judah and Samson speak to each other in the 하오체 with "당신" — They are fellow Israelites in a tense meeting, neither above the other; the 하오체 keeps distance without contempt.
- [ ] [JDG 16:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/16/#v5) L/B/R · register · The Philistine rulers speak to Delilah in the plain style (해라체); Delilah speaks to Samson in the polite informal style (해요체), and he answers her in the 하오체 — The rulers give orders to a woman they are paying. Between Samson and Delilah the speech levels follow those of Samson and his wife in chapter 14.
- [ ] [JDG 16:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/16/#v16) L · word_choice · "그의 영혼이 죽을 지경으로 괴로웠다" (his soul was distressed to the point of death) — The Hebrew says his soul was "short". Korean cannot say that of a soul; Literal keeps "영혼" and "죽을 지경", and Balanced and Readable say that he was worn out.
- [ ] [JDG 16:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/16/#v30) L/B/R · register · Samson's last words keep the formal polite style (합쇼체) of his prayer — "Let my soul die" is spoken in the same breath as the prayer of verse 28, so Korean treats it as said to God.
- [ ] [JDG 17:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/17/#v9) L/B/R · register · Micah speaks to the young Levite in the 하게체 with "자네"; the Levite answers in the formal polite style (합쇼체) — Micah is the older man and the householder who goes on to hire him; the 하게체 is how an older Korean man speaks to a younger one he respects but stands above.
- [ ] [JDG 18:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/18/#v3) L/B/R · register · The Danites speak to the young Levite in the 하오체 with "당신"; he answers them in the formal polite style (합쇼체) — They meet as strangers on the road; the armed men speak without deference, the young priest politely. Among themselves the Danites use the plain style.
- [ ] [JDG 19:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/19/#v5) L/B/R · register · The father-in-law speaks to the Levite in the 하게체 with "자네"; the Levite's few words to him would be in the formal polite style — As with Samson's father-in-law in chapter 15: an older man speaking to his son-in-law, warmly and from above.
- [ ] [JDG 19:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/19/#v20) L/B/R · register · The old man moves into the 하오체 once he takes the Levite under his roof — He greets the stranger politely and then speaks as host and elder, offering protection; the 하오체 keeps the courtesy while showing his standing.
- [ ] [JDG 20:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/20/#v2) L · word_choice · "우두머리들" at Literal for the Hebrew "corners" — The Hebrew calls the leaders the "corners" of the people, the stones that hold a building together. Korean "모퉁이들" would not be understood as people, so Literal says "우두머리들" (chiefs) and Balanced and Readable "지도자들".
- [ ] [JDG 20:43](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/20/#v43) L/B/R · word_choice · "므누하" as a place name — The Hebrew word can also mean "resting place"; Korean follows the English in reading it as a place, Menuhah.
- [ ] [JDG 21:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jdg/21/#v22) L/B/R · register · The elders speak to the fathers and brothers of Shiloh in the 하오체 with "당신들" — They speak to fellow Israelites as equals in a delicate matter; the 하오체 is courteous without being humble.

## 룻기 (7)

- [ ] [RUT 1:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rut/1/#v20) L/B/R · register · Naomi speaks to the women of the town in the formal polite style (합쇼체) — She speaks to a crowd of neighbours after ten years away, publicly and with some formality; her bitter words are her own, not a change in her standing.
- [ ] [RUT 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rut/2/#v3) L · word_choice · "우연히도" (as chance had it) — The Hebrew says "her chance chanced", a doubled phrase. Korean cannot double it; Literal says "우연히도" and Balanced and Readable "마침", which keep the narrator's quiet hint that it was more than chance.
- [ ] [RUT 2:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rut/2/#v20) L · word_choice · Literal leaves open whose "한결같은 사랑" it is — The Hebrew can mean the LORD's faithful love or Boaz's; Literal keeps the open wording as the English Literal does, and Balanced and Readable follow the English in naming the LORD.
- [ ] [RUT 3:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rut/3/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "여섯 번 되어" (measured six times) — The Hebrew does not name the measure; Korean says Boaz measured the barley out six times, which keeps the number without naming a unit.
- [ ] [RUT 4:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rut/4/#v1) L/B/R · register · Boaz and the nearer relative speak to each other in the 하오체 — Two landowners of the same town speak as equals before witnesses; Boaz speaks to the elders in the formal polite style (합쇼체).
- [ ] [RUT 4:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rut/4/#v11) L/B/R · register · The people and elders answer Boaz in the 하오체 — They speak to a respected man of their own town as equals in a public blessing.
- [ ] [RUT 4:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rut/4/#v14) L/B/R · register · The women speak to Naomi in the formal polite style (합쇼체) — As in 1:19-21, where Naomi spoke to them in the same style; the neighbours honour an older woman of the town.

## 사무엘상 (2)

- [ ] [1SA 30:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1sa/30/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "어스름" (L) and "해 질 녘" / "해 질 무렵" (B, R) for the Hebrew twilight — The Hebrew word can mean dusk or dawn; with "until the evening of the next day" most read it as dusk, the fight lasting about a day (meaning decision m1). Literal keeps the neutral "어스름".
- [ ] [1SA 30:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1sa/30/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "호르마", "보라산", "아닥" — Bor-ashan follows the Hebrew text as the English data reads it; the older Korean form "고라산" follows the other reading (Kor-ashan). A reviewer should confirm the Korean form.

## 사무엘하 (26)

- [ ] [2SA 1:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/1/#v4) R · supplied_words · "사울 임금님" at Readable — A Korean speaker would not name a dead king bare to another leader; Readable adds the title the man would naturally use.
- [ ] [2SA 1:26](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/1/#v26) L/B/R · register · David addresses the dead Jonathan with the poetic "그대" in the lament — In a song of mourning, "그대" is the natural Korean second person of poetry: warm and close without being casual.
- [ ] [2SA 2:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/2/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "성읍" for the towns (*ʿarim*) of Judah — "성읍" is the usual Bible word for a walled town; it is kept at every level here and in the book, and Readable uses it too because the story often turns on a town's walls and gate.
- [ ] [2SA 2:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/2/#v5) B/R · register · David's message to the men of Jabesh is in the formal polite style with "여러분" at Balanced and Readable; Literal keeps the plain style with "너희" — David writes as the new king of Judah to men of another region whose loyalty he wants to win; a respectful address fits the diplomatic letter better than commands.
- [ ] [2SA 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/2/#v14) L/B/R · register · Abner and Joab, the two rival commanders, speak to each other in the plain style (해라체) — They are equals in rank and enemies in the field; the plain style keeps their speech blunt, as the scene in verses 14-27 needs.
- [ ] [2SA 2:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/2/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "노루" for the gazelle (*tsevi*) — Korean readers know the roe deer as the swift wild animal of the fields; the comparison is about speed, not the exact species.
- [ ] [2SA 3:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/3/#v7) L/B/R · register · Ish-bosheth, as king, speaks to Abner in the plain style (해라체); Abner answers in the formal polite style (합쇼체), even in anger — The Samuel terms note has the king speak 해라체 to subjects and subjects answer in 합쇼체; Abner's anger shows in his words, not in a change of speech level.
- [ ] [2SA 3:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/3/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "개 머리" (Literal), "개 대가리" (Balanced), "쓸모없는 개" (Readable); Abner says "당신" to the king — Korean, like Hebrew, uses the dog as the picture of something despised; Balanced uses the coarse word for an animal's head to keep the insult, and Readable says what it means. Abner pointedly calls Ish-bosheth neither king nor lord in the Hebrew, so Korean keeps the bare "당신" within the polite …
- [ ] [2SA 3:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/3/#v13) L/B/R · register · David speaks to Abner in the plain style (해라체); Abner speaks to David in the formal polite style (합쇼체), calling him "당신" until he names him "내 주 임금님" (verse 21) — David is already a king and Abner comes to him offering to serve; the Samuel terms note has a king speak 해라체 to subjects.
- [ ] [2SA 3:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/3/#v29) L/B/R · grammar · David's curse is a wish ("-기를!") at Literal and a firm prediction ("-ㄹ 것이다!") at Balanced and Readable — Korean wishes of harm sound weak when spelled out as "I hope"; the strong future carries the force of a curse.
- [ ] [2SA 3:34](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/3/#v34) L/B/R · register · David's lament addresses the dead Abner with "너", in lines like the English — Abner was not David's covenant friend; the plain "너" of a lament fits a fallen commander.
- [ ] [2SA 5:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/5/#v1) L/B/R · register · The tribes speak to David in the formal polite style (합쇼체) and call him "당신", since he is not yet their king — They come to make him king; "임금님" begins once the covenant is made (verse 3). "당신" in 합쇼체 is respectful in this formal setting.
- [ ] [2SA 5:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/5/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "물길" (L, B) and "물이 흐르는 굴" (R) for *tsinnor* — The meaning decision reads the word as a water shaft into the city; Readable describes it.
- [ ] [2SA 5:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/5/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "발삼나무" (balsam tree) for *bekaʾim*, a tree not certainly known — The tree cannot be identified; Korean follows the English and the meaning decision rather than the older Korean "mulberry", which is no more certain.
- [ ] [2SA 6:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/6/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "수금, 비파, 소고, 방울, 심벌즈" for the five instruments; "잣나무" for the wood — The traditional Korean names for the stringed instruments and the drum are kept; "심벌즈" is today's word. The tree (*berosh*) is uncertain, cypress or juniper; Korean follows the English.
- [ ] [2SA 6:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/6/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "번제" and "화목제" for the burnt offerings and peace offerings — The established Korean names of the two offerings; the English keeps the offerings' names at every level too.
- [ ] [2SA 6:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/6/#v20) L/B/R · register · Michal and David speak in the polite style (해요체) at Balanced and Readable, the plain style at Literal; Michal's mocking honorifics for "the king of Israel" … — As the Genesis terms note sets for husband and wife; Michal speaks of David in the third person as "the king", and Korean honorifics used in scorn make the irony plain.
- [ ] [2SA 7:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/7/#v2) L/B/R · register · The king speaks to the prophet Nathan in the plain style at Literal and in the semi-formal 하오체 at Balanced and Readable; Nathan answers in 합쇼체 with "임금님" — Nathan is the king's subject but also the bearer of God's word; 하오체 is the traditional register of a king speaking with respect to an elder or counsellor.
- [ ] [2SA 7:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/7/#v11) B/R · grammar · God names himself in the third person without the honorific at Balanced and Readable ("{{divine_name:주|야훼}}가 ... 세워 주겠다"); Literal keeps the honorific of the … — In Korean a speaker does not honor himself; when God speaks of himself by name, Balanced and Readable drop the honorific particle so the words are clearly his own.
- [ ] [2SA 8:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/8/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "놋쇠" for *neḥoshet* (bronze) — The Bible word for the metal; "청동" is the technical alternative. A reviewer may prefer "청동" at Readable.
- [ ] [2SA 8:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/8/#v16) L/B · word_choice · "사관" (recorder) at Literal and Balanced; "왕실의 기록을 맡았다" at Readable — "사관" is the Korean court title for the official who keeps the records.
- [ ] [2SA 10:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/10/#v3) B/R · word_choice · The Ammonite officials call Hanun "임금님" at Balanced and Readable; Literal keeps the Hebrew "당신" — Korean courtiers would address their king by title; "당신" to a king sounds rude in Korean, though the Hebrew has only "you".
- [ ] [2SA 11:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/11/#v4) B · supplied_words · "월경 뒤의" (after her monthly period) added at Balanced — Korean "부정함" alone would suggest moral impurity; the meaning decision says this was the washing after her period.
- [ ] [2SA 11:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/11/#v8) B · supplied_words · "쉬어라" (rest) added to "wash your feet" at Balanced as well as Readable — In Korean the bare command to wash one's feet sounds like a hygiene order; adding "and rest" keeps the idiom's sense of going home to its comforts.
- [ ] [2SA 12:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/12/#v18) B/R · register · Among themselves the servants speak plainly but give the king the honorific -시- ("하지 않으셨는데"); Literal keeps the Hebrew's bare "he" — Korean servants would honor the king even when speaking about him among themselves.
- [ ] [2SA 12:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2sa/12/#v25) B · supplied_words · "나단은" (Nathan) named as the one who gave the name at Balanced — Korean needs a subject for the naming; the meaning decision says the name came from the LORD through Nathan. Literal keeps the unspoken subject.

## 열왕기상 (25)

- [ ] [1KI 1:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/1/#v11) L/B/R · register · Nathan and Bathsheba speak 합쇼체 to each other; Nathan calls her "부인" (B, R) — The prophet speaks with respect to the king's wife and the mother of a king's son, and she answers in kind; the Hebrew gives no title, so Literal keeps none.
- [ ] [1KI 1:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/1/#v13) L/B/R · register · David speaks to Bathsheba in 하오체 with "당신", here and wherever his oath is quoted — The king speaks to his wife with the respectful 하오체 of a king to a queen; Genesis gives husband and wife 당신, kept here.
- [ ] [1KI 1:33](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/1/#v33) L/B/R · register · David speaks to Zadok, Nathan and Benaiah together in 하오체 with "그대들" — The Samuel note has David speak 하오체 to Nathan; addressing the prophet, the priest and the commander together, he keeps that register.
- [ ] [1KI 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/2/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "규례, 계명, 법도, 증거" (L, B); "법, 명령, 규칙, 가르침" (R) — Four words for the covenant's requirements, kept distinct as the English keeps them; Readable uses plain words.
- [ ] [1KI 2:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/2/#v13) L/B/R · register · Bathsheba speaks to Adonijah in 하오체; he answers in 합쇼체 — The king's mother speaks to the king's older half-brother with reserve but not as to a servant; he, asking a favor, speaks up to her.
- [ ] [1KI 2:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/2/#v20) L/B/R · register · Bathsheba speaks to her son the king in 하오체; Solomon speaks to her in 합쇼체 with "어머니" — The king honors his mother, rising and bowing to her (verse 19); she, as queen mother, speaks to the king with respect but from above.
- [ ] [1KI 2:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/2/#v30) L/B/R · register · Benaiah and Joab speak 하오체 to each other — Two commanders of equal rank, the one bringing the king's order and the other refusing it; neither speaks up or down.
- [ ] [1KI 3:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/3/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "한결같은 사랑" (L); "인애" (B, R) for *ḥesed* — As the Samuel terms note: one wording for *ḥesed* through these books, Literal and the others apart.
- [ ] [1KI 3:26](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/3/#v26) L · idiom · "창자가 뜨거워졌다" — The Hebrew word for compassion is the inward parts; Literal keeps the picture, which Korean also uses (애가 타다, 창자가 끊어지다), and Balanced and Readable say the feeling.
- [ ] [1KI 5:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/5/#v17) L/B/R · register · Solomon and Hiram write to each other in 합쇼체, calling each other "왕" — Messages between two allied kings are courteous on both sides; Korean diplomatic letters use the formal polite style.
- [ ] [1KI 5:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/5/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "잣나무" for *berosh* — The tree is a tall evergreen, perhaps a juniper, cypress or fir; 잣나무 is the name long used for it in Korean Bibles, kept as the common word through the temple chapters.
- [ ] [1KI 10:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/10/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "백단목" for almug wood — The tree cannot be identified with certainty; Korean uses the name long given to it in Korean Bibles, with the transliteration as the alternative.
- [ ] [1KI 11:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/11/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "동생" for Tahpenes' sister — Korean must choose between older and younger sister; 동생 is assumed, as the more natural reading of a king's gift of a bride.
- [ ] [1KI 11:41](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/11/#v41) L/B/R · word_choice · "솔로몬의 행적의 책" (L); "솔로몬 실록" (B); "솔로몬의 역사를 적은 책" (R); a question at Literal, a statement at Balanced and Readable — The source formula of the Kings and Chronicles terms note, with 실록 so the book is not confused with the title of Chronicles (역대기).
- [ ] [1KI 12:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/12/#v6) L/B/R · register · Rehoboam speaks to his father's elders in 하오체 ("그대들", "-소") — The king speaks down to his subjects, but these are the senior men of his father's court. 하오체 gives them the respect a king can show, as David speaks to Nathan and Barzillai in Samuel, and it sets them apart from the young men he speaks to as equals in 해라체.
- [ ] [1KI 13:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/13/#v6) L/B/R · register · the king speaks to the man of God in 하오체 with "그대" — As David speaks to Nathan and Gad in the Korean Samuel, a king addresses a prophet with 하오체: above him in rank, yet with respect. The prophets answer kings in 합쇼체, as the terms note sets.
- [ ] [1KI 13:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/13/#v14) L/B/R · register · the two prophets speak 합쇼체 to each other, with "당신" and "저" — They meet as strangers, and the Genesis note sets 합쇼체 between strangers and hosts. The old prophet keeps it even when he speaks the LORD's sentence (verses 21-22).
- [ ] [1KI 14:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/14/#v6) L/B/R · register · Ahijah speaks to the queen in 하오체 ("들어오시오", "-소") — The old prophet speaks with authority to the king's wife, as a prophet speaks with a king's household; 하오체 is respectful without deference.
- [ ] [1KI 14:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/14/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "이스라엘 왕들의 날들의 일들을 적은 책" (L), "이스라엘 왕 실록" (B), "이스라엘 왕들의 역사를 적은 책" (R) — The terms note's names for the lost royal record. "역대기" and "역대지" are avoided because they are the Korean titles of the books of Chronicles; Literal keeps the Hebrew "book of the matters of the days". The Readable wording is marked uncertain in the terms note.
- [ ] [1KI 15:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/15/#v19) L/B/R · register · one king writes to another in 하오체 with "당신" — Kings of neighboring lands address each other as equals; 하오체 is courteous without the deference of a subject.
- [ ] [1KI 17:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/17/#v1) L/B/R · register · Elijah speaks to Ahab in 하오체 ("-오", "-소") — The terms note lets Elijah speak to Ahab without deference. 하오체 keeps him respectful of the king's office while standing as his equal before the LORD; Ahab answers in 해라체 (18:17). Held through this block.
- [ ] [1KI 17:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/17/#v10) L/B/R · register · Elijah speaks to the widow in 하오체, and she answers in 합쇼체 — The prophet asks a favor of a poor stranger with courtesy but not deference; she, seeing a man of God (verse 18), speaks up to him.
- [ ] [1KI 18:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/18/#v7) L/B/R · register · Obadiah speaks to Elijah in 합쇼체, calling him "내 주" (L, B), "엘리야 어른" (R), and himself "당신의 종" (L, B), "저" (R); Elijah answers in 하오체 — The king's steward honors the prophet as his lord; Elijah answers him with the courtesy he shows Ahab. "어른" is the Readable address the Korean Samuel uses for Samuel.
- [ ] [1KI 20:34](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/20/#v34) L/B/R · register · the two kings speak to each other in 하오체 with "당신" — Ahab has called Ben-hadad his brother and seated him in his chariot; they now speak as equals, as Asa and Ben-hadad do in 15:19.
- [ ] [1KI 22:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ki/22/#v13) L/B/R · register · the messenger and Micaiah speak to each other in 하오체 — A court official addressing a prophet he has been sent to fetch: courteous without deference, as Elijah and Obadiah in chapter 18.

## 열왕기하 (45)

- [ ] [2KI 1:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/1/#v9) L/B/R · register · The first two commanders speak to Elijah in the semi-formal 하오체 ("내려오시오"), an order passed on with little respect; the third pleads in 합쇼체 (verse 13) — The commanders come to arrest the prophet in the king's name (m3); 하오체 lets Korean show the difference the story draws between them and the third commander, who kneels.
- [ ] [2KI 1:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/1/#v17) R · supplied_words · "그의 동생 여호람" at Readable — The English Readable says "his brother"; Korean must choose older or younger. Jehoram, another son of Ahab (3:1), followed his brother, and is taken as the younger.
- [ ] [2KI 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/2/#v3) L/B/R · register · Elisha and the prophets speak to each other in 하오체, as men of one company; "your master" is "당신의 주인" (L), "당신의 스승" (B, R) — Before Elijah is taken, Elisha is one of the prophets' peers; 하오체 is the respectful address between grown men. "스승" says what kind of master Elijah was to Elisha.
- [ ] [2KI 2:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/2/#v9) R · supplied_words · "하늘로 올라가게 된다" at Readable — The Hebrew says only "taken from you"; Readable makes the passive clear in Korean, which has no easy passive for "be taken", using what verse 1 has told the reader.
- [ ] [2KI 2:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/2/#v16) L/B/R · register · After bowing to Elisha (verse 15) the prophets speak to him in 합쇼체, calling him "어른" and themselves "어른의 종들" (L, B) or "저희" (R); Elisha now answers in the … — The change follows the story: they have accepted Elisha as Elijah's successor and leader (m2 of verse 15).
- [ ] [2KI 3:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/3/#v7) L/B/R · register · The two kings speak to each other in 하오체 with "당신" — Kings of two kingdoms speak as equals; 하오체 is the dignified address between grown men of rank.
- [ ] [2KI 3:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/3/#v13) L/B/R · register · Elisha speaks to Jehoram in 하오체 with "당신", and the king answers him in 하오체 — The terms note lets a scene set another level than 합쇼체; Elisha's open contempt for Ahab's son (m1) would sound false in the humble 합쇼체. 하오체 keeps a grown man's courtesy without deference.
- [ ] [2KI 4:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/4/#v13) L/B/R · register · Elisha speaks to the Shunammite in 하오체, calling her "부인"; she answers in 합쇼체 — The Shunammite is a woman of standing and Elisha's host (m1 of verse 8); 하오체 gives her a courtesy the plain style would not.
- [ ] [2KI 4:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/4/#v23) L/B/R · register · The older husband speaks to his wife in 하오체, and she answers in 해요체 — The husband is old (verse 14); 하오체 is how an older Korean husband would speak to his wife, while her 해요체 keeps the warmth of verse 9.
- [ ] [2KI 4:39](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/4/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "들덩굴" and "들외" (wild gourds); Readable says "덩굴에 달린 열매" — The meaning decision (m2) takes the plant as a wild gourd, not a grape; "들외" names a wild cucumber-like gourd, and Readable avoids the rare word.
- [ ] [2KI 6:33](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/6/#v33) B/R · supplied_words · "왕이 말했다" at Balanced and Readable — The meaning decision (m1) takes the words as the king's, who has followed his messenger (verse 32); Korean needs a named subject, since the plain style of the words would not fit the messenger speaking to Elisha. Literal keeps "그가".
- [ ] [2KI 8:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/8/#v1) L/B/R · register · Elisha speaks to the Shunammite here in the plain style — This is a prophet's warning given in God's name, and the woman now comes to him as one he has helped; the plain style of the warning follows the style Elisha uses for God's word.
- [ ] [2KI 9:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/9/#v22) L/B/R · register · Jehu answers the king in the plain style ("네 어머니", "-느냐") — Jehu speaks to Jehoram as a rebel who no longer owns him as king; dropping all honor shows the break the scene describes.
- [ ] [2KI 12:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/12/#v5) L/B/R · register · The king speaks to the priests in the plain style here; he is still young and under Jehoiada's teaching — The king gives an order to the temple priests; in verse 8, where he summons Jehoiada himself, he uses 하오체 out of respect for his guardian.
- [ ] [2KI 12:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/12/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "요사바드" for Jozabad and "여호사바드" for Jehozabad — The names follow the Hebrew as the English does; "여호사바드" keeps this official apart from King Jehoshaphat ("여호사밧"), whose name is spelled differently in Hebrew.
- [ ] [2KI 13:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/13/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "이스라엘 왕들의 날들의 일을 적은 책" (L); "이스라엘 왕 실록" (B); "이스라엘 왕들의 역사를 적은 책" (R) — The source formula of the terms note. The name must not be confused with the Korean title of Chronicles (역대기), so Balanced uses "실록"; the question stays at Literal and is a statement at Balanced and Readable.
- [ ] [2KI 13:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/13/#v14) L/B/R · register · "내 아버지, 내 아버지! 이스라엘의 병거와 그 기병이여!" (L), "...병거와 기병이시여!" (B, R), worded as Elisha's cry in 2:12 — The same words as the Korean of 2:12 at each level let the reader hear the echo; the terms note's "내 아버지여" is given up here so the two cries match.
- [ ] [2KI 13:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/13/#v15) L/B/R · register · Elisha speaks to the king in the formal style (합쇼체: "가져오십시오") — The terms note: prophets speak to kings in 합쇼체 unless the scene sets otherwise; the king, who has just called him "my father", is still the king.
- [ ] [2KI 13:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/13/#v16) L · word_choice · "당신의 손" when Elisha speaks to the king — A prophet speaks as Yahweh's messenger, not as a subject; Literal keeps the possessive with "당신", and Balanced and Readable leave it out, as Korean usually does.
- [ ] [2KI 13:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/13/#v17) B/R · register · "임금님께서는 ... 끝장내실 것입니다" when Elisha speaks of the king's future — In a promise about the king himself, Balanced and Readable name him "임금님" with the honorific, the natural courtesy of Korean; Literal keeps "당신".
- [ ] [2KI 14:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/14/#v8) L/B/R · register · The two kings speak to each other in the plain style (해라체) — A challenge between equals and rivals; the plain style carries the defiance, and Jehoash's mocking answer in verses 9-10 keeps it.
- [ ] [2KI 14:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/14/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "가시나무" for the thistle — The fable sets a lowly prickly plant against the great cedar; "가시나무" is the plainer word and pairs naturally with "백향목".
- [ ] [2KI 16:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/16/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "번제물", "곡식 제물", "부어 드리는 제물", "화목제물" — "번제" and "화목제" are the settled terms of the earlier notes; for the grain and drink offerings Korean uses descriptive words a reader understands rather than the Sino-Korean church terms.
- [ ] [2KI 17:26](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/17/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · "그 땅의 신" with no honorific for him ("그가 ... 보냈는데") — The settlers speak of a local god as foreigners understand him, as the English lowercase "god" shows; as with the Philistines in Samuel, Korean leaves out the honorific in their mouths.
- [ ] [2KI 17:36](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/17/#v36) L/B/R · register · In God's command God speaks of himself in the third person without the honorific ("이끌어 올린 주", "그에게") — The speaker is Yahweh himself (verse 35); Korean does not let a speaker honor himself, so the -시- and "그분" of narration are dropped inside the quotation.
- [ ] [2KI 18:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/18/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "다르단과 랍사리스와 랍사게" (L); "총사령관과 궁중 관리들의 우두머리와 랍사게" (B); R names "랍사게라고 불리는 고위 관리" and then says "랍사게" — The terms note keeps "랍사게" for the Rabshakeh in Korean, the form readers know; the two other titles are translated at Balanced and Readable, and Readable says once that 랍사게 is a title of a high official.
- [ ] [2KI 18:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/18/#v19) L/B/R · register · The Rabshakeh speaks in the plain style (해라체), to the officials and in the king's message to Hezekiah ("네가") — The envoy of the great king speaks down to a rebel vassal and his men; the plain style carries his contempt through verses 19-35.
- [ ] [2KI 18:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/18/#v22) L/B/R · register · The Rabshakeh speaks of Yahweh without the honorific ("그", "그의 산당들") — In the mouth of the Assyrian, who counts Yahweh one god among many (meaning decision m3), Korean drops the honorific the narrator uses; the speech shows his scorn.
- [ ] [2KI 18:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/18/#v25) L/B/R · register · "주께서 ... 말씀하셨다" with the honorific here — When the king claims Yahweh's own command, the honorific serves his claim to divine backing; the drop in verse 22 is where he scorns Hezekiah's trust.
- [ ] [2KI 18:26](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/18/#v26) L/B · register · "당신의 종들" in the officials' formal speech (합쇼체) to the Rabshakeh; R "우리" — The polite self-reference is kept, as the English keeps "your servants" (meaning decision m4); "당신" in formal speech is the address to a stranger of rank whose title the officials do not use.
- [ ] [2KI 18:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/18/#v31) L · idiom · "나와 복을 맺고" (L); "화친하고" (B); "평화 조약을 맺어라" (R) — Literal keeps the Hebrew's word "blessing" with the verb "맺다" used for covenants, so the sense of an agreement shows; Balanced and Readable say what it means.
- [ ] [2KI 19:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/19/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "아모스의 아들 예언자 이사야" — The terms note's form; Korean Protestant tradition spells Isaiah's father like the prophet Amos, though the Hebrew names differ.
- [ ] [2KI 19:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/19/#v3) L/B/R · register · The officials speak to Isaiah in the formal style (합쇼체) and give the king's words with honorifics ("말씀하십니다"); Hezekiah's own message is also 합쇼체 — The king humbles himself and asks the prophet to pray; Korean shows the king's respect for Isaiah in the speech level of his message.
- [ ] [2KI 19:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/19/#v4) L/B/R · register · "선생님의 하나님" for "your God" said to the prophet — The king's men address the prophet with respect; "선생님" is the courteous title for a teacher or man of God, where "당신" would sound cold.
- [ ] [2KI 19:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/19/#v10) L/B/R · register · "너의 신" with no honorific in Sennacherib's letter — The king of Assyria calls Israel's God a deceiver (meaning decision m1); Korean marks his scorn by "신" and the plain verb, as in the Rabshakeh's speech.
- [ ] [2KI 19:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/19/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "이스라엘의 거룩한 이" — God speaks of himself here, so the title takes no honorific; a narrator or a person praying would say "거룩하신 분".
- [ ] [2KI 19:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/19/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "잣나무" for the tree paired with the cedars — The tree is a tall conifer of Lebanon; "잣나무" is the familiar Korean name for such a tree in this pairing, though the species is not certain.
- [ ] [2KI 20:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/20/#v5) L/B/R · register · God names himself without the honorific ("하나님 주가 이와 같이 말한다") — Here God himself dictates the message Isaiah is to give, so the self-reference has no -시-; where Isaiah reports God's word (verse 1) the honorific stays.
- [ ] [2KI 20:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/20/#v8) L/B/R · register · Hezekiah speaks to Isaiah in 하오체 ("무엇이오?") — As David speaks to Nathan and Gad in the Samuel drafts: a king to a prophet he honors, neither down to a subject nor up to a superior.
- [ ] [2KI 21:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/21/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "점을 치다", "징조를 풀다/살피다", "혼백을 불러내는 자", "영매" — Modern words for the practices Deuteronomy forbids; "신접한 자" and "박수" are the older church terms.
- [ ] [2KI 21:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/21/#v12) L/B/R · register · "주가 이와 같이 말한다" (L), "이렇게 말한다" (B), "주가 하는 말이다" (R), without the honorific — The messenger formula stands inside God's own speech (verse 10), so God names himself without -시-, as in 20:5.
- [ ] [2KI 22:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/22/#v8) L/B/R · register · Hilkiah and Shaphan speak to each other in 하오체 — Two senior officials of rank speaking as equals; 하오체 keeps them courteous without either deferring to the other.
- [ ] [2KI 22:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/22/#v18) L/B/R · register · "주께 여쭈라고" with the honorific in Huldah's words about the king's request; "주가 ... 말한다" in God's own words — Huldah reports what the king did (asking God), where the honorific is natural; in the oracle God names himself, without -시-.
- [ ] [2KI 23:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/23/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "남창" (L), "신전 남창" (B), "신당에서 몸을 파는 남자들" (R); "집들" (L), "휘장" (B, R) — Korean names the role plainly at each level, as the English does; Literal keeps the Hebrew "houses" for what the women wove, and Balanced and Readable say coverings.
- [ ] [2KI 25:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ki/25/#v24) L/B/R · register · Gedaliah speaks to the army commanders in 하오체 with "여러분" — A governor appointed by Babylon addressing Judah's own officers: he has authority but must win their trust, so neither the plain style nor deference fits.

## 역대상 (1)

- [ ] [1CH 20:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1ch/20/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "동생" — Korean must say whether a brother is older or younger; Lahmi is taken as Goliath's younger brother, which the Hebrew does not say.

## 역대하 (26)

- [ ] [2CH 1:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/1/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "그분을" (L), "주님을" (B), "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}를" (R) for "him" — The pronoun points to the LORD (m3). Korean uses no deity_pronoun span; Balanced names him with "주님" so the reader does not take it as Bezalel or the altar, and Readable names him as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [2CH 2:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/2/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "잣나무" for cypress, "백단목" for algum — As in 1 Kings 5:22 and 10:11; algum is Chronicles' spelling of almug (m1). Both trees are uncertain.
- [ ] [2CH 2:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/2/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "후람아비" as one name — The word data reads one name (m1); Korean writes it as one word, like other compound names.
- [ ] [2CH 3:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/3/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "내실" (L, R), "지성소" (B) — The Hebrew reads "in the inner room" (m1), a puzzling place for chains on outside pillars; Korean keeps the reading, as the English does, with the Balanced word of the terms note.
- [ ] [2CH 5:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/5/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "심벌즈", "비파", "수금" — Korean names for the Levites' instruments, used for the same Hebrew words in the Psalms drafts.
- [ ] [2CH 10:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/10/#v6) L/B/R · register · Rehoboam speaks to his father's elders in 하오체 ("그대들", "-소"); "원로들" (L, B), "나이 든 신하들" (R) — As 1 Kings 12:6.
- [ ] [2CH 11:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/11/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "산당" (L, B), "산꼭대기 제단" (R); "숫염소 귀신"; "송아지" (L, B), "송아지 우상" (R) — Beings worshiped in the form of goats (m1); "숫염소 귀신" says both the animal and the demon.
- [ ] [2CH 12:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/12/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "리비아 사람", "숙 사람", "구스 사람" — Peoples serving in the Egyptian army (m1, m2); "리비아" is the modern name, "숙" follows the Hebrew name.
- [ ] [2CH 13:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/13/#v4) L/B/R · register · Abijah speaks to Jeroboam and his army in 해라체 with "너희" — A king's challenge shouted to an enemy army; Korean takes the plain style of command and rebuke.
- [ ] [2CH 13:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/13/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "미드라시" (L), "주석" (B, R) — The word names a written study or account (m1); Literal keeps the Hebrew word, as the English Literal does.
- [ ] [2CH 15:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/15/#v2) L/B/R · register · The prophet speaks in 합쇼체 to the king and people, with "여러분" for the plural "you" — Prophets speak to kings in 합쇼체 (terms note); the plural address (m4) takes the polite plural "여러분".
- [ ] [2CH 17:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/17/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "그의 조상 다윗" at every level — The Hebrew says "his father"; David is Jehoshaphat's ancestor, and Korean "조상" says so, as the Korean Kings does at Balanced and Readable.
- [ ] [2CH 17:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/17/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "많은 일" (L), "많은 물자" (B, R) — "Much work" may be stores or building works (m1); Balanced and Readable follow the English reading.
- [ ] [2CH 17:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/17/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "여호사바드" for Jehozabad — Kept apart from King Jehoshaphat (여호사밧), as in the Korean 2 Kings 12:22.
- [ ] [2CH 19:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/19/#v10) B/R · setting_alternative · {{gender:동족|형제}}들 (B, R): "동족" (inclusive), "형제" (traditional) — The English Balanced and Readable offer "fellow Israelites" and "brothers"; Korean "동족" covers men and women of one people, "형제" keeps the Hebrew word. Literal keeps "형제들", as the English Literal does.
- [ ] [2CH 20:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/20/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "마온 사람들" for the Meunites — The Korean form follows the English reading of the Greek (m1) and the town name Maon, from which the people are usually thought to take their name; the same form is used at 26:7.
- [ ] [2CH 20:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/20/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "믿어라 ... 굳게 설 것이다" (Literal); "굳게 믿어라 ... 굳게 설 것이다" (Balanced) — Korean cannot repeat one root as the Hebrew does (m1); Balanced repeats "굳게" so that trusting firmly and being held firm still echo each other. Isaiah 7:9 is not yet drafted in Korean and should follow this when it is.
- [ ] [2CH 21:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/21/#v12) L/B/R · register · Elijah's letter in 해라체 ("말씀하신다", "너"), with Yahweh's words inside single quotation marks — A written judgment from the prophet who spoke plainly to Ahab; the Kings drafts give Elijah plain speech toward the house of Ahab (uncertain). The quotation marks follow the Korean usage of Kings for an oracle inside a prophet's words.
- [ ] [2CH 23:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/23/#v3) L/B/R · register · Jehoiada speaks to the assembly in 하오체 ("보시오", "하오"), and the boy king takes the honorific (이분, -시-) — The 2 Kings 11 draft gives Jehoiada 하오체 toward the commanders; the same level is kept here for the larger assembly, and the king is spoken of with respect even before his crowning (m3).
- [ ] [2CH 24:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/24/#v5) L/B/R · register · The young king speaks to the priests and Levites in 해라체 here, but to Jehoiada in 하오체 (verse 6) — A king gives orders to his officials in plain style; Jehoiada, who raised him and is the chief priest, is addressed with the respect the Kings draft gives him.
- [ ] [2CH 25:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/25/#v6) B/R · setting_alternative · A units span "약 3.4톤" / "100달란트", followed by "에 이르는 은" — A talent is about 34 kg (terms note), so a hundred talents is given in tonnes, the unit a Korean reader uses for such a weight; "에" fits after both wordings, where 을/를 would not.
- [ ] [2CH 25:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/25/#v16) L/B/R · register · The angry king turns to 해라체 with "너" toward the prophet, who keeps 합쇼체 — The king's contempt is shown by the drop in speech level, as in the Kings scenes where a king rebuffs a prophet; "조언자 ... 조언하신 ... 조언" keeps the play on "counsel" at Literal (m3).
- [ ] [2CH 26:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/26/#v18) L/B/R · register · The priests speak to the king in 합쇼체 while they rebuke him, and call him by name — They oppose the king face to face, but the scene keeps the respect owed to a king, as prophets speak to kings in the Kings drafts; the Hebrew's bare "Uzziah" is kept as "웃시야여" at Literal and "웃시야 임금님" at Balanced and Readable.
- [ ] [2CH 28:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/28/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "디글랏빌네셀" at every level — The Korean terms note sets the Chronicles spelling 디글랏빌네셀 for these books; the meaning decision (m1) says that he is the king called 디글랏빌레셀 in Kings.
- [ ] [2CH 32:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/32/#v11) L/B/R · register · "우리 하나님 주가 ... 건져 낼 것이다", Hezekiah's words quoted by the Assyrian without the honorific — The words are Hezekiah's, but they are put in the enemy's mouth, and the Kings drafts give Yahweh no honorific when the Assyrians speak (2 Kings 18:35).
- [ ] [2CH 35:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2ch/35/#v21) L/B/R · register · Neco speaks to Josiah in 하오체 with "당신", and speaks of God with the honorific — The Kings drafts give kings speaking to each other and foreign royal messages 하오체; Neco's warning is firm but not contemptuous. The honorific for God follows the narrator's view that the words came from God (verse 22).

## 에스더 (3)

- [ ] [EST 1:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/est/1/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "세달" for Shethar, the same spelling as Zethar in verse 10 — Korean has no z sound, and the traditional Protestant spellings write both names "세달"; the two lists keep them apart.
- [ ] [EST 2:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/est/2/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "나무" (L); "교수대" (B); "장대" (R) — As the English levels: "교수대" is the Esther word for Haman's gallows (5:14; 7:9-10), but the Hebrew word is wood, a tree or a pole, and the death may be impaling or the hanging up of a body (meaning decision m1).
- [ ] [EST 5:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/est/5/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · 교수대 — The Hebrew says 'tree' or 'wood'; as meaning decision m1 says, the execution may have been impaling or hanging up a body. 교수대 keeps the picture of a tall structure for execution, as the English 'gallows' does.

## 욥기 (4)

- [ ] [JOB 16:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/job/16/#v19) L/B/R · register · honorific "계시다" and "분" for the witness in heaven — The witness is in heaven, so Korean speaks of him with respect; the honorific suits God or a heavenly being alike and so keeps the question open, as the meaning decision does.
- [ ] [JOB 19:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/job/19/#v25) L/B/R · register · "살아 계신다", "서실", with the honorific and no deity_pronoun span — Korean has no deity_pronoun spans; the honorific -시- carries the reading that the Redeemer is God (meaning decision m2), as the English span does. If the other view were taken, the honorific would still suit a heavenly defender.
- [ ] [JOB 33:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/job/33/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "그분이 ... 말씀하신다" with honorifics — Korean must choose honorifics where the Hebrew leaves the speaker open (m1). God is the most likely speaker, and 그분 with -시- can also be said of a heavenly messenger, so no name is added.
- [ ] [JOB 42:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/job/42/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "[제 말을] 물리고 ... 회개합니다" — Follows m1 and m2: Job takes back his words and repents; 물리다 and 취소하다 say "take back" in plain Korean.

## 시편 (170)

- [ ] [PSA 2:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/2/#v12) L/B/R · register · "그분" with the honorific for "he" and "him" in this verse — The Hebrew leaves open whether he is the son or the LORD (meaning decision m3). Korean must choose a level of respect; the honorific fits either reading, so the ambiguity is kept, as the English keeps it.
- [ ] [PSA 3:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/3/#v2) L/B/R · register · Speaking to God the psalmist uses 합쇼체 and calls himself "저/제"; God is called "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}여" and addressed as "주님" — Terms note and style guide section 7a, as the published Psalms 23 and 139. Korean addresses a superior by title, not by a pronoun; the vocative "여" stands outside the span and fits both forms. Where a psalm speaks about God rather than to him, the plain written style with "나" returns (verse 5). …
- [ ] [PSA 3:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/3/#v5) L/B/R · register · Verses 5-7 speak about the LORD in the plain written style with the honorific, and the psalmist is "나" — The Hebrew turns from "you" to "he" here; Korean marks the turn as the published Psalm 23 does, and returns to 합쇼체 with "저" when verse 8 speaks to God again. A reviewer may prefer one level for the whole psalm.
- [ ] [PSA 5:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/5/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "피리에 맞추어" (for flutes) — The English takes the word as flutes; it may instead name a tune (meaning decision m3). Korean follows the English reading with the plain word for a wind instrument.
- [ ] [PSA 6:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/6/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "스미닛" kept as a name (L, B); "여덟째" (the eighth) at Readable — The Hebrew means "the eighth" but its musical sense is lost (meaning decision m3). Korean keeps the word as a name at Literal and Balanced, as the English keeps "sheminith", and translates it at Readable as the English does, without guessing more.
- [ ] [PSA 7:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/7/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "식가욘" kept as a name (L, B); "노래" at Readable; Cush is "구스" — The kind of song meant is unknown (meaning decision m2); Korean keeps the word as a name, as the English keeps "shiggaion", and says only "song" at Readable, as the English does.
- [ ] [PSA 7:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/7/#v11) L/B/R · register · Verses 11-18 speak about God in the plain written style with the honorific, and the psalmist is "나" — From here the psalm speaks of God in the third person ("God", "he"); Korean marks the change as in Psalms 3 and 23. "정직한 사람" is the terms note's word for the upright.
- [ ] [PSA 8:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/8/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "깃딧" kept as a name at every level — The word may name an instrument or a tune, perhaps from Gath (meaning decision m3); its sense is lost, so Korean keeps it as a name, as the English does.
- [ ] [PSA 9:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/9/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "뭇랍벤" kept as a name; Readable adds "곡조" (tune) as the English says "tune" — The words mean "death of the son", probably the name of a tune (meaning decision m3); Korean keeps the name, as the English does.
- [ ] [PSA 9:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/9/#v8) L/B/R · register · Where the psalm speaks about the LORD (verses 8-10, 12-13, 16-19) Korean uses the plain written style with the honorific; where it speaks to him (verses 2-7, … — The Hebrew moves between "he" and "you"; Korean follows the turns as in the published Psalm 23 (style guide section 7a). Verse 12 calls on the hearers to sing and is in 해라체. A reviewer may prefer one level for the whole psalm.
- [ ] [PSA 9:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/9/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "힉가욘" kept as a word at every level, before "셀라" — Like Selah, it is a musical direction whose exact sense is unknown (meaning decision m2); Korean keeps it as a sound, as the English keeps "Higgaion".
- [ ] [PSA 14:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/14/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "어리석은 자" (L, B), "어리석은 사람" (R) for the fool (nabal) — The fool here is one who lives without regard for God (meaning decision m2), a moral more than a mental failing. "어리석은" is the common Korean word for such folly; "미련한 자" stays for the Proverbs word kesil, so the two Hebrew words are not merged.
- [ ] [PSA 16:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/16/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "믹담" kept as a name (L, B); "노래" at Readable — The kind of psalm meant is unknown (meaning decision m2); Korean keeps the word as a name, as the English keeps "miktam", and says only "song" at Readable.
- [ ] [PSA 16:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/16/#v2) L/B/R · register · The psalmist speaks to his own soul in 해라체 ("너는"), and the words he has said to the LORD are quoted in 합쇼체 — The verse is the psalmist speaking to himself (meaning decision m1). Verses 3-4 and 7-9 then speak about God and others in the plain style with "나"; verses 1, 5-6 and 10-11 speak to God in 합쇼체 with "저", as the Hebrew turns to "you".
- [ ] [PSA 18:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/18/#v2) L/B/R · register · The song follows the Hebrew's turns: 합쇼체 with "저" where it speaks to God (verses 2, 16, 26-30, 36-46, 49-50), the plain written style with "나" where it speaks … — The song moves between "you" and "he" (meaning decision on verse 29). Korean marks each turn as in Psalms 3 and 23; the narrative title is in the plain written style. The whole song is one quotation, closed at the end of verse 51. A reviewer may prefer one level throughout.
- [ ] [PSA 18:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/18/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "그룹" for the cherub at every level — "그룹" is the established Protestant form of the name for these winged beings who serve God (meaning decision m1); "천사" (angel) would name a different kind of being. A reader may confuse it with the common word "그룹" (group), which a reviewer may weigh against "케루빔".
- [ ] [PSA 18:49](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/18/#v49) L/B/R · register · The verse changes from "나" (plain style) to "저" (합쇼체) where the Hebrew changes from "he" to "you" — The Hebrew turns to address God in the middle of the verse (meaning decision m2); Korean marks the turn in the same place, as the English does with "You".
- [ ] [PSA 20:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/20/#v2) L/B/R · register · The "you" of verses 2-6 is the king (meaning decision m1), addressed as "왕" with 합쇼체 and the honorific — The people bless their king before battle; Korean speaks to a king by his title and with respect, not with a pronoun. The people call themselves "우리" here, since they speak to the king, and "저희" when they turn to God in verse 10. Psalm 21:9-13 follows the same choice.
- [ ] [PSA 21:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/21/#v9) L/B/R · register · Verses 9-13 speak to the king (meaning decision m1) as "왕", with 합쇼체 and the honorific; verses 2-8 speak to God about the king, who then takes no honorific — As in Psalm 20: a king is addressed by title. When the people speak to God about the king (verses 2-8), Korean drops the king's honorific before the greater one addressed.

The psalm keeps the same words, so a reader finds the quotation unchanged; the rest of the prayer uses the speech level of prayer (style …
- [ ] [PSA 22:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/22/#v9) L/B/R · register · The mockers speak in 해라체, of the sufferer as "그" and of the LORD with the honorific, in scorn (B, R "-겠지", "-라지") — Here the mockers invoke the LORD's name against the sufferer (meaning decision m1); their scorn is for him, not a denial of God, so Korean keeps the honorific and carries the sneer in the endings.
- [ ] [PSA 22:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/22/#v24) L/B/R · register · Verses 24-25, 27 and 29-32 speak to the gathered people and about God in 해라체 and the plain style; verses 26 and 28, which still say "you" to God, are in 합쇼체 — After the prayer is answered (verse 22) the psalmist calls the assembly to praise; Korean follows the Hebrew's turns between "you" and "he" (meaning decision on verse 26).

- [ ] [PSA 23:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/23/#v4) L/B/R · register · When the psalm turns to speak to God (verses 4-5), the singer uses 합쇼체 and calls himself "저"; God is addressed by title, "주님" (주님께서, 주님의) — Style guide section 7a (speech levels in prayer). Korean addresses a superior by title, not by a pronoun; "당신" would be cold or rude. The change of level marks the turn to God that the Hebrew makes (meaning decision m2); verse 6 returns to speaking about the LORD. A reviewer may prefer one level …
- [ ] [PSA 23:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/23/#v6) L/B/R · register · Back to the plain written style with "나" — The verse speaks of "the house of the LORD" in the third person, so it is speech about God again (style guide section 7a), as in verses 1-3.
- [ ] [PSA 24:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/24/#v4) L · word_choice · "내 영혼" (my soul) kept at Literal, where "my" is the LORD's — In the Hebrew text as read the LORD speaks of himself here (meaning decision m2); Literal keeps the word as it stands, as the English does with "My", and Balanced and Readable say whose life is meant. Korean cannot mark the pronoun as God's with a capital, so a reader of Literal needs this note.
- [ ] [PSA 25:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/25/#v8) L/B/R · register · Verses 8-10 and 12-15 speak about the LORD in the plain written style with "나"; verses 1-7, 11 and 16-22 speak to him in 합쇼체 with "저" — Here the prayer turns to speak about God (meaning decision m2); Korean follows the turns as in the other psalms of this block.

- [ ] [PSA 27:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/27/#v1) L/B/R · register · Verses 1-6 and 13 speak about the LORD in the plain written style with the honorific, and the singer is "나"; verses 7-12 speak to him in 합쇼체 with "저"; verse 14 … — Style guide section 7a, as the published Psalm 23 does when it turns to God and back. The change of level marks the turn to prayer that the Hebrew makes at verse 7 (meaning decision m1 there) and the turn to the hearer in verse 14.
- [ ] [PSA 28:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/28/#v1) L/B/R · register · Verses 1-4 and 9 are spoken to God in 합쇼체 with "저"; verses 5-8 speak about the LORD in the plain written style with "나" — Style guide section 7a. Verse 5 begins to speak of the LORD in the third person and verses 6-8 praise him before the people, so they take the plain written style with the honorific; verse 9 turns back to prayer.
- [ ] [PSA 29:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/29/#v1) L/B/R · register · The call to the heavenly beings is in 해라체 ("돌려드려라", "인정하여라") with the vocative "-들아"; the psalm speaks about the LORD with the honorific throughout — The singer summons the beings around God's throne (meaning decision m2) as a herald calls an assembly; 해라체 is the plain voice of Korean poetry for such a summons, and the honorific belongs to the LORD, not to those called.
- [ ] [PSA 30:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/30/#v2) L/B/R · register · The psalm is spoken to God in 합쇼체 with "저"; verses 5-6 turn to the faithful in 해라체 ("찬송하여라") and speak of the LORD with the honorific — Style guide section 7a. Verse 5 calls on God's faithful ones, so it speaks to them, not to God; the prayer to God resumes in verse 7. The prayer quoted in verses 10-11 is 합쇼체 like the rest.
