The psalm moves between brooding about God and speaking to him; the Korean speech level follows each turn the Hebrew makes.
- [ ] [PSA 77:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/77/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "민족들" for ammim here, where the English Balanced and Readable say "nations" — The terms note gives "백성들" for the peoples, but in Korean "백성들" here would sound like God's own people; the verse means the other nations (meaning decision), so "민족들" is used at every level.
- [ ] [PSA 78:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/78/#v1) L/B/R · register · The teacher speaks to "my people" in 해라체 (들어라, 너희) and tells the story of God in the plain written style with the honorific — A teacher calling his people to listen (meaning decision) speaks as the father does in Proverbs, in the plain style; God is spoken of with 께서 and -시- throughout the history. Readable explains "maskil" as "가르치려고 지은 노래", as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 78:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/78/#v19) L/B/R · register · The people speak of God without the honorific in their complaint ("하나님이 ... 있겠느냐?") — Their words doubt and test God (meaning decision); Korean shows the defiance by dropping the honorific the narrative uses.
- [ ] [PSA 79:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/79/#v1) L/B/R · register · A prayer of the people in 합쇼체; God is "하나님" or "주님", the people "저희" — As Psalm 74 (style guide section 7a): the community prays to God with the humble "저희".
- [ ] [PSA 80:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/80/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "소산님 에둣" kept as a name (L, B); "\"언약의 백합화\" 곡조" at Readable — As Psalm 69:1: the Hebrew words stay names where the English keeps them, and Readable gives their likely sense as a tune ("on lilies, a testimony", meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 80:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/80/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "그룹들" (L, B), "그룹 천사들" (R) for the cherubim — The established Protestant word; Readable adds "천사" so that the word is not read as the everyday "그룹" (group).
- [ ] [PSA 81:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/81/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "깃딧" kept as a name at every level — Its meaning is lost: an instrument or a tune, perhaps from Gath (meaning decision). The English keeps the word at every level, and so does Korean, in its established Protestant form.
- [ ] [PSA 81:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/81/#v2) L/B/R · register · The singer calls the festival crowd in 해라체 (verses 2-6); God's own words (verses 7-17) are in 해라체 with "나", addressing Israel as "너" — Style guide section 7a and the terms note: God speaking uses 해라체 with 나. A call to a gathered people to sing is in the plain imperative, as the terms note renders "Praise Yah" with "찬양하여라".
- [ ] [PSA 81:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/81/#v16) L/B/R · register · God speaks of himself by name in the third person, with the honorific ("그분 앞에") — The Hebrew has God speak of the LORD as "him" here (meaning decision); Korean keeps the third person, and the honorific is natural for the LORD named.
- [ ] [PSA 82:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/82/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "신들" for elohim as the members of the council, with no honorific; "하나님" only for God — The meaning decision reads the gods as the members of God's heavenly council. Korean has two words where the Hebrew has one, "하나님" for God and "신들" for the others, so the difference the context makes is clear; God speaks to them in 해라체 (verses 2-7).
- [ ] [PSA 83:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/83/#v2) L/B/R · register · A prayer to God in 합쇼체 from verse 2 to the end; the enemies' own words (verses 5, 13) are in the plain style — Style guide section 7a, as the other Asaph psalms of this block.
- [ ] [PSA 83:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/83/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "앗시리아" for Assyria — The modern name, as "이집트" over "애굽"; "앗수르" is the older church form.
- [ ] [PSA 83:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/83/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "그 이름이 {{divine_name:주|야훼}}이신 주님" with the name inside a span — Where the verse is about the name itself, the default "주" makes "your name is the LORD" read as a title, as the English "the LORD" does; the setting gives the name "야훼". The copula "이신" fits both forms.
- [ ] [PSA 84:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/84/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "깃딧" kept as a name; "고라 자손" (L, B), "고라 집안 사람들" (R) — The meaning of gittith is not known (meaning decision m3), so Korean keeps it as a name, as the English does, in the form Korean Bible readers know. Readable says "family" for the sons of Korah, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 84:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/84/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "내 영혼" (L), "내 온 존재" (B), the plain subject (R) for nefesh; the singer says "내" here, between lines addressed to God — "영혼" is the term note's word at Literal; Balanced and Readable say the whole self, as the English does (meaning decision m1). The verse speaks about the LORD in the third person, so the singer's "내" belongs to the plain voice; Korean poetry allows the first-person 나 in a line not spoken to God.
- [ ] [PSA 85:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/85/#v9) L/B/R · register · Verses 9-14 speak about God in the plain written style with the honorific, and the singer is "나" — From verse 9 the psalmist no longer speaks to God but tells what God will say and do (meaning decision m1), so Korean turns to the voice the style guide (section 7a) gives to speech about God. "평화" (peace, well-being) is used for shalom in this psalm, of a land made whole.
- [ ] [PSA 86:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/86/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "진리" for God's truth here — To walk in God's truth is to live by what he has shown to be true and reliable; Korean "진리" says the truth one lives by, while "진실" (the terms note's word for emet in general) stands where truthfulness or faithfulness is the sense, as in verse 15.
- [ ] [PSA 88:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/88/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "마할랏 르안놋" and "마스길" kept as names (L, B); explained at Readable as "고난의 때에 부르는 곡조" and "가르치는 노래" — These words are probably a tune and a kind of song whose exact meaning is lost (meaning decisions m4, m5). Literal and Balanced keep them as the terms note does; Readable follows the English Readable's explanation, which is uncertain.
- [ ] [PSA 89:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/89/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "마스길" (L, B), "가르치는 노래" (R); "에스라 사람 에단" — As in 88:1: the meaning of maskil is uncertain (meaning decision m2), so Literal and Balanced keep the name and Readable follows the English Readable's explanation.
- [ ] [PSA 89:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/89/#v27) L/B/R · register · The king's prayer inside God's words speaks to God in 합쇼체 and calls him "주님", but calls himself "나" — God reports what the king will say, so the prayer keeps the polite style a person uses to God; the king's "나" is kept because the words are quoted inside God's own speech and "저" would read as the speaker of the psalm.
- [ ] [PSA 90:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/90/#v4) L/B · word_choice · "경점" for a watch of the night (L, B); "밤의 몇 시간" (R) — A watch was one of the parts into which the night was divided for guards (meaning decision m1); "경점" is the old Korean word for such a division of the night. Readable says "a few hours of the night", as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [PSA 91:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/91/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "가장 높으신 분" (L, B; "모든 것 위에 계신 하나님" at R) for Elyon; "전능하신 분" for Shaddai — Plain modern words for the two titles (meaning decision m2), with the honorific; "지존자" and "전능자" are the older church forms. Elyon is "지극히 높으신 분" where it stands as a name in a statement (87:5), and "가장 높으신 분" here, with the same sense.
- [ ] [PSA 91:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/91/#v2) L · word_choice · "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}께 아뢴다" (L), "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}를 두고 말한다" (B, R) — The Hebrew "say to the LORD" can mean saying of him or to him (meaning decision m1); Literal keeps the preposition's own sense, while Balanced and Readable say "about" as the English does. The words quoted speak of God in the third person either way.
- [ ] [PSA 91:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/91/#v9) L/B/R · register · The first line speaks to God in 합쇼체; the second returns to "너" in 해라체; the speaker here says "나" — The verse changes speaker and addressee suddenly (meaning decision m1), and Korean marks the change with the speech level of each line. The speaker keeps "나" because the line is a cry inside a poem spoken to a fellow believer, not a prayer of the whole psalm.
- [ ] [PSA 92:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/92/#v13) L/B/R · register · Verses 13-16 speak about the righteous and about the LORD in the plain written style — The prayer to God ends with verse 12; the closing lines speak of the righteous and of God in the third person, and Korean uses the voice the style guide (section 7a) gives to speech about God. Korean "의인" and "그들" need no gender span.
- [ ] [PSA 93:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/93/#v4) L/B/R · register · The LORD is spoken of in the third person with the honorific inside the prayer of verses 2-5, in 합쇼체 — The verse names the LORD in the third person but stands between lines addressed to him, so Korean keeps the polite style of the surrounding prayer.
- [ ] [PSA 94:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/94/#v7) L/B/R · register · The wicked speak of God with the honorific (-시-) in the plain style — In Korean the honorific is part of naming God, and the wicked here deny that God sees, not that he is God; Korean keeps the usual way of speaking of God and lets their words show their contempt. Yah is {{divine_name:주|야}}, as in 89:9.
- [ ] [PSA 94:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/94/#v18) L/B/R · register · Verses 18-20 speak to God in 합쇼체, and the singer is "저"; verses 16-17 and 21-23 speak about God with "나" — The psalm moves between speaking about the LORD and speaking to him, as Psalm 23 does, and Korean follows each turn with its speech level (style guide section 7a).
- [ ] [PSA 95:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/95/#v1) L/B/R · register · The call to worship (verses 1-7) is in 해라체 with the hortative "-자" (let us) and the command "오너라" (come) — The singer calls the congregation to praise and speaks of God in the third person with the honorific; the plain written style of Korean poetry has the hortative "-자" for "let us". God's own words in verses 8-11 are also in 해라체.
- [ ] [PSA 98:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/98/#v5) R · word_choice · "수금" (lyre) at every level — The English Readable says "harp" for a common word; "수금" is the word Korean readers know for this small stringed instrument (meaning decision m1) and is plain enough for Readable.
- [ ] [PSA 100:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/100/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "감사의 시" (L), "감사드리는 시" (B), "감사드리는 노래" (R) — The title may name a song of thanks or one sung with a thank offering (meaning decision m2); Korean says thanks and leaves the offering unnamed, as the English does.
- [ ] [PSA 101:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/101/#v1) L/B/R · register · The king's whole vow (verses 1-8) is spoken to God in 합쇼체 with "저/제" — The psalm opens by addressing the LORD and asks "When will you come to me?" (verse 2); the promises that follow are made before him (meaning decision m2), so Korean keeps the polite style of prayer throughout. The English sets this psalm as prose, and so does the Korean.
- [ ] [PSA 102:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/102/#v17) L/B/R · register · Verses 17-24 speak of the LORD in the third person but stay in 합쇼체, the speech level of the whole prayer — The psalm is one long prayer: it turns to the third person here and back to "you" in verse 16 and verse 25. Korean keeps the polite style of prayer through the turn, so the psalm does not change its relationship to God from verse to verse.
- [ ] [PSA 104:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/104/#v1) L/B/R · register · Verses that speak to God (1-2, 6-13, 20-30) are in 합쇼체 with 주님; verses that speak about him in the third person (3-5, 14-19, 31-35) are in the plain written … — The Hebrew hymn moves between "you" and "he" for God, and Korean follows each verse with the speech level the style guide (section 7a) gives to speaking to God or about him, as the published Psalm 23 does. The call to the soul (verses 1 and 35) is in 해라체, as in 103:1. The English sets this psalm as …
- [ ] [PSA 104:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/104/#v13) L/B/R · register · The verse keeps the Hebrew's change from "his" to "your" and is in 합쇼체, the speech level of its second half — A Korean sentence takes one speech level. The verse ends speaking to God ("your works"), so the whole of it is in the polite style of prayer, while the first half still names God in the third person, as the Hebrew does.
- [ ] [PSA 105:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/105/#v31) L/B/R · word_choice · "각다귀" (gnats) at Literal and Balanced; "모기 같은 작은 벌레들" at Readable — The insect (kinnim) is small and biting, but its kind is not known (meaning decision). "이" (lice) names one kind; "각다귀" is a gnat-like fly, and Readable describes it in common words.
- [ ] [PSA 106:33](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/106/#v33) L · word_choice · "그분의 영" (God's Spirit) and "그" (Moses) at Literal — Korean marks God with the honorific "그분" and Moses with plain "그", so Literal shows the reading taken in the meaning decision (God's Spirit) as the English capital does; the footnoted alternative, Moses' spirit, would be "그의 영".
- [ ] [PSA 108:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/108/#v2) L · word_choice · "제 영광" (my glory) kept at Literal; "온 마음을 다해" (B), "온 힘을 다해" (R) — Literal keeps the Hebrew word; Balanced and Readable give its sense here, the whole inner self singing (meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 108:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/108/#v3) L/B/R · register · Plain style with "나" when the singer calls to his instruments — The singer turns from God to call on the harp and lyre, and Korean speaks to them as one speaks to things, calling oneself "나"; the prayer to God resumes in 합쇼체 in verse 4. "하프" and "수금" name two stringed instruments (meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 108:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/108/#v11) L/B/R · register · The king's questions are in the plain style with "나"; verses 12-13 turn to God in 합쇼체 with "저희" — The questions are asked aloud, not yet to God; the answer, addressed to God, comes in verse 12 ("is it not you, God?"), and Korean marks that turn by the speech level.
- [ ] [PSA 108:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/108/#v14) L/B/R · register · The last verse speaks about God in the plain style, with "우리" (we) — The Hebrew turns from "you" to "he", a word of confidence spoken about God; Korean follows the turn with the plain style and "우리", as style guide section 7a sets for speech about God.
- [ ] [PSA 109:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/109/#v1) L/B/R · register · The psalm is prayer in 합쇼체 with "저/제"; the wishes against the accuser (verses 6-20) are requests to God, "-게 하십시오" (let it be) — The whole psalm is addressed to God (verses 1, 21, 26), so the wishes of verses 6-20 are put to him as requests, which keeps one speech level and shows, as the meaning decision says, that the psalmist hands the case to God rather than cursing for himself.
- [ ] [PSA 109:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/109/#v21) L/B/R · setting_alternative · YHWH with Adonai is "{{divine_name:하나님|야훼}} 나의 주님" at Literal, in the Hebrew order, and "주 {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}" at Balanced and Readable — Where the name stands beside Adonai, the English sets it as "GOD", and Korean gives it the default "하나님" (alternative "야훼"), as the Korean Psalter does for "Lord GOD", so that "주" is not said twice; Adonai is "주님" / "주". Literal keeps the Hebrew order, name first (meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 110:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/110/#v1) L/B/R · register · The king is addressed as "너" in 해라체 through the psalm (verses 1-3, 5), as God addresses him in verses 1 and 4 — The psalm is a prophetic word: what the speaker says to the king in verses 2-3 and 5 carries on the LORD's own charge ("rule!", meaning decision). One form of address keeps the oracle whole; a reviewer may prefer the honorific where the speaker, rather than God, is heard.
- [ ] [PSA 110:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/110/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "그" (plain) for the one who drinks, the king — Korean must choose between the honorific "그분" (the Lord) and plain "그" (the king), where English can leave "he" open. The meaning decision takes the king as the one who drinks; the Lord of verses 5-6 keeps "그분".
- [ ] [PSA 115:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/115/#v1) L/B/R · register · Verses 1-2 are prayer to God in 합쇼체 with "저희"; from verse 3 the psalm speaks about God in the plain style, with "우리" (verses 3, 12, 18), and to the worshippers … — The Hebrew turns from "you" (the LORD, verse 1) to "our God ... he" (verse 3), and to calls and blessings spoken to the people. Korean marks each voice by its speech level, using the humble "저희" only where the people speak to God.
- [ ] [PSA 115:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/115/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · The response keeps "그들의" (their) after the call to "you", in verses 9-11 alike — The Hebrew changes from "trust" (to you) to "their help", perhaps a reply sung by another voice (meaning decision); Korean keeps the change rather than smoothing it.
- [ ] [PSA 115:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/115/#v14) L/B/R · register · The blessing is spoken to the worshippers as "너희", with the wish "-하시기를 빈다" — A blessing spoken by priests over the people (meaning decision on verse 15) in the plain style of the psalm; "너희" is the plural of address used in calls to the congregation through this block.
- [ ] [PSA 116:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/116/#v1) L/B/R · register · The singer speaks about the LORD in the plain style with "나" (verses 1-7, 9-15, 18-19) and to him in 합쇼체 with "저" (verses 4b, 8, 16-17) — The psalm moves between telling the people what the LORD did and speaking to him, and the Hebrew marks the turns by "he" and "you"; Korean marks them by speech level (style guide section 7a), as the published Psalm 23 does.
- [ ] [PSA 116:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/116/#v6) L · word_choice · "어리석은 이들" (the simple) at Literal; "자기를 지킬 힘이 없는 사람들" (B), "스스로를 지키지 못하는 사람들" (R) — Literal keeps the word Korean Proverbs uses for peti at Literal (어리석은 자); here it means the open and unprotected (meaning decision), which Balanced and Readable say.
- [ ] [PSA 118:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/118/#v1) L/B/R · register · The refrain in its one Korean form (as 106:1, 107:1), and the voices of the liturgy by speech level: plain commands to the people (1-4, 19, 29), the singer's … — The psalm is a liturgy with several voices (meaning decision); the Hebrew marks who is spoken to by "you" and "he", and Korean must mark it by speech level (style guide section 7a). In verse 25 the people pray together with "저희".
- [ ] [PSA 118:26](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/118/#v26) L/B/R · register · The one who comes is spoken of with the honorific ("오시는 이", "오시는 분"); the gathered worshippers are "너희" — The one who comes in the LORD's name is probably the king at the head of the procession (meaning decision), and the Korean of Matthew 21:9 has "오시는 분"; the plural "you" is the whole procession, blessed by the priests.
- [ ] [PSA 119:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/119/#v4) L/B/R · register · From here the psalm speaks to God in 합쇼체; the psalmist is "저/제"; God is addressed by title, "주님" (주님께서, 주님의), and called by name with "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}여" — Style guide section 7a (speech levels in prayer) and Psalm 139. Korean addresses a superior by title, not by a pronoun, so every "you" and "your" for God is "주님". Verses 1-3 speak about God and keep the plain written style.
- [ ] [PSA 120:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/120/#v2) L/B/R · register · Speaking to God the singer turns to 합쇼체 and calls himself "저/제"; God is called "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}여" and, where a "you" is needed, addressed by the title "주님" — Style guide section 7a (speech levels in prayer), as the published Psalms 23 and 139. Korean addresses a superior by title, not by a pronoun. The vocative "여" stands outside the span and fits both forms.
- [ ] [PSA 121:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/121/#v3) L/B/R · register · The one addressed is "너", in the plain style (해라체); the wish is "-기를" — From verse 3 a voice speaks to one person (meaning decision m3), a blessing to a fellow pilgrim or the singer's own heart; 해라체 with 너 is the plain voice of Korean poetry and of a blessing spoken to an equal. "그대" is a possible literary alternative.
- [ ] [PSA 122:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/122/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "다윗의 [노래]" (L), "다윗의 노래" (B, R) for "Of David" in the titles of Psalms 122-145 — The Hebrew says only "of David", which may mean by, for or about him (meaning decision); "다윗의 노래" keeps that openness at every level. "다윗의 시" stays for titles that say "a psalm" (mizmor).
- [ ] [PSA 122:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/122/#v6) L · word_choice · "평화를 구하여라 ... 평안하기를" — The Hebrew plays on the sound of "peace" and "Jerusalem" (meaning decision). Korean cannot copy the play; at Literal "평화" and "평안" at least share their first syllable, a faint echo of it.
- [ ] [PSA 123:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/123/#v2) L/B/R · register · Verse 2 speaks about the LORD in the plain written style with the honorific, between the prayers of verses 1 and 3 — The verse names "the LORD our God" and "he" in the third person, so it is speech about God (style guide section 7a); verses 1, 3 and 4 speak to him.
- [ ] [PSA 127:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/127/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "솔로몬의 [노래]" (L), "솔로몬의 노래" (B, R) — As "다윗의 노래" in this block: the Hebrew "of Solomon" may name the author or the one the song concerns (meaning decision), and "의" keeps that open.
- [ ] [PSA 130:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/130/#v5) L/B/R · register · Verses 5-8 speak about the LORD in the plain written style, the singer as "나" — Verses 1-4 speak to God; here the LORD is named in the third person ("his word"), so the voice is speech about him (style guide section 7a), and verses 7-8 turn to Israel.
- [ ] [PSA 133:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/133/#v1) L/B/R · setting_alternative · {{gender:형제자매|형제}}, with "가" outside the span — The word takes in the whole family and people, women and men (meaning decision), so Korean offers the same two wordings as the English; both end in a vowel, so one particle fits.
- [ ] [PSA 134:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/134/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "너" (singular) for the one blessed — The Hebrew "you" here is singular (meaning decision); Korean keeps the singular, as a blessing given to each worshiper in turn.
- [ ] [PSA 135:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/135/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "이집트" for Egypt — "이집트" is the name of the country in modern Korean; "애굽" is the older church form, which the style guide's preference for the modern word sets aside; a native reviewer may prefer the traditional Protestant form.
- [ ] [PSA 136:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/136/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · The refrain is "참으로 그분의 한결같은 사랑은 영원하다" (L), "그분의 한결같은 사랑이 영원하다" (B), "그분의 변함없는 사랑은 영원하다" (R) in all 26 verses — As the terms note, so that the refrain reads alike in Psalms 106, 107, 118 and 136. Each verse ends with the refrain as a sentence of its own; Literal gives the Hebrew "for" (ki) as "참으로" (indeed), since a Korean clause of reason would have to end the line with "-기 때문이다" twenty-six times.
- [ ] [PSA 137:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/137/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "바빌론" for Babylon (verses 1, 8) — "바빌론" is the modern Korean form; "바벨론" is the older church spelling. As with "이집트" (135:8), the modern word is preferred; a native reviewer may restore the traditional form.
- [ ] [PSA 137:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/137/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "포플러 나무" for the trees, "수금" for the lyre — The trees are probably poplars that grow by water (meaning decision), as the English says; "버드나무" (willow) is the familiar older rendering. "수금" is the established Korean word for the biblical lyre.
- [ ] [PSA 138:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/138/#v5) L/B/R · register · 합쇼체 is kept in verses 5-6 and 8 although the LORD is named in the third person — The verses stand inside a prayer that speaks to God before and after them (verses 4 and 7); changing style for a line would break the prayer, so Korean keeps the polite style and simply names the LORD.
- [ ] [PSA 139:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/139/#v1) L/B/R · register · The whole psalm is spoken to God in 합쇼체; the singer is "저/제"; God is addressed by title, "주님" (주님께서, 주님의), and called by name with "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}여" — Style guide section 7a (speech levels in prayer). Korean addresses a superior by title, not by a pronoun. The vocative particle "여" stands outside the divine_name span and fits both forms (주여, 야훼여). There are no deity_pronoun spans in Korean (no capitals); respect is carried by 주님, 께서 and -시-.
- [ ] [PSA 139:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/139/#v16) L · supplied_words · "그것을 위해 그 가운데 하나하나가 [있었습니다]" — Literal follows the form to be read, as the English does (meaning decision m3); "하나하나" (each one) gives the distributive sense the Korean needs, and the verb is supplied in brackets.
- [ ] [PSA 140:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/140/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "지휘자를 위하여" (L), "음악 지휘자를 위하여" (B), "찬양 인도자를 위해" (R); "다윗의 시" (L, B), "다윗이 지은 노래" (R), as the published Psalm 139:1 — The terms note follows the published Psalm 139:1 for the director line and the style guide for "a psalm of David"; the older "영장" is not current Korean.
- [ ] [PSA 140:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/140/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "셀라" kept at the end of the verse at every level (verses 4, 6, 9) — The word's meaning is no longer known; it is probably a musical direction (meaning decision). The terms note keeps it as a name, "셀라", where the English has it.
- [ ] [PSA 142:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/142/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "마스길" (L, B); Readable "가르치려고 지은 노래" (a song to teach) — The meaning of maskil is uncertain (meaning decision); the terms note keeps it as a name, and Readable gives the sense the English Readable gives.
- [ ] [PSA 142:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/142/#v2) L/B/R · register · Verses 2-3 speak about the LORD in the plain written style with "나"; from verse 4 the prayer speaks to him in 합쇼체 with "저" — Verses 2-3 name the LORD and "him" in the third person; verse 4 turns to "you" (style guide section 7a).
- [ ] [PSA 143:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/143/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "셀라" kept at the end of the verse — As at 140:4: a word whose meaning is no longer known, probably a musical direction (meaning decision).
- [ ] [PSA 143:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/143/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "주님의 선하신 영", with the honorific — The English reads God's own Spirit (meaning decision); the honorific "-신" marks this in Korean without naming the Holy Spirit, which the Hebrew does not.
- [ ] [PSA 144:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/144/#v3) L · setting_alternative · "사람" with no span; {{gender:한낱 인간|사람의 아들}} for the son of man — Korean "사람" already includes everyone, so the first English span has no second wording in Korean; for "son of man" Korean has both an inclusive and a traditional wording, as the English does. Both end in a consonant, so "이" fits.
- [ ] [PSA 144:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/144/#v12) L/B/R · register · Verses 12-15 speak of the people's hoped-for life in the plain style with "우리" — These verses are a blessing over the people rather than words spoken to God (meaning decision); they end in verse 15 with the LORD in the third person, so Korean uses the plain style, as Psalm 128.
- [ ] [PSA 145:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/145/#v1) L/B/R · register · The whole psalm is in 합쇼체 with "저", also in the verses that speak of the LORD in the third person (3, 8-9, 14, 17-21) — The psalm is one hymn of praise spoken before God, moving between "you" and "he" from verse to verse; changing the speech level each time would break it, so Korean keeps the polite style and simply names the LORD, as in Psalm 138.
- [ ] [PSA 150:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/psa/150/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "뿔나팔" for the ram's horn (shofar); "하프와 수금" for harp and lyre (also 144:9) — "뿔나팔" says it is a horn, as the meaning decision explains; the two stringed instruments follow the English names, with "수금" kept for the lyre through this block.

## 잠언 (6)

- [ ] [PRO 7:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/pro/7/#v14) L/B/R · register · The woman speaks to the young man in the polite informal style (해요체) and calls him "당신" (verses 14-20) — Her speech is coaxing and intimate; in Korean a woman speaking softly to a man she is luring would naturally use 해요체 with "당신", which sets her voice apart from the father's plain style. A reviewer may prefer plain intimate speech (반말).
- [ ] [PRO 8:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/pro/8/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "가지셨다" (L), "있게 하셨다" (B), "만드셨다" (R) for qanah — The verb can mean to acquire or possess, or to bring into being (meaning decision m1, which is uncertain). Literal keeps the possession sense as the English Literal does; Balanced says Wisdom was brought into being without choosing the word for creating; Readable follows the English Readable. A …
- [ ] [PRO 11:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/pro/11/#v30) L · word_choice · "영혼들을 얻는 자" for "one who takes lives" — The phrase means winning people over (meaning decision m1, which is uncertain). Korean "목숨을 취하다" would say killing, the opposite sense, so Literal uses "영혼", its word for nefesh, with "얻다" (gain, win); Balanced says winning people's hearts.
- [ ] [PRO 29:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/pro/29/#v10) L · word_choice · "그의 목숨을 보살핀다" (care for his life) at Literal, not word for word "목숨을 찾는다" (seek his life) — In Korean "목숨을 찾다" can only be read as hunting someone down to kill them. The meaning decision (m1) takes the upright as caring for the blameless one's life, so Literal gives that sense rather than a form Korean would read the other way.
- [ ] [PRO 30:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/pro/30/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · Names "아굴", "야게", "이디엘", "우갈"; the oracle (massa) is "신탁" (L), "엄숙한 선언" (B) — The names take the forms of the Protestant tradition (style guide section 5; the terms note gives "아굴"). "Massa" is a weighty, solemn message (meaning decision m1); Literal names it with the word for an oracle, Balanced says what it is, and Readable says "the message he gave", as the English …
- [ ] [PRO 30:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/pro/30/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "미련한 자" (L, B), "미련한 사람" (R) for nabal, as for kesil — The terms note does not list nabal. Korean uses the fool's word, as the English does with "fool"; "어리석은 자" is kept for the simple (peti), and "망령된 자" is archaic. The godless, boorish sense of nabal (meaning decision m1) is left to the note.

## 전도서 (1)

- [ ] [ECC 3:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ecc/3/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "영원" (L); "영원에 대한 감각" (B); "영원한 것을 그리워하는 마음" (R) — The book's settled wording for 'olam here, following the reading the meaning decision takes (m2). The familiar "영원을 사모하는 마음" of the Korean Bibles is avoided.

## 아가 (2)

- [ ] [SNG 1:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/sng/1/#v8) R · register · the man answers her, in 해요체 with "당신" — Korean must choose a speech level, and so a speaker (m1). Readable follows the reading that the man answers her question of 1:7; Literal and Balanced use "그대" and the plain style, which fit either speaker.
- [ ] [SNG 8:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/sng/8/#v6) L/B/R · setting_alternative · L "{{divine_name:주|야}}의 불꽃"; B "맹렬한 불꽃"; R "활활 타오르는 불" — As in English, Literal shows the short divine name in the word, and Balanced and Readable read it as making the flame great; both readings are possible (meaning decision).

## 이사야 (31)

- [ ] [ISA 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/1/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "아모스의 아들 이사야" — The terms note's published form (2 Kings 19:2); Korean Protestant tradition spells Isaiah's father like the prophet Amos, though the Hebrew names differ.
- [ ] [ISA 1:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/1/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "이스라엘의 전능하신 분" with "말한다" (B, R) for the declaration formula — The terms note's form for 1:24 and for *ne'um*: the titles keep the honorific, while the formula "말한다" is the oracle's own (Literal "...의 선언이다").
- [ ] [ISA 4:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/4/#v1) L/B/R · register · The women plead in the formal style (합쇼체) with "당신" — They are begging a man to take them as wives; 합쇼체 shows the pleading, and "당신" is the usual polite "you" where no title fits.
- [ ] [ISA 4:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/4/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "싹" for *tsemach*, the branch — The terms note keeps "싹" at every level so that this verse stays linked with Jeremiah 23:5 and 33:15; "가지" is kept for *netser* in 11:1. Whether the branch is a coming king is left open (meaning decision m1).
- [ ] [ISA 5:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/5/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "나의 사랑하는 이" (L); "내가 사랑하는 친구" (B); "내가 아끼는 친구" (R) — The singer's dear friend, who turns out to be the LORD (meaning decision m1). Literal keeps the word of love; Balanced and Readable say "friend" as the English does, and "이" avoids the dismissive "자".
- [ ] [ISA 5:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/5/#v10) B/R · setting_alternative · metric by default, the Hebrew measure as the alternative: "{{units:약 22리터|한 밧}}" — The Korean style guide sets metric as the default, as in the terms note (bath about 22 liters, homer about 220 liters, ephah about 22 liters). A "yoke" of land, what a pair of oxen ploughs in a day, is given as Korean "겨리"; ten of them are about 4 hectares. Literal keeps the units.
- [ ] [ISA 5:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/5/#v19) L/B/R · register · The mockers speak of God without the honorific ("그가 ... 해 보라지", "이스라엘의 거룩한 이") — They are making fun of God (meaning decision m1); dropping "-시-" marks their scorn, as Sennacherib's words do in 2 Kings 19:10.
- [ ] [ISA 6:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/6/#v9) B · grammar · "깨닫지 못할 것이다", "알지 못할 것이다" (B) for the commands "do not understand", "do not perceive" — The commands announce what will happen (meaning decision m2); a Korean command not to understand would read as if God forbade understanding, so Balanced and Readable put the outcome. Literal keeps the commands.
- [ ] [ISA 7:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/7/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}께서 이와 같이 말씀하신다" (L); "이렇게 말씀하신다" (B); "하시는 말씀이다" (R), closing with a period — The terms note's forms for *koh amar YHWH* (2 Kings 19:6); here inside God's message the formula keeps the honorific because the message is quoted as the LORD's words. The words that follow take quotation marks because they are a message inside a message.
- [ ] [ISA 7:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/7/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "그 젊은 여자" (L, B); "한 젊은 여자" (R), with "임신하여" — The terms note: "처녀" is kept for *betulah* (2 Kings 19:21) and for the Greek of Matthew 1:23, which the English footnote cites; the Hebrew word here means a young woman of age to marry (meaning decision m1).
- [ ] [ISA 7:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/7/#v16) L/B/R · register · "임금님" for the singular "you", Ahaz — Isaiah turns from the royal house to Ahaz himself; "임금님" with "-시-" is the respectful address to a king in this translation.
- [ ] [ISA 8:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/8/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "실로아" — The Hebrew name here is Shiloah; John 9:7's "실로암" follows the Greek form of the same pool.
- [ ] [ISA 8:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/8/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "임마누엘이여" — The name of 7:14 in the vocative; "-이여" is the respectful form used for addressing a name in this translation.
- [ ] [ISA 8:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/8/#v9) L/B/R · grammar · "부서질 것이다" for the command "be shattered" — The command announces what will surely happen (meaning decision m2); a Korean command "부서져라" would sound like a curse, so the outcome is stated.
- [ ] [ISA 8:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/8/#v13) L/B/R · register · "그" without the honorific, since God himself speaks of himself in the third person — The LORD is still speaking (verses 11-15); God names himself without "-시-" (terms note), so "그" and "거느린" are used.
- [ ] [ISA 8:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/8/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "신접한 자들과 점쟁이들" (L); "혼백과 귀신" (B); "죽은 사람의 영들" (R) — The English Isaiah table: *'ob* is "spirit of the dead" (L) and "ghost" (B, R). Korean "무당" would bring in Korean shamanism.
- [ ] [ISA 9:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/9/#v2) L/B/R · register · The prophet addresses God in the formal style (합쇼체) with "주님" in verses 2-4; verse 5 returns to proclamation in the plain style — Words spoken to God are 합쇼체 (terms note). Verses 5-6 do not address God and announce the child to all who hear, so they take the plain style of the oracles.
- [ ] [ISA 9:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/9/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · L "놀라운 조언자, 용사이신 하나님, 영원의 아버지, 평화의 통치자"; B "놀라운 일을 계획하는 이, ..., 영원한 아버지, 평화의 통치자"; R "놀라운 계획을 세우는 이, ..., 평화를 가져오는 통치자" — The terms note's forms, which follow the English reading of four titles of two words each (meaning decisions m4-m8) rather than the familiar older wording.
- [ ] [ISA 9:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/9/#v5) L/B/R · register · "그" and plain verbs, without "-시-", for the child — The terms note: the child of 9:5 takes no honorific, as the English has no deity_pronoun span for him; "용사이신 하나님" keeps the honorific inside the title itself.
- [ ] [ISA 9:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/9/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "뽕나무" for the sycamore fig — The common Korean Bible name for the tree; the point is cheap wood against costly cedar (meaning decision m1).
- [ ] [ISA 10:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/10/#v10) L/B · word_order · Verse 10 left as an open clause ending in a comma, completed by verse 11 — The Hebrew sentence runs from verse 10 into verse 11. Korean puts the main verb last, so verse 10 is left open with a comma and the question of verse 11 completes it.
- [ ] [ISA 12:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/12/#v2) L/B/R · setting_alternative · "{{divine_name:주|야}}, 곧 {{divine_name:주|야훼}}" for "Yah, Yahweh" — The Psalms note gives Yah as "{{divine_name:주|야}}". By default the two read "주, 곧 주", but with the setting they show the Hebrew's short form and full name side by side (meaning decision m2); the line follows the song of the sea and Psalm 118:14.
- [ ] [ISA 13:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/13/#v13) L/B/R · register · God names himself in the third person: "그의" at Literal and Balanced, Readable "거느린 {{divine_name:주|야훼}}가 ... 내 분노" — God speaks of himself by name (meaning decision m1). Literal and Balanced keep the Hebrew's third person; Readable, which spells the sentence out, keeps God's own voice without "-시-", as the terms note asks.
- [ ] [ISA 14:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/14/#v13) L/B · word_choice · "사본" for Zaphon (L, B); "북쪽" (R) — The name of the northern mountain is kept as a name (meaning decision m2), with "먼 북쪽" at Balanced to show where it is; Readable says only "the north", as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [ISA 16:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/16/#v3) L/B/R · register · Moab's refugees plead with Zion in the plain style with "다오" (B, R) — Moab's refugees ask Zion for shelter (meaning decision m1); "-아/어 다오" keeps the pleading tone of the Hebrew commands within the plain style of the oracle.
- [ ] [ISA 19:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/19/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "주님, 곧 만군의 {{divine_name:주|야훼}}" — Here the Hebrew has the article, "the Lord", followed by "Yahweh of hosts" (meaning decision m3); Korean keeps the title "주님" apart, as the English does, rather than the compound form "주 하나님".
- [ ] [ISA 19:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/19/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "멤피스" for Noph — The English gives the city's known name at every level (meaning decision m1); Korean does the same, though older Korean Bibles transliterate the Hebrew "놉".
- [ ] [ISA 20:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/20/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "총사령관" at every level — Here the English takes the Assyrian title as a title at every level (meaning decision m2), so Korean does too; 2 Kings 18:17 L keeps it as the name "다르단".
- [ ] [ISA 41:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/41/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "내가 그다" (L), "내가 바로 그다" (B), "나만이 하나님이다" (R) for 'ani hu — The Korean terms note's wording for 'ani hu throughout Isaiah 40-66; Readable says what the phrase claims, as the English Readable does (m2). The main note is at 43:10.
- [ ] [ISA 43:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/43/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "내가 그인 줄" (L), "내가 바로 그인 줄" (B), "나만이 하나님이라는 것을" (R) for 'ani hu, the main note for the phrase in Korean — The Hebrew is "I am he", which the Korean keeps at L and B as the terms note sets; the Greek of John 8:24 and 8:28 (egō eimi) is published as "내가 나임을", and readers may hear this verse behind it, but Isaiah is drafted from the Hebrew. Readable says what the claim means, as the English Readable does …
- [ ] [ISA 66:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/isa/66/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "뿔", "룻", "두발", "야완" (L, B), "그리스" for Javan (R) — Lud, Tubal and Javan have the forms published at Genesis 10 and 1 Chronicles 1; the king Pul is "불" at 2 Kings 15:19, but here a distant people is meant (m3), and "뿔" keeps it apart from the king and from the common word "불" (fire). Readable says Greece, as the English Readable does.

## 예레미야 (32)

- [ ] [JER 31:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/31/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "여자가 남자를 둘러쌀 것이다" (L, R); "감싸 안을 것이다" (B) — The saying is very uncertain (meaning decision m3); Korean keeps the bare words, as the English does, without choosing one explanation.
- [ ] [JER 35:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/35/#v5) L/B/R · register · Jeremiah speaks to the Rechabites in 하오체 ("드시오"); they answer him in 합쇼체 ("마시지 않습니다") — A prophet speaking to the elders of a clan as an equal and a host: 하오체 is the register the Kings drafts use between grown men (2 Kings 10:15, Jehu and Jehonadab). The Rechabites answer a prophet in the temple with respect.
- [ ] [JER 36:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/36/#v5) L/B/R · register · Jeremiah speaks to Baruch, his scribe, in 해라체 — Jeremiah is Baruch's master in the work; the officials and Baruch speak to each other in 하오체 and 합쇼체 (verses 15-19).
- [ ] [JER 36:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/36/#v14) L/B/R · register · The officials and Baruch speak to each other in 하오체 ("오시오", "앉으시오"); Baruch answers them in 합쇼체 (verse 18) — Royal officials speaking to a scribe they respect; the register used between grown men in the Kings drafts.
- [ ] [JER 36:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/36/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "신하" for the king's servants at every level — The terms note gives 종들 (L) and 신하들 (B, R) for court officials; here Literal also says 신하 because the room is the king's own court and "종" would read as household slaves.
- [ ] [JER 37:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/37/#v13) L/B/R · register · Irijah accuses Jeremiah in 해라체 ("너는"); Jeremiah answers him in 하오체 ("거짓말이오") — A hostile arrest; Jeremiah answers a guard captain as one grown man to another.
- [ ] [JER 38:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/38/#v12) L/B/R · register · Ebed-melech speaks to the prophet in 합쇼체 ("대십시오") — A palace servant speaking with respect to a prophet he is rescuing; the polite "please" of the Hebrew is carried by the honorific ending.
- [ ] [JER 38:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/38/#v22) L/B/R · register · The women's lament over the king is in 해라체 ("너를") inside Jeremiah's 합쇼체 — The women speak in a mocking dirge, not to the king's face; Korean keeps the bare second person of the song.
- [ ] [JER 39:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/39/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "네르갈사레셀, 삼갈느보, 살스김, 네르갈사레셀" — Korean spells the names in the Protestant pattern (느보 as 1 Chr 5:8), divided as the Hebrew divides them, following the meaning decision.
- [ ] [JER 40:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/40/#v2) L/B/R · register · Nebuzaradan speaks to Jeremiah in 하오체 and speaks of Jeremiah's God with "-시-" — A conquering officer speaking with respect to a prophet his king protects (39:12); 하오체 is the register Gedaliah uses with the commanders in 2 Kings 25:24.
- [ ] [JER 40:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/40/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "여사냐" for Jezaniah here; "느도바 사람", "마아가 사람" as 2 Kings 25:23 — 2 Kings 25:23 has the form Jaazaniah (야아사냐); Jeremiah's Hebrew has Jezaniah, and each verse keeps its own form.
- [ ] [JER 41:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/41/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "팔십 명" (L); "80명" (B, R) — Numbers of people in figures at Balanced and Readable as the terms note sets; Literal writes this count in words, as the Kings Literal does for small counts.
- [ ] [JER 41:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/41/#v6) L/B/R · register · Ishmael speaks to the pilgrims in 하오체 ("갑시다"); the ten men beg him in 합쇼체 (verse 8) — A false welcome between grown men who do not know each other; the men who plead for their lives speak to him with respect.
- [ ] [JER 42:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/42/#v2) L/B/R · register · The people ask Jeremiah in 합쇼체; Jeremiah answers them in 하오체 ("들었소", "알려 주겠소") and frames God's word with "말씀하시오" (verse 9) — The people come as petitioners to a prophet; Jeremiah speaks to the assembly as a grown man to grown men, while God's own words inside his speech keep 해라체.
- [ ] [JER 43:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/43/#v2) L/B/R · register · The leaders now answer Jeremiah in 하오체 ("거짓을 말하고 있소"), dropping the 합쇼체 of their request (42:2) — The change of speech level marks their turn against the prophet without making them openly insolent; they still speak of God with "-시-".
- [ ] [JER 44:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/44/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "멤피스" for *Noph* at every level; "바드로스" (L, B), "이집트 남쪽 바드로스 지방" (R) — As the English, which says Memphis for *Noph* at every level (2:16); 믹돌 and 바드로스 keep the Protestant spellings.
- [ ] [JER 44:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/44/#v16) L/B/R · register · The people answer Jeremiah in 하오체 ("듣지 않겠소"), as the leaders did in 43:2 — A blunt refusal made to the prophet's face; the speech level drops from the 합쇼체 of their first request (42:2).
- [ ] [JER 44:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/44/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "표징" for the sign — "표적" is the style guide's word for the signs of John; "표징" is a confirming token, as here.
- [ ] [JER 46:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/46/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "유향" for the balm of Gilead — The resin of Gilead, used as medicine (8:22); Readable says what it was for.
- [ ] [JER 46:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/46/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "너의 강한 자" (L, B), "너의 힘센 자" (R) — The meaning decision leaves the word open (a strong man, a bull, Egypt's champion); Korean keeps a word that fits all three.
- [ ] [JER 47:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/47/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "갑돌 섬" (L), "갑돌 바닷가 땅" (B); "크레타 섬" (R) — Caphtor keeps its biblical name at Literal and Balanced; Readable names the island as the English does.
- [ ] [JER 48:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/48/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "노간주나무" (L); "덤불" (B, R) — Literal names the shrub as the English Literal does ("juniper"); the meaning decision says it is a lone shrub in dry land, and Balanced and Readable say so.
- [ ] [JER 48:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/48/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "날개" — The English reads "wings"; the meaning decision says the word is uncertain, and Korean follows the English reading.
- [ ] [JER 48:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/48/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · The town names in verses 21-24 in the Protestant spellings (홀론, 야하스, 메바앗, 디본, 느보, 벧디블라다임, 기랴다임, 벧가물, 벧므온, 그리옷, 보스라) — As published for the same towns in Numbers and Joshua where they occur; the list is prose, as the English sets it.
- [ ] [JER 49:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/49/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "믿음 없는 딸" at every level for *habbat hashovevah* — The terms note's root for turning (*shuv*): "돌아선" is kept for Israel; for Ammon the English "faithless" is followed.
- [ ] [JER 50:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/50/#v7) L/B/R · register · The enemies speak of the LORD with "-시-" ("목장이신") — Korean narration gives God the honorific whoever speaks of him; the words of the enemies are still Jeremiah's report.
- [ ] [JER 50:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/50/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "이스라엘의 거룩한 이" — The terms note: God naming himself takes no honorific; the commands of this verse are God's own speech (verse 31).
- [ ] [JER 50:39](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/50/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "사막의 짐승들", "하이에나들", "타조들" — The animals as the English names them; Isaiah 13:20-21, which the meaning decision cites, should be compared when reviewed.
- [ ] [JER 51:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/51/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "립가매", with "곧 바빌로니아 사람들" added at R — The meaning decision: a coded name for the Chaldeans; Korean keeps the name and Readable explains it, as the English does.
- [ ] [JER 51:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/51/#v13) L/B/R · idiom · "네가 끊어질 [실의] 길이가 [찼다]" (L); "생명의 실을 잴 만큼 재었으니 이제 끊을 때다" (B); "베틀에서 실을 잘라 내듯이" (R) — The meaning decision: the length of the thread is measured and cut; Korean keeps the weaver's picture.
- [ ] [JER 51:43](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/51/#v43) L · setting_alternative · "{{gender:사람|사람의 아들}}" (L), as at 49:18 — The English Literal keeps "son of man" here without a span; Korean uses the same span as 49:18 and 50:40 so the phrase reads alike.
- [ ] [JER 51:59](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jer/51/#v59) L/B/R · word_choice · "숙영 담당관" (L, B); "쉬어 갈 곳을 마련하는 일을 맡은 관리" (R) — The meaning decision: probably the officer in charge of the stops on the journey; Readable says so in full.

## 예레미야애가 (1)

- [ ] [LAM 4:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/lam/4/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "귀족들" for Zion's "princes" — The word is not the usual one for officials (sar, "고관" / "관리" / "지도자" in this book), and the meaning decision reads it as Zion's nobles, so Korean uses "귀족들" at every level and keeps "고관" for sar.

## 에스겔 (46)

- [ ] [EZK 1:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/1/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "호박금" (L); "빛나는 금속" (B); "반짝이는 금속" (R) — The substance is uncertain; "호박금" (electrum) keeps a single named substance at Literal, as the English "amber", and the other levels describe its shine. Used again at 1:27 and 8:2.
- [ ] [EZK 1:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/1/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "귀감람석" (chrysolite) — The published Korean of Exodus 28:20 and 39:13 gives this stone of the breastpiece as "귀감람석"; the same word is kept here.
- [ ] [EZK 2:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/2/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "사람의 아들아" at every level, with no gender span — God's address to Ezekiel means "human being, mortal"; "인자" is Jesus's title in the Korean New Testament, so Ezekiel keeps a plain wording that is not the Gospel title. Later uses in the book refer back to this decision.
- [ ] [EZK 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/2/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "반역하다" (L, B); "죄를 짓다" (R) for the verb of transgression; "거역하다" for the verb of rebellion — The term sheet's wording for the transgression verb; the rebellion verb takes another word so that Literal keeps the two Hebrew verbs apart.
- [ ] [EZK 5:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/5/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "내가 살아 있음을 [두고 맹세한다]. $LG의 선언이다." (L); "내가 살아 있음을 두고 맹세한다. $LG의 말이다." (B, R) — The oath and the formula "declares the Lord GOD" as the term sheet fixes them; Balanced and Readable say "…의 말이다" so that no particle follows the span that would read wrongly with one of its two forms.
- [ ] [EZK 6:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/6/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "내가 어떻게 상심했는지" (L, B); "내 마음을 얼마나 아프게 했는지" (R) — Korean cannot say of God that he "was broken" without suggesting an object shattered; "상심하다" (to be heartbroken) keeps the meaning decision's sense of grief.
- [ ] [EZK 7:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/7/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "군주" (L, B); "통치자" (R) — The term sheet's wording for Ezekiel's prince (nasi'), used through the book.
- [ ] [EZK 10:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/10/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "회오리 바퀴" (L); "도는 바퀴" (B, R) — The term sheet's wording for the whirling wheels (galgal), kept apart from the ordinary word for the wheels ("바퀴") in 10:6 and 10:13.
- [ ] [EZK 11:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/11/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "$DN에게서" in the people's words, without "께" — The people of Jerusalem speak dismissively, and the particle 에게서 fits both forms of the span; this follows the English, which does not soften their words.
- [ ] [EZK 12:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/12/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "사람의 아들아" for God's address to Ezekiel (ben-adam), at every level — As decided at 2:1: 인자 is Jesus's title in the Korean New Testament, and Ezekiel's address means only "human being", so it is kept plain. No gender span, since Ezekiel is one man.
- [ ] [EZK 12:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/12/#v9) L/B/R · register · The exiles ask Ezekiel in 합쇼체 with 당신 ("당신은 무엇을 하고 있습니까?") — The term sheet's address for the people speaking to the prophet (12:9; 21:12; 24:19; 37:18): polite but not deferential, as strangers and adults address a man in public.
- [ ] [EZK 12:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/12/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "군주" (L, B), "통치자" (R) for the prince (nasi'); "신탁" (L, B), "말씀" (R) for the oracle (massa) — The term sheet's words. Korean cannot keep the Hebrew play of nasi' and massa' ("burden"); the shared footnote explains it.
- [ ] [EZK 12:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/12/#v11) L/B/R · register · Ezekiel speaks to the exiles in his own voice in 하오체 with 여러분 ("나는 여러분의 표징이오") — The term sheet's register for the prophet addressing the people in his own person, as Jeremiah 42:4, 9; God's oracles that he passes on stay 해라체. Here God gives him the words, but the 'I' is Ezekiel.
- [ ] [EZK 12:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/12/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "주 {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}의 선언이다" (L), "주 {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}의 말이다" (B, R) for "declares the Lord GOD", as its own sentence — The particle rule for the span: 가 cannot follow both 하나님 and 야훼, so B and R use 의 말이다. Inside God's speech he names himself without -시-.
- [ ] [EZK 13:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/13/#v6) L/B/R · register · "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}께서는 그들을 보내지 않으셨다" with -시-, though God himself is speaking — The sentence names the LORD in the third person, as the prophet's narrative voice; the English reads it so too ("the LORD has not sent them"). Where God says "I" (13:7) he takes no -시-.
- [ ] [EZK 13:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/13/#v11) L · word_order · "너희 우박덩이들아, 떨어져라" keeps the storm addressed directly — The Hebrew turns to speak to the hailstones (see the shared decision); Literal keeps the vocative, and Balanced keeps "너희" inside a statement.
- [ ] [EZK 13:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/13/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "띠" (L, B), "부적 띠" (R) for the bands; "너울" (L, B), "머릿수건" (R) for the veils; "영혼" (L), "목숨" (B, R) for nefesh — The objects are uncertain (see the shared footnote), so L and B name them plainly and R says what the English R says, that they were used for magic. 영혼 at L matches the term sheet's L for nefesh in 18:4; 목숨 gives the sense "lives" that the hunting image needs.
- [ ] [EZK 14:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/14/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "내가 살아 있음을 [두고 맹세한다]" (L), "내가 살아 있음을 두고 맹세한다" (B, R) for the oath "as I live", as its own sentence — The term sheet fixes this wording for chai 'ani, which Jeremiah had rendered unevenly; Literal brackets the words the Hebrew leaves unsaid. The oath and the formula stand as two short sentences, so the conditional clause before them stays open.
- [ ] [EZK 16:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/16/#v10) L/B · word_choice · "고운 가죽" for the fine leather (tachash) and "비단" for the costly fabric — Both materials are uncertain (see the shared decisions); the Korean names them plainly, as the English "fine leather" and "costly fabric" do, and does not name an animal.
- [ ] [EZK 19:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/19/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · 애가 (L, B), 슬픈 장례 노래 (R); nasi' is 군주 (L, B), 통치자 (R) — 애가 is the published word for a lament (2 Sam 1:17; 2 Chr 35:25); Readable says what it was. Nasi' follows the term sheet for Ezekiel: 군주 / 군주 / 통치자, kept apart from 왕 (melekh) and 고관 (sar).
- [ ] [EZK 20:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/20/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · 사람의 아들아, at every level — Ben-adam is God's address to Ezekiel: "human being, mortal". Korean keeps the plain 사람의 아들 at every level, so that it is never read as 인자, Jesus's title in the Korean New Testament; no gender span, since Ezekiel is one man. 인자야, the older Protestant wording, is the alternative. As at 2:1.
- [ ] [EZK 20:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/20/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · Thus says the Lord GOD: 주 {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}께서 이와 같이 말씀하신다 (L), 이렇게 말씀하신다 (B), 께서 하시는 말씀이다 (R), ending in a period; declares the Lord GOD: 주 … — 하나님 ends in a consonant and 야훼 in a vowel, so no particle that changes form (가, 는, 를) may follow the span; the term sheet's forms use only 께서, 의 and 이다. In God's own mouth "Thus says" keeps 께서, as the published prophets do. Recorded once for chapters 19-24.
- [ ] [EZK 21:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/21/#v12) L/B/R · register · the exiles ask Ezekiel in 합쇼체 with 당신; he answers for himself in 하오체 (소식 때문이오), and the oracle he passes on is in 해라체 — The term sheet's address forms for Ezekiel and the people (it names 21:12). The change of level marks where Ezekiel's own answer ends and the LORD's word begins.
- [ ] [EZK 21:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/21/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · 내 아들의 막대기가 모든 나무를 업신여긴다 (L, B), 깔본다 (R), kept word for word — The sense of these words is uncertain (meaning decision); like the English, Korean keeps them as they stand rather than guessing. 막대기 keeps the link to "the rod that despises" in verse 18.
- [ ] [EZK 21:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/21/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · pesha': 반역 (L, B); 죄 (R) — The term sheet's wording; Readable folds it into the plain word for sin.
- [ ] [EZK 23:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/23/#v1) L/B/R · register · chapter 23 in Korean: Literal follows the Hebrew; Balanced and Readable say the sexual picture plainly and soberly (동침하다, 성관계를 가지다, 욕정), adding nothing; the … — As the English register decision for this chapter, and as Korean Leviticus 18 words such matters. The chapter is marked for careful review by a native Korean reviewer.
- [ ] [EZK 23:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/23/#v20) L/B/R · register · 살 and 사정 (L); 성기 and 사정 (B); 성기 and 정액 (R) — As the English register decision: Literal keeps "flesh", Balanced and Readable name what is meant in plain, sober words, neither softer nor coarser than the Hebrew.
- [ ] [EZK 23:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/23/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · 브곳, 소아, 고아 — 브곳 is the published form (Jer 50:21); 소아 and 고아 are spelled the same way from the Hebrew.
- [ ] [EZK 24:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/24/#v19) L/B/R · register · the people ask Ezekiel in 합쇼체 with 당신, and he answers in 하오체 (24:20); the LORD's word he passes on stays 해라체 — The term sheet's address forms for the exiles and Ezekiel, which name this verse.
- [ ] [EZK 25:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/25/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "사람의 아들아" for God's address to Ezekiel (ben-adam), at every level, with no gender span — As decided at 2:1: 인자 is Jesus's title in the Korean New Testament, while Ezekiel's address means only "human being", so it is kept plain. Ezekiel is one man, so no gender span.
- [ ] [EZK 25:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/25/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "주 {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}의 선언이다" (L); "주 {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}의 말이다" (B, R) for "declares the Lord GOD", standing as its own sentence — The term sheet's wording (uncertain): the Isaiah form "…가 말한다" cannot follow {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}, whose two forms need different particles, so B and R say "…의 말이다", as Ezekiel 13-21 do.
- [ ] [EZK 26:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/26/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "군주들" (L, B), "통치자들" (R) for the princes (nasi') of the sea — The term sheet's word for nasi' in Ezekiel (uncertain), kept apart from 왕 (melekh).
- [ ] [EZK 27:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/27/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "반낙" (L, transliterated) for pannag; "기장" (millet) (B, R) — Follows the English: nobody knows what pannag was, so Literal keeps the word and Balanced and Readable give the likeliest food, as the shared footnote explains (uncertain; open for the founder).
- [ ] [EZK 30:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/30/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "굽" for Kub, transliterated at every level — As the English: nobody knows this people, so the name is only written in Hangul (open for the founder, with pannag in 27:17).
- [ ] [EZK 33:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/33/#v10) L/B · word_choice · "반역" (L, B), "죄" (R) for pesha' — The term sheet's wording (uncertain).
- [ ] [EZK 34:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/34/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "사람의 아들아" at every level, with no gender span — God's address to Ezekiel (ben-adam) is the plain "human being", kept apart from 인자, Jesus's title in the Korean New Testament, as decided at Ezekiel 2:1 for the whole book.
- [ ] [EZK 34:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/34/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "주 {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}의 선언이다" (L); "주 {{divine_name:하나님|야훼}}의 말이다" (B, R), as its own sentence — "Lord GOD" ends in a different sound in each form of the span, so no subject particle can follow it; "의 선언이다" and "의 말이다" read correctly after both (term sheet, section 0). Used for every "declares the Lord GOD" in chapters 34-40.
- [ ] [EZK 34:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/34/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "군주" (L, B); "통치자" (R) for nasi' — The Ezekiel term sheet's word for Ezekiel's ruler of Israel, used again in 37:25 and chapters 44-48; "왕자" would suggest a king's son.
- [ ] [EZK 37:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/37/#v18) L/B/R · register · the people ask Ezekiel in 합쇼체 ("…말해 주지 않겠습니까?", "…말해 주십시오"), with "당신" at Literal — The exiles address the prophet politely, as the term sheet sets for their questions to Ezekiel (12:9; 24:19; 37:18).
- [ ] [EZK 38:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/38/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "메섹과 두발의 으뜸 군주" (L, B); "메섹과 두발을 다스리는 최고 통치자" (R) — Rosh is read as "chief", as the English does. The other reading takes it as a people, "로스와 메섹과 두발의 군주"; 로스 is an ancient name and has nothing to do with 러시아 (Russia). Gog and Magog are 곡 and 마곡, as in Revelation 20:8.
- [ ] [EZK 40:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/40/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "문간방" (L, B); "통로 옆 방" (R) for the alcove; "현관" for the porch at every level — The term sheet's temple words; the porch is "현관" as in Korean 1 Kings 6:3. Used throughout chapters 40-48.
- [ ] [EZK 40:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/40/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "벽기둥" (L, B); "옆 기둥" (R) for the jambs — The term sheet's temple words for the thick walls on each side of an opening; used throughout chapters 40-48.
- [ ] [EZK 40:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/40/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "닫힌 창" (L); "안으로 좁아지는 창" (B); "깊숙이 들어간 창" (R) — The sense is uncertain (narrowing frames or lattices); Literal keeps the Hebrew's word "closed", and the other levels describe a recessed window, as the English does.
- [ ] [EZK 44:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/44/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "군주" (L, B) / "통치자" (R) for the prince (nasi') — The term sheet's wording (uncertain). Ezekiel avoids calling the ruler of the restored people a king; 왕자 would wrongly make him a king's son. Readable says "ruler", as the English does.
- [ ] [EZK 47:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/47/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · Place names in the Protestant forms of the Korean Numbers 34 where they occur there (르보하맛, 스닷, 하살에난, 이집트 시내); the others transliterated in the same style (헤들론, … — Ezekiel's borders retrace Numbers 34, so the shared names are spelled as there; the names found only here follow the Protestant spelling conventions of the style guide.
- [ ] [EZK 48:35](https://openwordbible.org/ko/ezk/48/#v35) L/B/R · word_choice · The city's name '{{divine_name:주|야훼}}께서 거기 계시다' (L, B, in single quotation marks), quoted as "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}께서 거기 계신다" at Readable; the name gives God … — The name is what the people will call the city, so it is worded as they would say it, with -시-, as in published names that speak of God. A transliteration would hide the meaning, and the divine_name span keeps the reader's setting. Readable sets the name in quotation marks, as the English does.

The heavenly beings take no -시-, like the angels.

Readable's "사람처럼 보이는 분" keeps the same noun, which also keeps the figure apart from the angels Daniel 8-10 calls "사람처럼 보이는 이". In the vision he is a figure in human …
- [ ] [DAN 7:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/7/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "지극히 높으신 분의 거룩한 이들" (L, B); "하나님의 거룩한 백성" (R) — "성도" is the New Testament word for believers and would decide the open question the footnoted meaning decision leaves open (God's people, heavenly beings, or both); "거룩한 이들" keeps it open, and Readable follows the English Readable.
- [ ] [DAN 7:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/7/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "한 때와 두 때와 반 때" (L, B); "세 기간 반" (R) — The term sheet's wording, as Revelation 12:14 (L, B); Readable keeps "periods" as the English Readable does and does not say years, which the footnoted meaning decision gives only as the usual reading.
- [ ] [DAN 7:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/7/#v27) L · word_choice · "그 나라 … 그를" (L), keeping the Aramaic singular; "그들" (B, R) — The singular may refer to the people or to the Most High, as the footnoted meaning decision says; Literal keeps an unhonoured singular so as not to decide, and Balanced and Readable say "them" as the English does.
- [ ] [DAN 8:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/8/#v13) L/B/R · register · The holy ones speak to each other in 해라체 ("언제까지 계속되겠느냐?") — Angels take no honorific in Daniel; one asking another is speech between equals, which the Korean style guide puts in 해라체.
- [ ] [DAN 8:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/8/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "사람의 아들아" at every level, as in Ezekiel — Here ben-adam is the angel's address to a mortal, as God's to Ezekiel; "인자" is Jesus's title in the Korean New Testament and is kept for the "one like a son of man" of 7:13, so this plain form keeps the two apart.
- [ ] [DAN 9:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/9/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "일흔 번의 일곱" (L, B); "일곱씩 일흔 번" (R) — The text gives a number and no unit; "이레" means seven days and would decide the reading, so the term sheet keeps the bare "일곱". Sixty-two and seven sevens follow the same pattern in verses 25-27.
- [ ] [DAN 9:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/9/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "기름 부음 받은 이, 곧 지도자" (L); "기름 부음 받은 지도자" (B); "하나님께서 택하신 지도자" (R), with no -시- — The term sheet's wording: a description, not a title, so it takes no honorific, as the English takes no deity pronoun; Readable follows Isaiah 45:1.
- [ ] [DAN 10:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/10/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "군주" (L, B); "다스리는 천사" (R) for an angelic sar; "미가엘" — The term sheet's wording for the princes of 10:13-21 and 12:1; Michael is spelled as in Jude 9 and Revelation 12:7. The angels take no honorific.
- [ ] [DAN 10:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/10/#v16) L/B/R · register · "내 주여" at every level, Readable included, with Daniel in 합쇼체 and 저 — The term sheet keeps "내 주여" for adoni addressed to an angel and "주님" for God alone; the English Readable "Sir" has no Korean equivalent that is both polite and fitting for a heavenly being.
- [ ] [DAN 12:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/12/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "큰 군주" (L, B); "큰 천사" (R) for Michael — The term sheet's wording for the angelic sar, as in 10:13, 21; Michael takes no honorific.
- [ ] [DAN 12:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/12/#v6) L/B/R · register · The question to the man in linen is in 합쇼체 ("언제까지입니까?") — The man in linen is the chief messenger of 10:5; the one who asks is put below him, as Daniel is when he asks the same thing in 12:8.
- [ ] [DAN 12:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/12/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "한 때와 두 때와 반 때" (L, B); "세 기간 반" (R) — The term sheet's wording, as 7:25 and Revelation 12:14; Readable does not say three and a half years, because the English Readable keeps "periods".
- [ ] [DAN 12:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/dan/12/#v8) L/B/R · register · "내 주여" at every level, Readable included — As at 10:16: Daniel addresses the angel as "내 주여" in 합쇼체; "주님" is kept for God.

Keeping "긍휼" in both the name and the verb lets the reversal in 2:3 and 2:25 be seen. Romans 9:25 has "사랑받지 못하던 …

- [ ] [HOS 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/hos/2/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "암미", "루하마" (L); "내 백성", "긍휼 받음" (B, R) — The names of 1:6 and 1:9 without the "not". "긍휼 받음" keeps the word of "긍휼 없음" so the reversal can be heard. Romans 9:25 and 1 Peter 2:10 ("긍휼을 얻었습니다") draw on these names.

- [ ] [HOS 10:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/hos/10/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "살만", "벧아벨" — Spelled after Protestant convention for these names (cf. 살만에셀, 2 Kings 17:3); the shared footnote says the event is otherwise unknown.
- [ ] [HOS 14:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/hos/14/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · "잣나무" for *berosh* — The Kings word for the tree (1 Kings 5:22 and 6:15 in published Korean); "늘 푸른" at R carries the English "always green".

## 요엘 (2)

Joel 2:25 repeats the list.
- [ ] [JOL 4:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jol/4/#v21) L/B/R · register · "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}가 시온에 산다" without -시- — The last line is still God's own speech, which names him without -시- (Isaiah note); the English final line reads the same way.

Literal keeps the unclear "it" ("그것"), which most read as the punishment, as the English does; Balanced and Readable say the punishment.

- [ ] [AMO 4:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/amo/4/#v1) B/R · register · The women's words to their husbands are 해요체 ("가져와요") at B and R; L keeps the Hebrew "주인들" with plain 해라체 — The term sheet gives 해요체 for husband and wife (Genesis note, Hosea 3); the demand is a wife's to her husband, while Literal keeps the Hebrew "lords".

- [ ] [AMO 5:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/amo/5/#v27) L/B/R · register · The closing "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}가 말한다. 그의 이름은 만군의 하나님이다" without -시-; "다메섹 너머" — The verse is God's own speech, which names him without the honorific; Stephen's "beyond Babylon" (Acts 7:43) adapts the text, and Korean keeps the Hebrew "Damascus".

"Canaan" here means traders, which Balanced and Readable say as the English does.
- [ ] [ZEP 3:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zep/3/#v18) L · word_choice · "a burden on her" is "그에게 짐이" at Literal — The Hebrew turns from "you" to "her" (the city), and the meaning is uncertain (footnoted); Literal keeps the turn with a plain "그" rather than "그녀", per the term sheet's rule for Zion.

## 학개 (3)

Literal uses a distinct word for *mal'akhut*, which the Hebrew uses only here.

Korean "어떤 사람" and "자기" cover both sexes, so the English gender span has no counterpart.
- [ ] [HAG 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/hag/2/#v14) L/B/R · register · Haggai's answer passes on the LORD's word, so it is 해라체 with God's "나", not Haggai's 하오체 — As the term sheet says for Ezekiel: the prophet speaks in his own voice in 하오체, but the oracle he passes on stays 해라체 (uncertain).

## 스가랴 (15)

- [ ] [ZEC 1:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/1/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "they repented" (*shuv*) is "돌이켜" (L, R) and "회개하며" (B) — Here *shuv* is a change of mind after judgment, not a return from exile; the English Balanced says "repented", which Korean says with "회개하다", while Literal and Readable keep the turning picture.
- [ ] [ZEC 1:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/1/#v9) L/B/R · register · "the angel who talked with me" is "나와 말하던 천사" at every level; Zechariah addresses him as "내 주여" in 합쇼체, and the angel answers in 해라체 without -시- — The term sheet's forms (Revelation 21:15 L): "주님" is kept for God, so the polite *adoni* is "내 주여" (uncertain). Recorded once for all of Zechariah's visions (1:9–6:8), in both halves of the book.
- [ ] [ZEC 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/2/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · *charash* is "대장장이" (L, B) and "솜씨 좋은 일꾼" (R) — The word covers workers in wood, stone and metal; men who strike down horns are pictured best as smiths, while Readable keeps the general sense as the English does.
- [ ] [ZEC 3:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/3/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "he showed me" takes no -시- ("보여 주었다") — The meaning decision leaves open whether the LORD or the angel who talked with me is meant; the English has no deity span, so the Korean reads it as the angel.
- [ ] [ZEC 3:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/3/#v2) L/B/R · register · "May the LORD rebuke you" is "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}가 너를 꾸짖을 것이다", without -시-, since the LORD himself speaks — In God's own mouth his name takes no honorific; a Korean wish ("…하기를 바란다") from God would sound like a request, so the sentence is a firm declaration. The meaning decision footnotes that the LORD may speak through his angel here.
- [ ] [ZEC 4:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/4/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "the tin stone" is "주석 돌" (L) and "다림추" (B, R) — Literal keeps the Hebrew words; Balanced and Readable name the builder's plumb weight, as the English "plumb stone" does (footnoted in the meaning decision). It is kept apart from Amos's "다림줄" (plumb line), which is a different word.
- [ ] [ZEC 5:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/5/#v1) L/B/R · register · Narration in the plain written style with "나" for Zechariah ("눈을 들어 보니", as 2:1); the angel speaks to him in 해라체 and he answers in 합쇼체 — As the term sheet's address forms for the angel and Zechariah (Lamentations-Malachi terms, Korean section 3), as recorded at 1:9 for the whole book.
- [ ] [ZEC 6:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/6/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "내 주여" for the angel at every level — As the term sheet: Zechariah addresses the angel as "내 주여" (adoni), never "주님", which is kept for God; as recorded at 1:9 for the whole book.
- [ ] [ZEC 7:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/7/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "70년" in figures at every level, as in 1:12 — These are Jeremiah's seventy years of exile, which are written in figures at every level as a set phrase (1:12; 2 Chronicles 36:21), an exception to the rule that counts under a hundred in running text are in words (term sheet, numbers).
- [ ] [ZEC 11:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/11/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · '은총' and '연합' as the staffs' names, in inner quotation marks — The English capitalizes Favor and Union as names; Korean marks them with single quotation marks. "은총" keeps the sense of favor and grace; "연합" the binding together of verse 14.
- [ ] [ZEC 11:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/11/#v12) L/B/R · register · The shepherd speaks to his employers in 하오체 with "여러분" — As Ezekiel speaks to the people in his own voice (term sheet section 3): neither the 합쇼체 of a servant nor the 해라체 God uses.
- [ ] [ZEC 12:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/12/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "족장들" (L, B); "집안들" (R) — 'Alluf here means Judah's clans and their leaders (meaning decision); Literal and Balanced name the leaders who speak, Readable the family groups, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [ZEC 13:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/13/#v5) L/B/R · register · The former prophet and his questioner speak 하오체 ("나는 예언자가 아니오", "당신") — Two adults speaking guardedly as equals; the line still echoes Amos 7:14 (meaning decision), where the Korean has 해라체 "나는 예언자가 아니다" in Amos's answer to Amaziah.
- [ ] [ZEC 13:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/13/#v8) L/B/R · word_order · "{{divine_name:주|야훼}}의 선언이다" and "…가 말한다" kept inside the first line — In this poetic line the formula stands between the setting and the sentence, as in the Hebrew and the English; Korean marks it off with commas.
- [ ] [ZEC 14:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/zec/14/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "아살" for Azal — An otherwise unknown place (meaning decision); written from the Hebrew 'Atsal so that it is not confused with the tribe of Asher, "아셀".

## 말라기 (3)

- [ ] [MAL 1:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mal/1/#v14) L/B/R · word_order · The curse placed after its relative clauses at Literal, so the first two Hebrew lines become one Korean line there — Korean puts the relative clause before "사기꾼", so the English line break after "flock" cannot fall in the same place at Literal; B and R keep two lines. "자기" and "그" need no gender span.
- [ ] [MAL 2:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mal/2/#v15) L · word_choice · "그 하나" without the honorific (L); "그분께서" (B), "하나님께서" (R) — The Hebrew is very hard (meaning decision): Literal leaves "one" open, as the English Literal does without a deity span, while B and R follow the reading that God made husband and wife one.
- [ ] [MAL 2:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mal/2/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "이혼을 미워한다" with no subject (L); "나는 이혼을 미워한다" (B, R) — The term sheet's "나는 이혼을 미워한다". The Hebrew has "he hates", which the English Literal supplies as "[he]"; Korean can leave the subject unsaid, so Literal keeps the Hebrew open without bracketing a pronoun, and the meaning decision footnotes the other readings.

## 마태복음 (20)

- [ ] [MAT 10:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/10/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "평안" for the greeting's peace, through 10:12-13 — The greeting of peace is a blessing of God's wholeness spoken over a house (meaning decision), the peace given as a gift, so it takes the key term "평안", as in the Korean Luke 10:5-6 and John 20:19. "평화" is kept for peace between people (10:34).
- [ ] [MAT 10:28](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/10/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · "영혼" for psyche here — The style guide's word for psyche is "목숨", but here the word is set against the body and means the self that lives on beyond bodily death (meaning decision); "목숨" would say the soul can be killed by no one, which loses the contrast. 10:39 keeps "목숨".
- [ ] [MAT 11:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/11/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "폭행을 당하다" (L), "폭력에 시달리다" (B), "공격을 받다" (R); "빼앗다" — The meaning decision (footnoted) reads the verb as passive: the kingdom suffers violence from violent people. "침노" is the word of older versions and is no longer understood; the reading that the kingdom forcefully advances is the alternative in the footnote.
- [ ] [MAT 13:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/13/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "가라지" (L, B); "잡초" (R), through 13:24-40 — The weed is darnel, a grass that looks like wheat until it forms heads (meaning decision). "가라지" is the ordinary Korean name for a weed grass among grain and is how Korean readers know this parable; "독보리" names the plant exactly but is unfamiliar. Readable says plainly "weeds". A reviewer may …
- [ ] [MAT 14:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/14/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "분봉왕 헤롯" (L, B); "갈릴리를 다스리던 헤롯" (R) — Herod Antipas ruled Galilee and Perea as tetrarch (meaning decision); "분봉왕" is the established Korean title for a ruler of part of a kingdom, and Readable says what he ruled, as the English Readable does. A reviewer may prefer a plainer modern title.
