- [ ] [MAT 17:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/17/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "뇌전증" (L); "발작을 일으키다" (B, R) — The Greek word means "moonstruck", the ancient name for an illness with seizures (meaning decision, with footnote). "뇌전증" is the current Korean name; the older "간질" is now avoided as stigmatizing. Balanced and Readable describe the seizures.
- [ ] [MAT 19:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/19/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "고자" for eunouchos; Readable says what it means and that the third kind chose not to marry — Eunuchs are men unable to father children, from birth or by castration (meaning decision); "고자" is the plain Korean word for such a man, where "내시" is a court official. It can also be used as an insult, so a native reviewer should judge it.
- [ ] [MAT 21:43](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/21/#v43) L/B/R · word_choice · God as the one who takes and gives (L, R: "빼앗아... 주실 것이다"); passives at Balanced — The Greek passives mean that God acts; Korean passives of these verbs are heavy, so Literal and Readable use the active with the honorific for God, and Balanced keeps the passive form.
- [ ] [MAT 22:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/22/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "친구여" — A polite address that carries a note of reproach here (meaning decision); "친구여" is a dignified address that can carry that edge; "여보게" is a reviewer's alternative. The same address comes back in 20:13 and 26:50.
- [ ] [MAT 23:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/23/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "성구함" (L, B), described at Readable; "옷술" (L, B), "겉옷 귀퉁이에 다는 술" (R) — Small leather cases holding passages of the law (meaning decision); "성구함" says what they are, where "경문" is the older church word. The tassels as in the Korean Matthew 9:20.
- [ ] [MAT 23:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/23/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "십일조" (L, B), "십분의 일" (R); "박하", "딜", "커민" for the three herbs — The tithe as in the Korean Luke 11:42. The herbs are given their plain modern names; the older church words name other plants or are no longer understood.
- [ ] [MAT 24:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/24/#v15) L · word_choice · "다니엘을 통하여 말씀하신" (L); "다니엘이 말한" (B) — The Greek "spoken through Daniel" implies that God spoke by the prophet; Literal keeps "through" and so honours the unnamed speaker, God. Balanced and Readable name Daniel as the speaker, as the English does.
- [ ] [MAT 24:40](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/24/#v40) L · word_choice · "데려가지고 ... 남겨진다" (L), present passives — Literal keeps the Greek present passive for the future event (meaning decision). Balanced uses active verbs with no named subject, which Korean prefers, and leaves who takes them unsaid, as the Greek does.
- [ ] [MAT 24:42](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/24/#v42) L/B/R · register · "너희의 주" (L), "너희 주" (B, R), with no honorific ("오는지", "올지") — The Lord is Jesus himself (meaning decision); he speaks of himself, so Korean gives no honorific, as with "인자". "주" rather than "주인" keeps the title.
- [ ] [MAT 25:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/25/#v11) L · word_choice · "우리" (L, B); "저희" (R) — Readable uses the humble "저희" of people pleading with a superior; Literal and Balanced keep the plain "우리" of the Greek and the earlier dialogue.
- [ ] [MAT 25:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/25/#v14) B/R · setting_alternative · {{units:은 다섯 자루|다섯 달란트}} (B, R); "다섯 달란트" (L); the wage value explained once (R) — The amounts recur through the parable, so the default says "sacks of silver", as the English says "bags of silver", and Readable gives the worth once (meaning decision: about twenty years' wages). Both forms end in a vowel, so "를" fits either; the later counts "둘", "하나" need no span.
- [ ] [MAT 25:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/25/#v21) R · word_choice · "나와 함께 기뻐하자" (R) — The master's own joy, which the servant shares (meaning decision); Readable lets the master say it in the first person, since Korean "your master's joy" from the master himself sounds stiff in plain speech.
- [ ] [MAT 25:41](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/25/#v41) B/R · word_choice · "그의 천사들" (L); "그 부하들" (B, R) for the devil's angels — Korean "천사" names God's angels; Balanced and Readable say the devil's messengers are his underlings, so no reader mistakes them for God's. The English gives the devil's "his" no span.
- [ ] [MAT 26:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/26/#v14) L/B/R · register · Judas speaks to the chief priests in 합쇼체 with "당신들"; "그를" (L), "예수를" (B, R) with no honorific — A man bargaining with the authorities speaks politely but as an equal in a deal; he names Jesus plainly, without honour. The English "him" has a deity_pronoun span, which Korean does not carry.
- [ ] [MAT 26:74](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mat/26/#v74) R · word_choice · "내가 거짓말을 한다면 하나님께 벌을 받겠다" (R) — Peter most likely calls a curse down on himself if he lies (meaning decision, footnoted); Readable spells it out as a Korean oath, as the Korean Mark 14:71 does.

## 마가복음 (11)

- [ ] [MRK 1:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/1/#v23) L/B/R · register · the unclean spirit speaks to Jesus in 합쇼체, with "당신", "나" / "우리" for itself and no honorific -시- or "께" for him, through Mark (1:24; 3:11; 5:7-12) — The style guide gives 합쇼체 for those who speak to Jesus and allows 해라체 for open opponents. The spirit both resists Jesus and fears him; formal endings with the cool "당신" and no honorific -시- keep that distance, as the envoys' questioning does in John 1:19-25. Every unclean spirit or demon that …
- [ ] [MRK 3:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/3/#v11) L/B/R · register · The spirits address Jesus in 합쇼체 with "당신" and no honorific -시- ("아들입니다"), as in 1:24 — The spirits bow and name him truly, so they speak up to him in formal endings; "당신" keeps the stressed "you" of the Greek, and leaving out the honorific -시- keeps their distance, as at 1:24 and 5:7. A reviewer may prefer the plain style for hostile spirits.
- [ ] [MRK 5:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/5/#v6) L/B/R · register · The spirit speaks to Jesus in 합쇼체 with "당신", the address "예수여" and no honorific -시- or "께" for him, and of itself as "나" — As in the Korean Mark 1:24 and 3:11, the spirit bows and names Jesus truly, so it speaks up to him in formal endings; "당신" keeps the stressed "you" of the Greek, "예수여" (as at 1:24) and the missing -시- keep its distance, and "나" (not the humble "저") keeps its defiance. A reviewer may prefer another …
- [ ] [MRK 6:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/6/#v24) L/B/R · register · The girl speaks to her mother in 해요체 ("할까요?"); her mother answers in 해라체 — A daughter asking her mother at home uses the polite informal style in Korean; 합쇼체 would sound like a servant. A reviewer may prefer another level.
- [ ] [MRK 9:49](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/9/#v49) L/B/R · word_choice · "소금 쳐지다" (L); "소금 침을 받다" (B, R) — The saying is obscure (meaning decision, footnoted) and every level keeps its picture of salting as a sacrifice is salted; Korean says to salt with "소금을 치다", and the passive is made with "받다" at Balanced and Readable.
- [ ] [MRK 10:49](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/10/#v49) L/B/R · register · the crowd speaks to Bartimaeus in 해라체 (L) and 하오체 with "당신" (B, R), honouring Jesus with "부르신다" — Strangers addressing a grown man would not use the plain style of a parent to a child; Balanced and Readable use the plain-polite 하오체 that Korean uses between adults in such a scene. Literal keeps the bare imperatives. A native reviewer may prefer another level.
- [ ] [MRK 13:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/13/#v14) L/B · word_choice · "자기가 서서는 안 될 곳에" (L, B) — The Greek participle is masculine, pointing to a person (meaning decision). Korean has no grammatical gender, so Literal and Balanced add "자기가", which suits a person, while the noun "가증한 것" stays as Daniel's word.
- [ ] [MRK 14:71](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/14/#v71) R · word_choice · "내가 거짓말을 한다면 하나님께 벌을 받겠다" (R) — Peter most likely calls a curse down on himself if he lies (meaning decision, footnoted); Readable spells it out as a Korean oath, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [MRK 14:72](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/14/#v72) L · word_choice · "북받쳐서 울고 있었다" (L) — The Greek participle is uncertain (meaning decision, footnoted); "북받치다" (to be overcome) gives the reading that he broke down, and the "-고 있었다" keeps the continuing weeping of the imperfect.
- [ ] [MRK 15:39](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/15/#v39) L/B/R · register · "이 사람은 ... 이었다" (L); "이분은 ... 이셨다" (B, R) — Literal keeps the Greek "this man"; in Balanced and Readable the centurion's confession of who Jesus is would sound grudging in Korean without the honorific. A reviewer may prefer to keep the soldier's plain words.
- [ ] [MRK 16:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/mrk/16/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "살아나셨다" (L, B); "하나님께서 그분을 ... 다시 살리셨다" (R) — The Greek passive means that God raised him (meaning decision); Korean has no natural passive of "raise", so Literal and Balanced use the usual intransitive verb, and Readable names God as the English Readable does.

## 누가복음 (20)

- [ ] [LUK 1:28](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/1/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · "기뻐하여라" for the greeting, at every level; "은혜" for the favor shown — The greeting is ordinary but carries the sense "rejoice" (meaning decision). Korean greetings ("안녕") carry nothing of it, and an angel greeting with "안녕하냐" would sound casual, so every level keeps "기뻐하여라", which still works as an opening call. The verb is built on the word for grace, so "은혜" keeps …
- [ ] [LUK 1:41](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/1/#v41) L/B/R · register · Elizabeth speaks to Mary in 해라체 ("너", "받았다"), but honours her as "내 주님의 어머니" with the honorific ("오시다니", 1:43) — Elizabeth is the older relative, and an older Korean woman speaks to a young kinswoman in the plain style; the honorific for "the mother of my Lord" shows the reverence the Greek expresses (meaning decision on 1:43). A reviewer may prefer the polite style for the whole greeting.
- [ ] [LUK 2:34](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/2/#v34) L/B/R · register · Simeon speaks to Mary in 해라체 ("보라", "네") — An old man speaking as a prophet to a young mother uses the plain style in Korean, as Elizabeth does in 1:42-45. A reviewer may prefer a softer style.
- [ ] [LUK 4:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/4/#v3) L/B/R · register · The devil speaks to Jesus in 해라체 with "너" ("네가", "말하여라"), through 4:3-12 — The devil is Jesus's open opponent, testing and challenging him, and the speech-level rule allows open opponents to speak without honorifics. The demons of 4:34 and 4:41 are different: they are afraid and acknowledge who he is, so they keep 합쇼체.
- [ ] [LUK 4:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/4/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "시리아 사람 나아만" — The modern name of the region is preferred where an older church form competes with it (Korean style guide, Settled); a reviewer may prefer "수리아".
- [ ] [LUK 5:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/5/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "선생님" for epistata (Master), through Luke — Korean has no common word that keeps the shade of one who gives orders; "선생님" is how disciples address Jesus in Korean Mark, and "주님" is kept for kyrie (5:8). "주인님" would suggest a slave's master.
- [ ] [LUK 5:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/5/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "사람아" (L); "친구여" (B, R) for anthrōpe — Korean has no neutral address that matches the plain, kind Greek word; "사람아" shows the Greek at Literal, "친구여" gives its warmth. A native reviewer should choose the most natural form.
- [ ] [LUK 8:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/8/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "가졌다고 생각하는 것" (L, R); "가진 줄로 여기는 것" (B) for "what he seems to have" — The verb can mean "seems" or "thinks" (meaning decision); Korean "생각하다" and "여기다" hold both the person's own view and the appearance better than "보이다", which names only the outward look.
- [ ] [LUK 8:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/8/#v31) L/B/R · word_choice · "깊은 구렁" (L, B); "악한 영들을 가두어 두는 깊은 구덩이" (R) for abyssos — "무저갱" is the older church word of familiar versions and is not current Korean; "깊은 구렁" says a deep pit plainly. Readable says what the place is for (meaning decision).
- [ ] [LUK 8:50](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/8/#v50) R · word_choice · "다시 살아날 것이다" (R) — The girl is reported dead, and the meaning decision reads "saved" as rescued from death; "낫다" alone would sound odd of one who has died.
- [ ] [LUK 9:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/9/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "떠나가심" (L, B); "이 세상을 떠나실 일" (R) for exodos — "떠나가심" keeps the picture of a departure that the meaning decision describes; "출애굽" names only the event in Exodus, and "별세" is the wording of older Korean Bibles and says only death.
- [ ] [LUK 10:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/10/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "평안" for eirēnē at every level, in 10:5-6 — "평안" is the key term for peace in the Korean John and Luke 1-8 (style guide, section 6), and of the same instruction in the Korean Matthew 10:12-13; the greeting is in 합쇼체, the polite form to a household.
- [ ] [LUK 10:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/10/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "하데스" (L); "죽은 사람들의 세계" (B); "죽은 사람들이 가는 곳" (R) — Hades is worded as in the Korean Matthew 11:23 and 16:18: Literal keeps the name, Balanced and Readable say what it is, the realm of the dead (meaning decision). "음부" is the wording of older Korean Bibles and no longer understood.
- [ ] [LUK 10:35](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/10/#v35) L/B/R · register · The Samaritan speaks to the innkeeper in 하오체 ("주시오", "드리겠소") — A traveller dealing with an innkeeper, a stranger and no superior: the plain polite style of literary Korean fits better than either deference or plain speech.
- [ ] [LUK 10:39](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/10/#v39) L/B/R · word_choice · "동생" for adelphē — Korean must say whether a sister is older or younger; the text does not say, but Martha acts as the head of the house, so Mary is taken as the younger.
- [ ] [LUK 14:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/14/#v8) L/B/R · register · The host speaks to his guest in 하게체 ('내어 드리게', 14:10 '올라오게'); Jesus speaks to the listener as "너" in 해라체 — The plain style would be rude from a host to a guest and 합쇼체 too stiff for "Friend" in 14:10; 하게체 is the respectful but familiar speech of a senior host to an adult guest.
- [ ] [LUK 16:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/16/#v5) L/B/R · register · The manager speaks to the debtors in 하오체 with "당신" ("빚졌소", "쓰시오"); they answer in 합쇼체 ("백 바트입니다") — The debtors are tenants or traders, not his servants (meaning decision); the plain-polite 하오체 suits a steward dealing with grown men he wants to win over, and they answer the master's steward politely.
- [ ] [LUK 18:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/18/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "자기를 향하여" (L); "자기 자신에 대해" (B); "자기 자랑을 늘어놓으며" (R) — The meaning decision (footnoted) reads the phrase as praying about himself; Literal keeps the Greek's "toward himself", which can also be read as "to himself".
- [ ] [LUK 18:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/18/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "형제" with no gender span — The English has no span here; the meaning decision allows brothers and sisters, but in a list of family members Korean "형제" may be read either way.
- [ ] [LUK 22:70](https://openwordbible.org/ko/luk/22/#v70) L/B/R · word_choice · "내가 그라고" for "that I am"; the stress on "너희" kept at every level — The answer is a yes, but in their words (meaning decision, footnoted); Korean needs a predicate, and "그" is the word of the Korean Mark 14:62 "내가 그다". The sentence leaves the yes in their mouth, as the Greek does.

## 요한복음 (49)

- [ ] [JHN 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/2/#v3) B/R · register · His mother speaks to him in the plain style a Korean mother uses to a grown son ("-구나") — The style guide sets the formal polite style for people speaking to Jesus, but that would make his mother sound like a stranger. A mother naturally uses the plain style to her son; she still speaks to him with the honorific dative "께" in the narrative. A reviewer may prefer a softer polite ending.
- [ ] [JHN 2:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/2/#v4) L/B/R · register · Jesus speaks to his mother in the polite style ("-나요", "-세요") and calls himself "저" (B, R) — The plain style Jesus uses to others would be disrespectful from a son to his mother in Korean, and the Greek address is courteous. The polite style keeps the courtesy while the words still decline her request.
- [ ] [JHN 2:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/2/#v18) L/B/R · register · The leaders speak to Jesus in the formal style but with "당신" and no honorific "-시-" — They are challenging him, so the respectful "-십니까" that people use to Jesus elsewhere does not fit; open contempt ("-느냐") would overstate a demand that is not yet hostile. "당신" with "-습니까" is formal and cool.
- [ ] [JHN 3:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/3/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "위로부터 태어나다"; "위로부터 새로 태어나다" (R) — No Korean word means both "from above" and "again" as the Greek does. "위로부터" keeps Jesus's primary sense (birth from God); the familiar "거듭나다" says only "again", the sense Nicodemus hears in 3:4. Readable adds "새로" so the second sense is not lost; the footnote explains the play.
- [ ] [JHN 3:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/3/#v10) L/B/R · register · Jesus speaks to Nicodemus in the plain style ("너", "-느냐") — The style guide gives Jesus the plain style toward everyone he teaches. Nicodemus is an older leader, and a reviewer may feel the plain style is too blunt for Korean readers; it also matches the gentle rebuke in this verse.
- [ ] [JHN 4:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/4/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · "예언자" — "예언자" is the current word that new readers understand; "선지자" is the older Protestant term and may be preferred by a reviewer. The guide does not settle it.
- [ ] [JHN 4:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/4/#v21) L/B/R · register · "여자여" (L); "여인이여" (B); "부인" (R) — The Greek address is respectful. Literal keeps the word; Balanced uses the more courteous "여인이여" as the guide sets, and Readable "부인", the ordinary polite way to address a grown woman.
- [ ] [JHN 4:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/4/#v29) L/B/R · register · the woman addresses the townspeople in 합쇼체 ("보십시오", "제가", "저에게") — She speaks to the people of the town as a group, and 합쇼체 is the polite public address; the humble "저" fits a woman of low standing speaking to them.
- [ ] [JHN 4:41](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/4/#v41) L/B/R · word_choice · "구원자" — "구원자" says plainly "the one who saves" and is understood by new readers; "구주" is the familiar church word and "구세주" is common in everyday speech. A reviewer may prefer either.
- [ ] [JHN 4:44](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/4/#v44) L/B/R · word_choice · "예언자" — The current word, as in 4:19; "선지자" is the older Protestant term.
- [ ] [JHN 5:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/5/#v19) L/B/R · register · honorifics for the Father ("아버지께서 하시는"), none for the Son in Jesus's own words ("아들도 ... 한다"); no deity_pronoun spans — Jesus is speaking of himself as "the Son", and a Korean speaker does not use the honorific of himself; the Father receives it throughout. Korean has no capital letters, so the deity_pronoun setting has nothing to change and the Korean text has no such spans in this passage.
- [ ] [JHN 6:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/6/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "빵" for "artos" — "떡" is the traditional church word for bread in older Korean Bibles, but to modern readers it means rice cake; "빵" is what the barley loaves were. This holds for the whole chapter.
- [ ] [JHN 6:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/6/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "그 예언자" — "예언자" is the current word; "선지자" is the older Protestant one. "그" marks the expected Prophet, as the capital does in English.
- [ ] [JHN 6:63](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/6/#v63) L/B/R · word_choice · "성령" for the Spirit, "영" in "words are spirit" — The meaning decision reads the first as the Spirit of God, for which the style guide uses "성령" with honorifics; Korean then cannot repeat one word as the Greek does, and the second keeps "영". The link between them is in the meaning decision's footnote.
- [ ] [JHN 6:71](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/6/#v71) L/B/R · word_choice · "가룟 시몬의 [아들] 유다" (L); "가룟 사람 시몬의 아들 유다" (B, R) — The SBLGNT makes "Iscariot" describe Simon (meaning decision), so Korean attaches "가룟" to Simon rather than writing the familiar "가룟 유다"; "가룟 사람" says it is a place name (man of Kerioth). Literal brackets "아들", which the Greek leaves understood.
- [ ] [JHN 7:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/7/#v3) L/B/R · register · Plain style (해라체: "가거라", "해라") from the brothers to Jesus — People speaking to Jesus normally use the formal polite style, but these are his own brothers, who do not believe in him (verse 5) and speak with a challenge. Family members speaking among themselves would not use 합쇼체. A reviewer should judge this.
- [ ] [JHN 7:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/7/#v20) L/B/R · register · The crowd speaks to Jesus in the plain style ("너", "말이냐") — The style guide gives 합쇼체 to people speaking to Jesus, but allows plain speech where the scene is hostile. Here the crowd retorts "You have a demon!", a rude outburst; formal politeness would contradict it. A reviewer should judge the level.
- [ ] [JHN 7:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/7/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "때" for hour (hora) at every level — "그분의 때" is the natural Korean for an appointed time and keeps the thread of Jesus's "hour" through the book. Korean uses the same word for "my time" (kairos) in 7:6 and 7:8, so the two Greek words are not told apart.
- [ ] [JHN 7:38](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/7/#v38) L/B/R · word_choice · "살아 있는 물" (L); "생수" (B); "생명을 주는 물" (R) — "생수" is the familiar Bible word, but in everyday Korean it now means bottled mineral water. Literal keeps the image of living, flowing water; Balanced keeps the familiar word; Readable says what the water does.
- [ ] [JHN 7:40](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/7/#v40) L/B/R · word_choice · "그 예언자" — "예언자" is the current everyday word; "선지자" is the older church word many readers know. "그" marks the one promised prophet, as the article does in Greek; Korean has no capital to do it.
- [ ] [JHN 7:51](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/7/#v51) L/B/R · register · Nicodemus speaks to his fellow council members in the plain style (해라체) — Among equals the style guide's default is 해라체; the council answers him in the same level (7:52).
- [ ] [JHN 7:53](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/7/#v53) L/B/R · word_choice · "올리브 산" — "감람산" is the traditional Protestant name, but "감람" is properly a different tree and the word is unknown outside the church; "올리브 산" names the place plainly. A reviewer should decide whether to keep the familiar name.
- [ ] [JHN 8:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/8/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "율법학자들" (L, B); "율법 선생들" (R) — "서기관" is the traditional Protestant word but suggests a clerk to a modern reader; these men were experts who taught and applied the Law of Moses. The guide does not list the word; a reviewer should confirm it for the whole book.
- [ ] [JHN 8:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/8/#v13) L/B/R · register · The Pharisees speak to Jesus in the plain style ("너", "참되지 않다") — The style guide allows open opponents to speak to Jesus without honorifics where the scene is hostile; here they reject his testimony to his face. This holds through the dispute in this passage.
- [ ] [JHN 8:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/8/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "내가 나임을" (that I am), with no "he" supplied (also 8:28) — Korean must give "I am" a complement. "내가 나임을" (that I am I) supplies nothing from outside and echoes God's self-naming "나는 나다" (Exodus 3:14) as the meaning decision explains; "그" (he) would turn it into an ordinary claim. It reads oddly at first, which is part of its force. A native reviewer …
- [ ] [JHN 8:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/8/#v25) L/B/R · register · The hostile hearers speak to Jesus in the plain style ("너는 누구냐") — The style guide allows open opponents to speak to Jesus without honorifics where the scene is hostile; the emphatic "you" is a challenge.
- [ ] [JHN 8:28](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/8/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · "내가 나임을" as in 8:24 — The same absolute "I am" as in 8:24, rendered the same way so the two sayings stay linked.
- [ ] [JHN 8:33](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/8/#v33) L/B/R · register · These hearers still address Jesus in the formal style (합쇼체: -습니다, -십니까) in 8:33-41 — They had just come to believe him (8:30-31), so their first replies stay formal even as they bristle. When the exchange turns to open insult in 8:48, they drop the honorifics.
- [ ] [JHN 8:48](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/8/#v48) L/B/R · register · The hearers now speak to Jesus in the plain style without honorifics ("너", -느냐) through 8:59 — The style guide allows open opponents to speak to Jesus without honorifics where the scene is hostile. Here they call him a Samaritan and demon-possessed, and in 8:59 they take up stones; the drop from the formal speech of 8:33-41 marks the turn. The narrator keeps the honorific for Jesus (그분께).
- [ ] [JHN 8:52](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/8/#v52) L/B/R · word_choice · "예언자" for prophētēs — As through the book (docs/korean-style.md): "예언자" is the current word that new readers understand; "선지자" is the established older Protestant term, and a reviewer may prefer it for the whole book.
- [ ] [JHN 9:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/9/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "예언자" for prophētēs — As through the book (docs/korean-style.md): "예언자" is the current word that new readers understand; "선지자" is the established older Protestant term, and a reviewer may prefer it for the whole book.
- [ ] [JHN 9:40](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/9/#v40) L/B/R · register · The Pharisees speak to Jesus in the plain style without honorifics (-느냐) — The style guide allows open opponents to address Jesus without honorifics where the scene is hostile; these are the men who have just thrown out the man Jesus healed. The narrator keeps the honorific (그분께).
- [ ] [JHN 11:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/11/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "자매" (sister) for Martha, not "언니" or "동생" — Korean names a sibling by relative age, which the text does not give. The neutral "자매" avoids guessing; a reviewer may prefer the traditional assumption that Martha was the elder.
- [ ] [JHN 11:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/11/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "형제" (brother) for Lazarus, not "오빠" or "남동생" — Korean words for a woman's brother say whether he is older or younger, which the text does not tell. "형제" stays neutral; a reviewer may prefer "오빠" as more natural, especially at Readable.
- [ ] [JHN 12:36](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/12/#v36) L/B · setting_alternative · 빛의 자녀, with no gender span — 자녀 is the plain inclusive Korean word for sons and daughters, and the guide limits gender spans to 형제자매|형제; a reviewer may prefer a span with 아들들.
- [ ] [JHN 12:37](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/12/#v37) L/B/R · word_choice · 예언자 for "prophet" — 예언자 is the current standard word; 선지자 is the older church term that many Protestant readers know, and a reviewer may prefer it.
- [ ] [JHN 16:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/16/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · 진실 (L, B); 사실 (R) for "the truth" here — Here "the truth" is an assurance that what Jesus says is so, not the key term for God's truth; 진리 would sound like a teaching about truth. 진리 stays for the key term (verse 13).
- [ ] [JHN 16:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/16/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · 괴로움 (L, B); 아프다 (R) for lypē here — The Greek uses the same word as the disciples' sorrow, but Korean 슬픔 does not fit the pain of labour; the link between the two is lost, as the meaning note explains.
- [ ] [JHN 18:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/18/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · 내가 [그 사람]이다 (L); 내가 그 사람이다 (B); 내가 바로 그 사람이다 (R) for egō eimi, also in verses 6 and 8 — Korean must say who "I am" is, so the plain sense the English follows is given; the echo of God's name cannot be kept in Korean wording and is left to the meaning note and its footnote.
- [ ] [JHN 18:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/18/#v29) L/B/R · register · Pilate speaks to the accusers in the plain style (해라체: "고발하느냐") — Pilate is the Roman governor and the judge; the style guide's default to those below one is 해라체. The same through John 18-19.
- [ ] [JHN 18:33](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/18/#v33) L/B/R · register · Pilate speaks to Jesus in the plain style (해라체: "네가 ... 이냐?") — The judge questions a prisoner whom he treats as beneath him; the narrator still uses honorifics for Jesus around the speech. A reviewer may prefer the older 하오체 for the governor. The same through John 18-19.
- [ ] [JHN 18:34](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/18/#v34) L/B/R · register · Jesus answers Pilate in the plain style (해라체: "네가 ... 하느냐") — The style guide gives Jesus 해라체 to everyone except the Father (section 4); a prisoner answering the governor so shows the authority John gives him in the trial. A reviewer may prefer 하오체 here. The same through John 18-19.
- [ ] [JHN 18:40](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/18/#v40) L/B/R · register · the crowd's shouted demands to Pilate in 하오체 ("바라바요", "풀어 주시오"); their spoken arguments in 합쇼체 — A shouting crowd in 합쇼체 sounds too polite, and 해라체 would make them address the governor as an inferior; 하오체 keeps a blunt, public respect. The same for the shouts in 19:6 and 19:15; the argument of 19:12 stays in 합쇼체.
- [ ] [JHN 19:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/19/#v8) L/B/R · register · Pilate speaks to Jesus in the plain style (해라체: "너는 ... 왔느냐?"), as in 18:33-38 — The judge keeps the form he used with the prisoner in chapter 18; his growing fear shows in the narrative, not in a change of speech level. A reviewer may prefer 하오체.
- [ ] [JHN 19:35](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/19/#v35) L/B/R · register · "너희" (L); "여러분" (B, R) for the narrator's "you" addressed to the readers — Here the Gospel turns from the story to its readers. "여러분" is how modern Korean writing addresses its readers; "너희" in the plain style sounds as if the writer were talking down to them. Literal keeps the plain pronoun.
- [ ] [JHN 20:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/20/#v2) L/B/R · register · Mary speaks to the disciples in the polite style (해요체: "옮겨 갔어요", "몰라요"); to the angels and the supposed gardener in 합쇼체 — The style guide leaves speech among equals to the scene; a woman speaking to fellow disciples, men she follows Jesus with, would naturally use 해요체, while strangers get the formal style. Jesus speaks to her in 해라체.
- [ ] [JHN 21:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/21/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "얘들아" at every level — "얘들아" is the warm, familiar call an older man gives a group of younger men, close to the Greek "children" as the meaning decision reads it; "친구들" sounds translated and "여보게들" is old-fashioned. A reviewer should judge whether it sounds too much like speaking to boys.
- [ ] [JHN 21:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/21/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "사랑하다" for both Greek verbs (agapaō and phileō) throughout 21:15-17, at every level — As docs/john-terms.md and the meaning decision: John uses both verbs of the Father's love for the Son, so the change is most likely style. Any second Korean verb would be weaker than "사랑하다" and would make Peter's answers sound like a step down, which the text need not mean. Some readers hold that a …
- [ ] [JHN 21:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/jhn/21/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "사랑하다" again, though Jesus now uses Peter's verb (phileō) — Follows the decision at 21:15: both verbs are "사랑하다". Whether the change of verb carries meaning is uncertain; see the footnote at 21:15.

## 사도행전 (2)

- [ ] [ACT 18:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/act/18/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "총독" for the proconsul (anthypatos), at every level, with "로마의" added at Readable; "재판석" (L, B), "재판정" (R) for the judgment seat — The Greek uses a different title for the governor of a senatorial province such as Achaia than for Felix and Festus (meaning decision), but Korean has one current word for a Roman governor of either kind; this decision says the title is a proconsul's. "갈리오" and "아가야" are the Protestant forms. …
- [ ] [ACT 28:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/act/28/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "멜리데" for Malta, with "몰타" added at Readable — "멜리데" is the Protestant form of the name (style guide, Settled), as "아덴" and "구브로"; Readable adds the island's current name so the reader can find it. Uncertain: a reviewer may prefer "몰타" at every level, since the current name is well known.

## 로마서 (12)

- [ ] [ROM 3:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/3/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "예수 {{christos:그리스도|메시아}}를 믿는 믿음" at every level — The meaning decision reads the phrase as faith that has Jesus as its object, and gives his own faithfulness as the other reading. Korean "예수 그리스도의 믿음" would be read as Jesus' own faith, so every level says "... 를 믿는 믿음". The same holds in verse 26.
- [ ] [ROM 3:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/3/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "속죄의 제물" (L, B); "죄를 없애고 하나님의 진노를 돌이키는 제물" (R) — The meaning decision gives the word as the means of atonement, the reality the mercy seat of the ark stood for, and says it can also mean an atoning sacrifice. Korean has no word that holds both: "속죄소" names the lid of the ark and would not be understood here, and "화목제물", the older church word, is …
- [ ] [ROM 7:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/7/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "법" (L, B); "법칙" (R) for the law the speaker finds at work — The meaning decision reads this "law" as a rule or pattern at work in the speaker, not the law of Moses, and footnotes the other reading. Korean "법" covers both without deciding, as in 3:27; "율법" would decide for the law of Moses. Readable says "법칙", a rule that always holds.
- [ ] [ROM 8:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/8/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "영" (L); "성령께서" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads this as God's Spirit, who is life, and footnotes the reading "your spirit is alive". Literal keeps the bare "영", which allows both; Balanced and Readable follow the meaning decision with "성령" and the honorific.
- [ ] [ROM 9:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/9/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "바로" for Pharaoh at every level; "이집트 왕" added (R) — "바로" is the established Protestant form of the name (style guide, "Settled"); "파라오" is the modern general form and an alternative for a reviewer. Readable says who he was. The quotation is God speaking, in 해라체 with "너" and "나".
- [ ] [ROM 9:32](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/9/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "그를" for "him" inside the quotation, without the honorific — The meaning decision says the stone is Christ, but within the quotation God speaks of the stone in 해라체, so the plain "그" is kept; Paul's own sentences about Christ keep the honorific.
- [ ] [ROM 10:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/10/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "그를" for "him" inside the quotation, without the honorific, as at 9:33 — The same Isaiah quotation as 9:33, rendered the same way: God speaks in the quotation in 해라체, so the plain "그" is kept, although the meaning decision says the one believed is Christ.
- [ ] [ROM 10:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/10/#v14) L/B/R · supplied_words · "[말씀을] 듣지 못한 분" (L); "그분의 말씀을 듣다" (B, R) — The Greek says they have not heard him: the meaning decision reads this as Christ heard speaking in the message, and footnotes the reading "heard of him". Korean cannot say "hear him" without an object, so it supplies "말씀", which keeps Christ as the speaker.
- [ ] [ROM 11:25](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/11/#v25) L/B/R · word_choice · "구원자" with plain verbs inside the quotation ("와서 ... 없앨 것이다") — The meaning decision says the Deliverer is most likely Christ, and footnotes the other view. As with the quotation at 9:33 and 10:11, the words of Scripture keep the plain 해라체 and do not decide the question with an honorific.
- [ ] [ROM 12:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/12/#v20) R · supplied_words · "그런 친절을 받으면 그는 부끄러워하고 뉘우치게 될지도 모릅니다" (R) — The meaning decision says the coals are a painful shame that may lead the enemy to change, and footnotes the other reading; Readable explains the picture as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [ROM 15:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/15/#v13) L · word_choice · "믿음 안에서" (L) for the verb "believing" — Korean has no verbal noun as short as the Greek; a reviewer may prefer "믿는 가운데", which keeps the verb.
- [ ] [ROM 15:23](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rom/15/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "서바나" at every level, here and in verse 28 — The places of Paul's journeys take their traditional Protestant forms through the Korean New Testament, so they read as one map: "서바나" here, with "마게도냐", "아가야" (15:26), "일루리곤" (15:19) and "겐그레아" (16:1), as Mark keeps "이두매", "두로" and "데가볼리" (3:8; 7:31). "스페인", the country's current name, is the …

## 고린도전서 (54)

- [ ] [1CO 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · the whole letter in 합쇼체 (-습니다, -십시오) at every level; the readers are "여러분"; Paul speaks of himself as "나" and "우리"; God, Christ and the Holy Spirit take the … — As in Romans, Galatians and the other letters already drafted, a letter to a whole church, read aloud when it gathered, calls for the formal style of a letter and a public address, so every level uses 합쇼체 and its imperatives (-십시오), with "여러분" for the plural "you". Paul writes as an apostle with …
- [ ] [1CO 1:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/1/#v8) R · supplied_words · "하나님께서는" (R) for "who" — The meaning decision reads "who" as God, most likely; Literal and Balanced keep "그분" as the Greek's pronoun, and Readable names God, as the English Readable keeps "He" after God in verse 4.
- [ ] [1CO 1:30](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/1/#v30) L/B/R · word_choice · "주" (L); "주님" (B, R), with no divine_name span — In Jeremiah the word is the divine name; Paul's Greek has "Lord", and the meaning decision reads it here as Christ, so Korean says "주" in Literal, as Romans does in quotations, and "주님" at the other levels.
- [ ] [1CO 3:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/3/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "하나님의 동역자들" (L); "하나님을 섬기며 함께 일하는 사람들" (B); "하나님을 위해 함께 일하는 일꾼들" (R) — The meaning decision leaves open whether Paul and Apollos work with God or together for God; Literal keeps the Greek's "God's fellow workers", which can bear both, and Balanced and Readable follow the English.
- [ ] [1CO 4:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/4/#v6) L/B/R · punctuation · the saying in quotation marks, in the plain style of a proverb ("넘어서지 말라") — The meaning decision reads the words as a saying Paul quotes, most likely about keeping within Scripture.
- [ ] [1CO 5:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/5/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "사탄에게 넘겨주다" at every level; "육신은 멸망하게 하다" (L, B); "그를 지배하는 죄의 욕망이 없어지게 하다" (R) — "사탄" is the name in the Gospels and Romans 16:20; "넘겨주다" the verb of Romans 1:24. The meaning decision reads "the flesh" as the man's sinful self, so Literal and Balanced keep "육신", the letter's word for the flesh, and Readable says what is meant, as the English does.
- [ ] [1CO 6:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/6/#v9) L/B/R · word_choice · two items at every level: "부드러운 [남자들] ... 남자와 동침하는 남자들" (L); "남자와의 성관계에서 수동적인 역할을 하는 남자들 ... 남자와 잠자리를 같이하는 남자들" (B); "다른 남자가 자기와 성관계를 하게 하는 남자들 ... 다른 남자와 성관계를 … — The project's choice for these verses keeps the two Greek words as two items, describing acts, never orientation, so Korean never says "동성애자". The older words "탐색", "남색" are not current Korean. The meaning decisions give the evidence and the debated alternatives.
- [ ] [1CO 7:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/7/#v1) L/B/R · punctuation · the saying in quotation marks, in the plain style of a slogan; Readable adds "여러분 가운데 어떤 사람은 ... 하고 말합니다" — The meaning decision reads the words as a saying from the Corinthians' letter, which Paul answers, as in 6:12-13.
- [ ] [1CO 7:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/7/#v4) R · word_choice · "혼자 결정하지 않습니다 ... 함께 결정합니다" (R) — The English Readable says "her husband is" in charge; Korean "남편이 주인이다" would sound one-sided against the meaning decision, which stresses that the claim runs both ways equally, so Readable says each decides together with the other.
- [ ] [1CO 7:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/7/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "결혼하지 않은 사람" for the unmarried at every level, here and in verses 11, 32 and 34 — The meaning decision notes that the word can include the widowed; "결혼하지 않은 사람" (a person not married) covers both, where "미혼자" means one never married.
- [ ] [1CO 7:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/7/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "거룩하게 되다" for the verb, the verb of 1:2 and 6:11; "깨끗하지 못하다" for unclean — The meaning decision reads the word as being set apart within the reach of God's holiness, not as being saved; Korean keeps the same verb as in 1:2 so the link is seen, and the meaning decision says what it means here.
- [ ] [1CO 7:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/7/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "종노릇 하다" (L); "얽매이다" (B); "결혼에 묶여 있다" (R) — Literal keeps the strong picture of the Greek, "has been enslaved", with the phrase Romans 6:6 (L) uses; Balanced and Readable give the sense. "형제나 자매" names both, with no gender span.
- [ ] [1CO 7:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/7/#v21) L · supplied_words · "[그 기회를] 이용하십시오" (L); "그 기회를 잘 살리십시오" (B); "그 기회를 잡으십시오" (R) — The Greek says only "rather use"; the meaning decision reads it as taking the opportunity to become free, with the other reading footnoted.
- [ ] [1CO 7:36](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/7/#v36) L/B/R · word_choice · "자기 처녀" (L); "약혼한 여자" (B); "약혼한 젊은 여자" (R), through verses 36-38 — The meaning decision reads the passage as a man and the woman he is engaged to, uncertain, with the reading of a father and his daughter footnoted; Literal keeps the Greek's open "his virgin".
- [ ] [1CO 7:36](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/7/#v36) L · word_choice · "한창때를 넘기다" (L); "마음이 강하다" (B); "마음이 너무 강하다" (R) — Literal keeps the Greek "beyond the peak"; the meaning decision reads it of the man's strong desire, with the reading of the woman's age footnoted, so Literal does not name an age.
- [ ] [1CO 9:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/9/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "삯" (L), "상" (B, R) for the wage, as in 3:8; "관리하는 직분" (L), "집안 관리인의 일" (B, R) for the stewardship, the word of 4:1-2 — The same words as in 3:8 and 4:1-2, so the links can be seen.
- [ ] [1CO 9:27](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/9/#v27) L/B/R · word_choice · "실격되다" (L, R); "실격자" (B) — The meaning decision reads the word as failing the test and so not receiving the prize, uncertain and footnoted; "실격" is the word of the games, where "버림을 받다" would decide that Paul speaks of losing salvation. The same word family returns in 2 Corinthians 13:5-7.
- [ ] [1CO 11:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/11/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "머리" at every level for the head, through verses 3-10; "남자" and "여자" for man and woman — The meaning decision reads "head" as the place of leadership, uncertain, with "source" footnoted; Korean "머리" is used of a leader, as the English "head" is, and keeps the play on the literal head in verses 4-10. The meaning decision reads man and woman in general, so Korean does not say "husband" …
- [ ] [1CO 11:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/11/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "형상" for the image, as in Genesis 1:27 and Colossians 1:15; "영광" at every level — "영광" is a key term and stays at every level; the meaning decision reads it here as honor, which Readable says with "나타내다" (show).
- [ ] [1CO 11:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/11/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "머리 위에 권위" (L, B); "자기 머리에 대한 권위" (R) — The meaning decision reads the words as the woman's own authority over her head, the precise sense uncertain and footnoted; Korean does not add "sign" or "covering". "권위" is used for authority here; "권리" in chapters 8-9 is the same Greek word as a right.
- [ ] [1CO 11:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/11/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "본성" (L); "자연" (B); "세상의 자연스러운 이치" (R) — The meaning decision reads "nature" as the way things are, as people of that time felt them to be, uncertain and footnoted; Korean avoids "law of nature", which would say more.
- [ ] [1CO 11:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/11/#v19) L/B · word_choice · "파당" (L, B) for factions; "인정받은 사람들" (L), "시험을 견뎌 낸 사람들" (B) for the approved — The meaning decision notes that the later sense of "heresy" came after the New Testament; "파당" is the word of Galatians 5:20 (L) for a faction. "인정받은" begins the word family of 2 Corinthians 10:18 and 13:3-7.
- [ ] [1CO 11:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/11/#v20) L · word_choice · "먼저 먹어 버리다" (L, B); "다른 사람들을 기다리지 않고 ... 먹어 버리다" (R) — The meaning decision reads the verb as eating one's meal before others arrive, uncertain and footnoted, which verse 33 ("wait for one another") supports.
- [ ] [1CO 11:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/11/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "분별하다" (L, B), "알아보다" (R) for discerning; "심판" for the judgment, through verses 29-34 — The meaning decision reads "the body" as the Lord's body given for them and the church it makes one; Literal and Balanced keep the Greek's bare "the body". "분별하다" returns in verse 31, so the play on words can be seen.
- [ ] [1CO 12:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/12/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "영적인 [은사들]" (L); "영적인 은사" (B); "성령께서 주시는 은사" (R), here and in 14:1 — The meaning decision reads the word as the gifts that come from the Spirit, with "the spiritual people" footnoted. Romans 1:11 already says "영적인 은사" (L, B); the plain "은사" is kept for the other Greek word of verses 4, 9, 28 and 31, so the two stay apart.
- [ ] [1CO 12:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/12/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "방언" for tongues and "통역" for their interpretation at every level, through chapters 12-14 — "방언" is the word of Mark 16:17 for tongues; it can mean both "tongue" and "dialect", as the Greek word means both a tongue and a language. The meaning decision gives "languages" as the footnoted alternative.
- [ ] [1CO 12:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/12/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "한 성령 안에서" (L, B, R) for "in one Spirit" — The meaning decision reads "in" as the Spirit being the one in whom believers are baptized, as in water, uncertain and footnoted.
- [ ] [1CO 12:28](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/12/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · "예언자" for the prophets, "교사" for the teachers; "돕는 일 ... 이끄는 일" (L), "돕는 능력 ... 이끄는 능력" (B), "돕는 사람 ... 이끄는 사람" (R) — "예언자" is the style guide's word, as in Ephesians 2:20. The meaning decision reads the last gift as giving direction like a ship's pilot, which "이끄는" says better than "다스리는" (rule).
- [ ] [1CO 12:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/12/#v31) L/B/R · word_choice · "열심히 구하다" (L); "간절히 사모하다" (B); "받으려고 힘쓰다" (R), here and in 14:1, 12 and 39 — The verb can mean jealous rivalry (3:3), but the meaning decision reads it here as a good eagerness, and a command.
- [ ] [1CO 13:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/13/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "불에 타게 하다" (L); "불길에 내어 주다" (B); "불태우게 하다" (R) — The SBLGNT reads "so that I will be burned"; the manuscripts are divided, and the meaning decision footnotes "so that I may boast".
- [ ] [1CO 13:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/13/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "감싸다" for the first verb; "소망하다" for hope — The meaning decision reads the first verb as bearing up under whatever comes, from a word for a roof that covers; "감싸다" (wrap round, shelter) keeps the picture of covering and bearing. "소망" is the word for hope in Romans, and returns in verse 13.
- [ ] [1CO 13:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/13/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "완전한 것" at every level — The meaning decision reads the word here as what is whole, set against "in part"; "완전한" says that plainly. It is the same Greek word as "the mature" (성숙한) of 2:6, where the sense is different.
- [ ] [1CO 13:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/13/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "믿음, 소망, 사랑" at every level; "남아 있다", "남다" for remain — "믿음" and "소망" are the words of Romans and 1 Thessalonians 1:3; the meaning decision reads the verb as lasting, in contrast with the gifts that end, which "남다" (remain) says.
- [ ] [1CO 14:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "영으로" (L); "성령 안에서" (B); "성령께서 ... 도우시지만" (R) — The meaning decision reads the word as the Holy Spirit who gives the speech, uncertain and footnoted; Literal keeps the bare "영" of the Greek, which has no article.
- [ ] [1CO 14:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v9) L · word_choice · "혀로" (L); "여러분의 혀가" (B) — The meaning decision reads the word here as the tongue in the mouth, the organ of speech, with the play on "tongues" left to be heard; Korean "혀" is the body part, distinct from "방언".
- [ ] [1CO 14:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v12) L · word_choice · "영들" (L); "성령께서 일하시는 것" (B); "성령께서 ... 일하시기를" (R) — The meaning decision reads the plural "spirits" as the many ways the one Spirit is at work in the gifts, uncertain and footnoted; Literal keeps the plural of the Greek.
- [ ] [1CO 14:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "내 영" for "my spirit"; "마음" (L, B), "생각" (R) for the mind, through verses 14-19 — The meaning decision reads "my spirit" as Paul's own inner self moved by God's Spirit, set against his mind, so Korean says "내 영", not "성령". "마음" for the mind is the word of Romans 12:2 (L, B), with "생각" at Readable as there.
- [ ] [1CO 14:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v16) L/B · word_choice · "문외한" (L, B); "알아듣지 못하는 사람" (R), here and in verses 23-24 — The meaning decision describes someone in the gathering who does not share the gift and cannot understand, perhaps not yet fully part of the church. "바깥 사람" is kept for "those outside" the church (5:12), so Korean says "문외한", one who stands outside a matter; Readable says what the English Readable …
- [ ] [1CO 14:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v22) L · supplied_words · "[표적이 됩니다]" (L) for prophecy; "위한 것입니다" (B, R) — The meaning decision leaves open whether prophecy is also called a sign; Literal brackets the words it supplies, and Balanced and Readable say only that it is "for" believers, as the English does.
- [ ] [1CO 14:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v29) L/B · word_choice · "분별하다" (L, B) for weighing, the verb of "분별함" in 12:10 — The meaning decision ties the verb to the gift of distinguishing spirits; Korean keeps the same root.
- [ ] [1CO 14:31](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v31) L/B/R · punctuation · "as in all the churches of the holy ones" ends verse 33, closing the teaching on orderly speaking — The meaning decision follows the SBLGNT, which joins the phrase to what comes before; the other reading is footnoted.
- [ ] [1CO 14:34](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v34) L/B/R · word_choice · "여자들" (not "아내들"); "잠잠하다"; "복종하다" with no one named to whom — The meaning decisions read the women in general, uncertain and footnoted, and note that the Greek names no one to whom they are to be subject; Korean does not add "to their husbands". The verb is "복종하다", the word of verse 32 for the prophets' spirits, as the Greek has the same verb.
- [ ] [1CO 14:38](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/14/#v38) L/B/R · word_choice · "인정하다 ... 인정을 받다" for the play on one verb — The meaning decision notes that the active and passive of the same verb make a play on words; Korean keeps it with one verb, "인정하다", and Readable names God, as the English does.
- [ ] [1CO 15:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "성경대로" (L); "성경에 기록된 대로" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads the phrase as the Scriptures of Israel as a whole, with no one passage named.
- [ ] [1CO 15:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v7) R · supplied_words · "주님의 동생 야고보" (R) — The English Readable adds "the Lord's brother", as Galatians 1:19 says; Korean must choose between an older and a younger brother, and the Gospels name Jesus first among his brothers (Mark 6:3), so Readable says "동생".
- [ ] [1CO 15:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "달이 차지 못하고 난 자" (L); "때가 되기도 전에 태어난 아이" (B); "때를 맞추지 못하고 태어난 아기" (R) — The meaning decision reads the picture as a child born dead or too early, uncertain and footnoted. "만삭" is an older word; "달이 차지 못하다" says it in current Korean.
- [ ] [1CO 15:28](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v28) L/B · word_choice · "복종하다", "복종하게 하다" for the verb of verses 27-28 at every level — One Korean verb for the Greek verb repeated through these verses, so the reader can follow who subjects and who is subjected; the meaning decision reads the Son's own subjection as his willing act, which the active "복종하시다" (L, B) says.
- [ ] [1CO 15:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "죽은 사람들을 위하여 세례를 받다" at every level, with no explanation — The meaning decision says the practice is not known; Korean keeps "위하여", which covers "on behalf of" and "for the sake of", and adds nothing.
- [ ] [1CO 15:32](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "맹수와 싸우다" at every level, with no explanation — The meaning decision reads the words as most likely a picture of fierce human opponents, uncertain and footnoted; every level keeps the picture, as the English does.
- [ ] [1CO 15:42](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v42) L/B/R · word_choice · "뿌려지다" (L, B), "묻히다" (R) for being sown; "썩을 것 ... 썩지 않을 것" (L), "썩을 몸 ... 썩지 않을 몸" (B), through verses 42-54 — The meaning decision reads sowing as burial, uncertain and footnoted; Literal and Balanced keep the farmer's verb, and Readable says what is meant. "썩다" is the verb of Romans 1:23 (L) and 8:21, and its negative gives "imperishable" in plain Korean.
- [ ] [1CO 15:45](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v45) L/B/R · word_choice · "살아 있는 존재" for the living being; "생명을 주시는 영", with the honorific, for the life-giving spirit — "생령" is an older word few readers know; "살아 있는 존재" says it plainly. The meaning decision reads the last Adam as the risen Christ, so his verb takes the honorific, while the quotation about the first Adam is in the plain style. "영" is not given the honorific "성령", as the English keeps a lowercase …
- [ ] [1CO 15:49](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/15/#v49) L/B · word_choice · "형상을 입다" (L, B) for wearing the image — The meaning decision reads "wear" as clothing worn, which prepares for "put on" in verses 53-54; "입다" (wear, put on) is the Korean verb for both, so the link is kept. The SBLGNT reads "we will wear"; the other reading, "let us wear", is footnoted.
- [ ] [1CO 16:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/16/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "갈 뜻" (L); "가려고 하지 않다" (B, R) — The Greek says only "will", without saying whose; the meaning decision reads it as Apollos's own will, uncertain and footnoted, so Korean does not add "God's".
- [ ] [1CO 16:22](https://openwordbible.org/ko/1co/16/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "마라나 타" (L), the Aramaic in Hangul; "우리 주님, 오십시오!" (B, R), in 합쇼체 to the Lord — The meaning decision reads the Aramaic, divided as the SBLGNT divides it, as a prayer, "Our Lord, come!", uncertain and footnoted. The prayer speaks to the Lord, so Korean uses 합쇼체 ("오십시오"), as believers speak to Jesus.

## 고린도후서 (55)

- [ ] [2CO 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · the whole letter in 합쇼체 (-습니다, -십시오) at every level, as in 1 Corinthians; the readers are "여러분"; Paul speaks of himself as "나" and "우리"; God, Christ and the … — As in 1 Corinthians and the other letters: a letter read aloud to a church takes the formal style, and Paul writes as the church's apostle. Korean has no capital letters, so the deity_pronoun setting has nothing to change; respect is carried by "그분" and the honorific.
- [ ] [2CO 1:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/1/#v8) L/B/R · register · "우리" for Paul through the letter, as the Greek "we" — The meaning decision reads "we" here as Paul himself, perhaps with his companions, uncertain; Korean keeps the Greek's "우리" and lets "나" stand where the Greek has "I".
- [ ] [2CO 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/2/#v3) B/R · supplied_words · "전에" (B), "그 편지" (R) for the letter Paul wrote — The meaning decision reads "I wrote" as an earlier, tearful letter sent in place of a visit, not 1 Corinthians, uncertain and footnoted; Korean does not say "this letter".
- [ ] [2CO 2:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/2/#v5) L/B · punctuation · the words "so that I may not be too severe" as an aside, in dashes (L) and parentheses (B) — The meaning decision reads the words as an aside, uncertain and footnoted.
- [ ] [2CO 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/2/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "개선 행렬" (L, B); "승리 행렬 ... 그분이 이기신 사람들처럼" (R) — The meaning decision reads the verb as God leading Paul in his triumphal procession, as those he has conquered, uncertain and footnoted; Korean keeps the picture of the Roman triumph, not a plain "makes us win".
- [ ] [2CO 3:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/3/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "없어져 가는" (L); "사라져 가는" (B); "오래가지 않을" (R) for the glory being set aside, through verses 7-14 — The project's choice reads the verb as brought to an end, not fading; "fading" is footnoted in the meaning decision.
- [ ] [2CO 3:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/3/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "옛 언약" at every level — The meaning decision reads the phrase as the Sinai covenant heard in the reading; "구약" would name the Christian book, the Old Testament.
- [ ] [2CO 3:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/3/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · "주" (L); "주님" (B, R), with no divine_name span, here and in verses 17-18 — The meaning decision reads the words as an adaptation of Exodus 34:34, where the Hebrew has the divine name; Paul's Greek has "Lord", given as in quotations elsewhere, and the meaning decision leaves open whether Christ or God is meant here.
- [ ] [2CO 3:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/3/#v17) L/B · word_choice · "영" (L, B), "성령" (R), with the honorific "이십니다", "계신" — The meaning decision reads the sentence as identifying the Lord just spoken of with the Spirit, uncertain and footnoted; Literal and Balanced keep the Greek's "Spirit" without adding "Holy", as the English does, and the honorific shows that a person is meant.
- [ ] [2CO 3:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/3/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "거울을 보듯이" for beholding as in a mirror; "변화되다" for being transformed, as in Romans 12:2 — The meaning decision reads the verb as seeing the Lord's glory as in a mirror, uncertain and footnoted, with "reflecting" as the other reading. "변화되다" is the verb of Romans 12:2 and of Jesus' transfiguration.
- [ ] [2CO 4:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/4/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "믿음의 영" (L, B); "믿음을 주신 성령" (R) — The meaning decision reads the word as the Holy Spirit who gives faith, uncertain and footnoted; Literal and Balanced keep the Greek's "spirit of faith", and Readable names the Spirit, as the English does.
- [ ] [2CO 5:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/5/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "벌거벗은" at every level, Readable adding "몸이 없는" — The meaning decision reads "naked" as being without a body, uncertain and footnoted; the SBLGNT reading "having put it on" is footnoted too.
- [ ] [2CO 5:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/5/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "미치다" (L); "정신이 나가다" (B, R) — The meaning decision leaves open whether Paul means ecstatic experience of God or what his critics said of him, uncertain and footnoted; Korean says "out of one's mind" without deciding.
- [ ] [2CO 5:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/5/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "새로운 창조" (L, B); "새 창조" (R), as in Galatians 6:15 — The meaning decision reads the verbless phrase as the new creation God has begun, of which the person in Christ is part, uncertain and footnoted; "새로운 창조" keeps the larger sense of Galatians 6:15, where "새로운 피조물" would narrow it to the person.
- [ ] [2CO 5:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/5/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "화해시키다", "화해" (L, B); "하나님께로 다시 돌아오게 하다" (R), here and in verse 20 — The words of Romans 5:10-11 and Colossians 1:20-22 for reconciliation (L, B); the meaning decision says God is the one who reconciles and people are reconciled to him, which "화해시키다" with God as subject keeps. Readable follows the English Readable, "bring back to himself", here, where the work of …
- [ ] [2CO 5:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/5/#v21) L/B/R · word_choice · "죄로 삼으셨습니다" (L, B), "죄 자체인 것처럼 대하셨습니다" (R); "하나님의 의" at every level — The meaning decision reads "made him sin" as God identifying Christ with sin and bearing its judgment in him, uncertain and footnoted with "a sin offering"; Literal and Balanced keep the Greek's bare "sin". "의" is a key term at every level.
- [ ] [2CO 6:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/6/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "다른 멍에를 함께 메다" (L); "맞지 않는 멍에를 함께 메다" (B); "하나로 묶이다 ... 서로 다른 짐승이 한 멍에를 메고" (R) — The meaning decision reads the picture as being harnessed with a partner of another kind, and the ties as those that bind believers to unbelievers' ways, above all idol worship, uncertain; Korean keeps the picture and does not narrow it to marriage.
- [ ] [2CO 7:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/7/#v3) L · word_order · "함께 죽고 함께 산다고" (L); "죽든지 살든지" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads the phrase as a pledge of loyalty; Literal keeps dying before living, as in the Greek.
- [ ] [2CO 7:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/7/#v10) L · word_order · "후회할 것이 없는 구원에 이르게 하는 회개" — The meaning decision reads "not to be regretted" as describing the repentance and with it the salvation; the Korean phrase sits before both, as in the Greek.
- [ ] [2CO 7:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/7/#v12) L · word_choice · "썼더라도" (L); "편지를 쓴 것은" (B); "그 편지를 썼습니다" (R) — The meaning decision reads the letter as the earlier, tearful letter of verse 8 and 2:3-4.
- [ ] [2CO 8:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/8/#v7) L · word_choice · "우리에게서 [나와] 여러분 안에 있는 사랑" — Follows the SBLGNT reading set by the meaning decision.
- [ ] [2CO 8:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/8/#v12) L · word_choice · "기쁘게 받아들여지다" (L, B); "하나님께서 기뻐하시다" (R) — The meaning decision reads the word as acceptable to God, as of offerings God accepts; Literal and Basic leave the one who accepts unnamed, as the Greek does, and Readable names God, as the English does.
- [ ] [2CO 8:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/8/#v18) L · word_choice · "뽑힌" (L); "뽑았습니다" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads the word as appointed by the churches, probably by a show of hands; the English Basic "voted to appoint" is carried by the plain Korean verb.
- [ ] [2CO 9:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/9/#v3) L · word_choice · "이렇게 확신했던 일" (L); "그토록 확신했던" (B); "그렇게 믿었던" (R) — Follows the meaning decision's reading of the word as confidence, not plan.
- [ ] [2CO 9:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/9/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "탐욕" (L); "마지못해 내는 선물" (B); "돈을 움켜쥐려는 것" (R) — The meaning decision reads the word, set against "blessing", as a gift given with a grasping spirit; "탐욕" is the word of Colossians 3:5.
- [ ] [2CO 9:9](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/9/#v9) L/B · word_choice · "그" (L, B) for the generous person of the psalm; "그들" (R) — The meaning decision reads the subject as the generous person of the psalm, not God, so the pronoun has no honorific.
- [ ] [2CO 10:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/10/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "비천하다" (L, B); "비굴하고 약하다" (R) — The meaning decision reads the word here in its bad sense, lowly or feeble, as Paul's critics say; "겸손" would praise him.
- [ ] [2CO 10:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/10/#v3) L · word_choice · "하나님께 능력 있는 것" (L); "하나님의 능력이 있다" (B, R) — The meaning decision leaves open whether the power comes from God or is used for him; Literal keeps the Greek's "to God" with "께", and the other levels follow the English.
- [ ] [2CO 10:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/10/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · A command, "보십시오", at every level — Follows the meaning decision's reading of the verb as a command, with the SBLGNT's full stop.
- [ ] [2CO 10:8](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/10/#v8) L · supplied_words · "[이렇게 말합니다]" (L) — The meaning decision notes that the purpose clause has no main verb close at hand.
- [ ] [2CO 10:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/10/#v10) L · word_choice · "그가 말합니다" (L); "어떤 사람은 ... 말합니다" (B, R) — Follows the SBLGNT singular, read by the meaning decision as one critic or "someone".
- [ ] [2CO 10:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/10/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "영역" (L); "일터" (B); "맡겨 주신 지역" (R) — The meaning decision reads the word as an assigned field of work, measured out by God; the English is "sphere" (L), "field" (B), "area" (R).
- [ ] [2CO 10:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/10/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · The wording of 1 Corinthians 1:31 at every level: "주" (L), "주님" (B, R), with no divine_name span — The same quotation of Jeremiah 9:24; the meaning decision reads "the Lord" here as most likely Christ.
- [ ] [2CO 11:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "참아 주십시오", a request, at every level — Follows the meaning decision's reading of the verb as an appeal, though the same form can be a statement.
- [ ] [2CO 11:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v2) L/B · word_choice · "열심" (L, B); "질투하는 사랑" (R) — The word names a jealous, protective love; "질투" alone in Korean would sound petty, so Literal and Basic use "열심", the word of 7:7 and 9:2, and Readable says "질투하는 사랑", following the English.
- [ ] [2CO 11:3](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v3) L · word_choice · "진실함과 정결함" (L); "순전하고 정결한 충성" (B); "온전하고 깨끗한 마음" (R) — Follows the SBLGNT reading, which includes "and the purity", as the meaning decision sets it.
- [ ] [2CO 11:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "참습니다" (L), present; "참아 줍니다" (B, R) — Follows the SBLGNT present, set by the meaning decision; "잘도" and "아주 잘" carry the irony.
- [ ] [2CO 11:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "그 지극히 뛰어난 사도들" (L); "그 대단한 사도들" (B); "자칭 대단하다는 그 사도들" (R) — The meaning decision reads the name as Paul's mocking name for his rivals; the Korean keeps the mockery with "그", and Readable adds "자칭", as the English adds "so-called".
- [ ] [2CO 11:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v12) L · word_order · "그들이 자랑하는 일에서 그들이 우리와 같은 사람으로 드러나게" (L) — Follows the meaning decision's reading: the rivals want to be seen as Paul's equals in the work they boast of.
- [ ] [2CO 11:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "뺨을 치다" (L, R); "얼굴을 때리다" (B) — The meaning decision reads the blow as a deep insult; "뺨을 치다" is the Korean picture of an insult.
- [ ] [2CO 11:26](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v26) L/B/R · word_choice · "여러 번 굶고" (L); "여러 날을 먹지 못했고" (B); "먹을 것이 없을 때가 많았습니다" (R) — The meaning decision reads the word as going without food, perhaps sometimes by choice; "금식" would make it only religious fasting.
- [ ] [2CO 11:28](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · "나를 누르는 일" (L); "나를 짓누르는 일" (B); "무거운 짐" (R) — Follows the SBLGNT reading "pressure", as the meaning decision sets it.
- [ ] [2CO 11:29](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v29) L/B/R · word_choice · "애타다" (L); "속이 타다" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads the word as burning with distress and indignation; the Korean idiom for the inward burning is "속이 타다".
- [ ] [2CO 11:32](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/11/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "민족 통치자" (L); "아레다 왕이 세운 통치자" (B); "아레다 왕을 섬기는 총독" (R) — The meaning decision reads the title as the Nabatean king's official; Literal keeps the sense of the Greek title, "ruler of a people".
- [ ] [2CO 12:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/12/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "환상", "계시" (L, B); "환상", "주님께서 보여 주신 것" (R) — Plain Korean words; the meaning decision reads "of the Lord" as naming the giver, so Basic and Readable say "주님께서 주신", "보여 주신".
- [ ] [2CO 12:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/12/#v6) L · punctuation · Literal keeps the phrase about the revelations at the end of verse 6's sentence, as in the SBLGNT — Follows the meaning decision's punctuation.
- [ ] [2CO 12:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/12/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "육체의 가시" (L, B); "몸을 찌르는 아픈 가시" (R) — The meaning decision reads the thorn as some painful, lasting trouble whose nature is not known; "육신" in this project names human weakness or sinful nature, so the plain "육체" (the body) is used.
- [ ] [2CO 12:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/12/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · The name of 11:5: "그 지극히 뛰어난 사도들" (L); "이 대단한 사도들" (B); "자칭 대단하다는 그 사도들" (R) — Paul's mocking name for his rivals, kept the same as at 11:5.
- [ ] [2CO 12:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/12/#v18) L/B · word_choice · "영" (L); "마음" (B); "같은 방식" (R) — The meaning decision leaves open whether this is the Holy Spirit or the same attitude; the English has "spirit" with a small letter, so Korean does not write "성령".
- [ ] [2CO 12:21](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/12/#v21) L · word_order · "다시" with "갈 때" (L); with "낮추시다" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads "again" with being humbled; Literal keeps the Greek's order, and Basic and Readable make the reading plain.
- [ ] [2CO 13:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/13/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "일" (L); "고발" (B, R) — The meaning decision reads the matter as a charge to be backed by witnesses at Paul's visit.
- [ ] [2CO 13:6](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/13/#v6) L · word_choice · "알게 되다" (L, B, R) — The meaning decision leaves open whether they will know it by their own testing or when Paul comes.
- [ ] [2CO 13:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/13/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "기뻐하십시오" (L, B); "기쁨이 가득하십시오" (R) — The word can also mean "farewell"; the English renders it "rejoice", and Korean follows.
- [ ] [2CO 13:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/13/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "위로를 받으십시오" (L); "격려를 받으십시오" (B); "힘을 내십시오" (R) — The meaning decision reads the verb as receiving the comfort and strength God gives, the "위로" of 1:3-7; it notes that the verb can also mean taking Paul's appeal to heart.
- [ ] [2CO 13:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2co/13/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "성령의 교제" (L), as in Philippians 2:1; "성령께서 주시는 교제" (B); "성령께서 ... 주시는 사귐" (R) — The meaning decision reads the phrase as the fellowship the Holy Spirit gives; the English Literal "sharing" is said with "교제", the project's Literal word for this noun with the Spirit.

## 갈라디아서 (12)

- [ ] [GAL 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gal/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · the whole letter in 합쇼체 (-습니다, -십시오) at every level; the readers are "여러분"; Paul speaks of himself as "나" and "우리"; God and Christ take the honorific -시- and … — As in the Korean of Colossians and Romans: a letter read aloud to the gathered churches calls for the formal style of a public address, so every level uses 합쇼체, even where Paul is angry, and "여러분" is the respectful plural "you". Paul writes with an apostle's authority, so he says "나" and "우리". …
- [ ] [GAL 1:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gal/1/#v12) L/B · word_choice · "예수 {{christos:그리스도|메시아}}의 계시" (L, B); "하나님께서 예수 {{christos:그리스도|메시아}}를 나에게 보여 주셨고" (R) — The meaning decision reads the phrase as God unveiling Jesus Christ to Paul, with Christ as the one who reveals as the other reading. Korean "...의 계시" keeps both open at Literal and Balanced, as the Greek does; Readable says the reading chosen, as the English Readable does.
- [ ] [GAL 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gal/2/#v14) L/B/R · register · Paul speaks to Cephas as "당신" in 합쇼체 — Paul rebukes a fellow apostle in public, as an equal. Korean 합쇼체 with "당신" is formal and direct without being insulting, which fits a public rebuke between equals; 해라체 would make Paul talk down to Peter, which the scene does not suggest. A reviewer may prefer another way of addressing him.
- [ ] [GAL 2:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gal/2/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "예수 {{christos:그리스도|메시아}}를 믿는 믿음" at every level — The meaning decision reads the phrase as faith with Jesus Christ as its object, with his own faithfulness as the other reading. Korean "...의 믿음" would be read as Jesus' own faith, so every level says "...를 믿는 믿음", as in Romans 3:22.
- [ ] [GAL 2:20](https://openwordbible.org/ko/gal/2/#v20) L/B/R · word_choice · "하나님의 아들을 믿는 믿음" at every level — As at 2:16: the meaning decision reads it as faith in the Son of God, and Korean "...의 믿음" would be read as the Son's own faith.

Readable says what is hoped for, as the English Readable does, with "선언하다" (declare), close to the letter's "의롭다고 인정하다".

"새로운 창조" (new creation) keeps both open; "새로운 피조물" (a new creature) would narrow it to the person. Readable says the wider reading, as the English Readable does.

"구원" is the key term and keeps both open, as the English "salvation" does.
- [ ] [PHP 2:14](https://openwordbible.org/ko/php/2/#v14) L · word_order · Literal ends verse 15 with "빛나고 있습니다" and makes verse 16 a command of its own — The meaning decision reads "holding fast" with what comes before; Korean cannot hang a participle across the verse marker without moving verse 16's content, so Literal gives the participle as a command, which keeps the sense that they shine as they hold fast.

Korean "배" keeps the Greek word at Literal and Balanced; Readable says what it stands for, as the English Readable says "appetite".

"멍에의 동료" (L) keeps the Greek picture of sharing a yoke.

Korean "그" is used of a woman as well as a man, and "그녀" is a written coinage that sounds out of place read aloud; "그 여자" would sound cold. ## 데살로니가후서 (4)

- [ ] [2TH 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2th/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · the whole letter in 합쇼체 (-습니다, -십시오) at every level, with "여러분", "우리" and "나", the honorific for God and Christ, and no deity_pronoun spans; "실루아노", … — The same senders write to the same church (the meaning decision), so the Korean keeps every choice settled for 1 Thessalonians: the formal style of a letter read aloud to the church, Paul's "나" and "우리", and no deity_pronoun spans, since Korean has no capital letters for the setting to change.

Korean keeps the bare "what restrains" as the Greek does, and in verse 7 "막는 이" (the one who restrains), since there the word is masculine.
- [ ] [2TH 2:13](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2th/2/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "첫 열매" (L); "추수의 첫 열매" (B); "맨 처음 구원받는 사람들" (R) — The SBLGNT reads "as firstfruits", with "from the beginning" as the other reading (the meaning decision, marked uncertain). Korean follows the SBLGNT at every level, and Balanced and Readable say what the picture means, as the English does.
- [ ] [2TH 3:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/2th/3/#v5) L/B · word_choice · "하나님의 사랑과 {{christos:그리스도|메시아}}의 인내" (L, B) — The meaning decision reads these as God's love for them and the endurance Christ showed and gives, and marks it uncertain. Korean "...의 사랑", "...의 인내" keep the Greek's open genitives at Literal and Balanced; Readable says the reading chosen, as the English Readable does.

Korean must choose between a mother's and a father's mother; Acts 16:1 says Timothy's mother was a Jewish believer, and Lois shares her faith, so she is read as his mother's mother. A reviewer may prefer the neutral "할머니".

Korean "맡긴 것" keeps the deposit word used in 1:14 and 1 Timothy 6:20.

Literal and Balanced keep the picture; Readable says what it means and keeps the picture as a comparison, as the English Readable does.

Korean "하나님이시며 구원자이신 예수" names Jesus as both, as the English does, with the honorific "-이시며", "-이신".

## 요한계시록 (19)

- [ ] [REV 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/1/#v1) L/B/R · register · the narrative of the whole book in the plain written style (해라체: -었다, -다), with the honorific for God, Christ, the Lamb and the Spirit at every level; John … — Revelation is above all the account of what John saw, told like the narrative of the Korean Gospels, and its letter frame is short and runs straight into the visions (1:9-10), so one style holds the book together. Christ speaks to John and to the churches in 해라체, as Jesus speaks in the Korean …
