"땅에 사는 사람들" is kept for this repeated phrase (6:10; 8:13; 11:10; 13:8; 17:8).
- [ ] [REV 14:15](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/14/#v15) L/B/R · register · the angel speaks to the one on the cloud in 합쇼체 — The angel brings God's command from the temple (meaning decision), but the one he speaks to is the figure like a son of man, so Korean has him speak up, as to Christ; the angel of 14:18 speaks to another angel in 해라체.
- [ ] [REV 19:10](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/19/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "예언의 영" with "영", not "성령" — The testimony of Jesus as the spirit of prophecy is uncertain (meaning decision): the spirit may be the Holy Spirit or the heart of prophecy. Korean "영" leaves both readings open, as lowercase "spirit" does in English; "성령" would decide.
- [ ] [REV 19:12](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/19/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "피에 적신 옷" (L, R), "피에 적셔져 있다" (B) — Whose blood is uncertain (meaning decision), and the reading "dipped" is chosen over "sprinkled" (text variant); Korean says only that the robe was dipped in blood, and leaves whose blood it is open.
- [ ] [REV 20:2](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/20/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "천 년" at every level, without explanation — How the thousand years are understood is uncertain (meaning decision, with a footnote); Korean keeps the plain number, as the English does, and does not decide between a literal and a symbolic period.
- [ ] [REV 20:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/20/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "심판하는 권한" for the judgment given; "목 베임을 당한" for the beheaded — Thrones and judgment given echo Daniel 7, uncertain (meaning decision); Korean says the right to judge, as the English does. Beheading was execution with the axe (meaning decision), and "목 베임을 당하다" is the usual Korean for it.
- [ ] [REV 20:4](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/20/#v4) L/B/R · word_choice · "살아나다" (L, B), "다시 살아나다" (R) — That they came to life is the first resurrection, uncertain (meaning decision, with a footnote); "살아나다" keeps the plain verb of the Greek, and the word "부활" is left for 20:5, where the text itself names it.
- [ ] [REV 20:7](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/20/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "곡과 마곡"; "전쟁을 위하여" (L), "전쟁에" (B), "전쟁을 하려고" (R), as in 16:14 and 19:19 — Gog and Magog are the enemies of Ezekiel 38-39 (meaning decision), named as in the Korean Ezekiel tradition. The war is perhaps the same battle as in 16:14 and 19:19, uncertain (meaning decision); Korean has no article and repeats the wording of those verses, which leaves the link open.
- [ ] [REV 20:11](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/20/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "그 위에 앉아 계신 분", with the honorific, as for the one seated on the throne in 4:2; "그분 앞에서" for "from his face" — The one seated on the great white throne is God the judge, uncertain (meaning decision); Korean gives him the honorific of the one on the throne without naming him, so that the reader may also think of Christ.
- [ ] [REV 21:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/21/#v1) L/B/R · word_choice · "새 하늘과 새 땅"; "바다도 더 이상 없다" (L), "더는 없었다" (B), "더 이상 없었다" (R) — A new heaven and a new earth echo Isaiah 65-66 (meaning decision), with the words of the Korean 2 Peter 3:13. That there is no more sea may mean the end of chaos and threat, uncertain (meaning decision); Korean says only that the sea is gone.
- [ ] [REV 21:5](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/21/#v5) L/B/R · grammar · "그분이 ... 말씀하신다" with the honorific — He who speaks is most likely God, continuing, uncertain (meaning decision); the honorific keeps him the same speaker as in 21:5a, and Korean need not name him.
- [ ] [REV 21:16](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/21/#v16) B/R · setting_alternative · {{units:약 2,200킬로미터|12,000 스타디온}} (B, R); "12,000 스타디온" (L) — The Korean style guide makes metric the default and the original unit the alternative, as in 14:20. That 12,000 stadia is both immense and a number of God's people is uncertain (meaning decision), so Literal keeps the number itself, and the alternative keeps it at the other levels.
- [ ] [REV 21:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/21/#v17) B/R · setting_alternative · {{units:약 65미터|144 규빗}} (B, R); "144 규빗" (L) — Metric is the default and the original unit the alternative (Korean style guide). That 144 cubits is twelve times twelve is uncertain (meaning decision), and the alternative keeps the number for the reader who sees it.
- [ ] [REV 21:19](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/21/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · the current Korean mineral names: "벽옥", "사파이어", "옥수", "에메랄드", "홍마노", "홍옥수", "귀감람석", "녹주석", "황옥", "녹옥수", "풍신자석", "자수정", with "벽옥", "홍옥수" and "에메랄드" as in 4:3 — The twelve jewels recall the high priest's breastpiece and Isaiah's city (meaning decision), and the names of the stones are uncertain (meaning decision); Korean gives each Greek name its current mineral name, as the English keeps the traditional ones, and the same name at every level.
- [ ] [REV 21:24](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/21/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "민족들", "땅의 왕들", as through the book — Nations and kings come to the city's light, uncertain (meaning decision: Isaiah 60); Korean keeps the words used for the nations and the kings who opposed God in chapters 17-19, so the reader sees the same peoples now coming in.
- [ ] [REV 22:1](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/22/#v1) L/B/R · punctuation · "큰길 한가운데로" joined to the river's course, at every level — Whether "in the middle of its street" goes with the river or with the tree is uncertain (meaning decision); Korean follows the English and puts it with the river, so the trees stand on its banks.
- [ ] [REV 22:17](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/22/#v17) L/B/R · grammar · "오십시오!" (합쇼체) for the first two cries; "오너라" for the thirsty — Come! is a prayer to Christ, and then an invitation, uncertain (meaning decision); Korean must choose a speech level for each, so the first two take the form of prayer to Christ, as in 22:20, and the third is spoken to the thirsty in the plain imperative.
- [ ] [REV 22:18](https://openwordbible.org/ko/rev/22/#v18) L/B/R · grammar · "나는 ... 증언한다", first person in the plain style, without naming the speaker; no gender spans — Do not add or take away echoes Deuteronomy 4:2 (meaning decision). Whether "I testify" is Jesus or John is uncertain (meaning decision); Korean "나" with the plain verb fits either, as Jesus and John both speak so in this book.
